### [1] [Testing Van der Waals black holes using black hole photon rings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25097)
*Chengzhen Li,Meirong Tang,Zhaoyi Xu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates the Van der Waals black hole in AdS spacetime using photon ring observables—derived from null geodesics and effective potential analysis—to constrain the molecular volume parameter $b$ and pressure $P$, finding observational consistency with EHT data for M87* and Sgr A*, while distinguishing it from Schwarzschild BH via $b$-sensitive photon ring features tied to SBH-LBH phase transitions and microstructural discontinuities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To observationally test the physical viability of thermodynamic parameters (e.g., molecular volume $b$, pressure $P$) in the Van der Waals black hole model—a non-Kerr AdS BH exhibiting phase transitions—and to probe BH microstructure via strong-field photon ring signatures.

Method: Derived null geodesic equations and conserved quantities from the Van der Waals BH metric; analyzed effective potential to determine photon sphere conditions and radii; classified photon ring structures and radiative properties; constrained parameters using EHT measurements of M87* and Sgr A*; simulated optical appearances and verified energy conditions and thermodynamic stability.

Result: The molecular volume $b$ strongly governs photon sphere radius, ring morphology, and luminosity; a specific $b$-interval yields excellent agreement with EHT observations for both M87* and Sgr A*, satisfies all classical energy conditions (weak, strong, dominant), and lies within the stable Van der Waals-type phase transition region; photon rings clearly distinguish Van der Waals BH from Schwarzschild BH due to $b$-driven sensitivity arising from SBH-LBH transitions and microstructural discontinuities.

Conclusion: Photon rings serve as a robust observational probe of black hole microstructure and thermodynamics; this work establishes a direct link between BH molecular parameters, spacetime geometry, and EHT-measurable features, enabling empirical inference of BH microphysics.

Abstract: The Van der Waals black hole (BH) is a non-Kerr model in Anti-de Sitter (AdS) spacetime that exhibits characteristic thermodynamic phase transitions. Whether the physical validity of parameters such as the BH molecular volume $b$ and pressure $P$ is feasible still requires observational verification. As a sensitive probe of strong gravitational fields, the BH photon ring directly carries spacetime geometric information, making it an ideal object for model testing. In accordance with the spacetime metric associated with this BH, this study obtains the null geodesic equations for photons and conserved quantities, determines the photon sphere conditions and radii via effective potential analysis, investigates the photon ring classification and radiative properties, and employs measurements from the Event Horizon Telescope (EHT) to place constraints on parameters and simulate the optical appearance. The results show that the molecular volume parameter $b$ significantly regulates the radius of the photon sphere radius, photon ring structure, and the luminosity. Within a specific interval, the model exhibits a high degree of agreement with the EHT measurements for M87* and Sgr A*. This interval not only satisfies the energy conditions (weak, strong, and dominant) but also corresponds to a stable Van der Waals-type phase transition region. Furthermore, the photon ring can distinguish between the Van der Waals BH and the Schwarzschild BH: due to the small-black-hole-large-black-hole (SBH-LBH) phase transition and microstructural discontinuities, the photon ring of the Van der Waals BH is particularly sensitive to the parameter $b$, whereas the Schwarzschild BH lacks these features. This work uses the photon ring to probe BH molecular models, linking its microstructure, spacetime geometry and observational signatures, and provides a new way to derive BH microphysics from observations.

</details>


### [2] [A Closed-Form Master Solution for Gravitational Decoupling of a Unified Family of Isotropic Stellar Seeds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25110)
*Anirudh Pradhan,Safiqul Islam,Ajit Kumar,Muhammad Aamir*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a unified framework for generating anisotropic compact star models via Minimal Geometric Deformation (MGD), starting from a two-parameter isotropic seed metric family. A single closed-form solution for the MGD decoupling function θ-sector—expressed via the Gauss hypergeometric function _2F_1—enables exact anisotropic extensions for arbitrary parameters (n,m). Physical viability, matching to Schwarzschild exterior, and tidal deformability (Love number) are fully analyzed, revealing that anisotropy enhances deformability and that causal stability imposes compactness ceilings for some seed models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and generalize existing isotropic compact star solutions (e.g., Tolman IV, Durgapal IV–V) into a coherent anisotropic framework using Minimal Geometric Deformation, enabling systematic study of physical viability and tidal response under controlled anisotropy.

Method: Constructs a two-parameter isotropic seed metric ν(r) = Ln[C(1 + a r^{2n})^m] with analytically determined μ(r) from isotropy; applies MGD with a single decoupler source g(r); solves the θ-sector decoupling equation exactly in closed form using _2F_1; imposes Darmois–Israel junction conditions and standard physical acceptability criteria (regularity, energy conditions, causality, stability); computes tidal Love number k₂ and dimensionless deformability Λ.

Result: A master formula for anisotropic extension valid for arbitrary (n,m); explicit mapping of physically admissible parameter space for n=1; discovery that Tolman IV and Korkina–Orlyanskii allow arbitrarily high compactness under anisotropy, while Heintzmann IIa and Durgapal IV–V exhibit compactness ceilings beyond which causality fails; monotonic enhancement of tidal deformability with decoupling strength, sharply increasing near causal limits.

Conclusion: The proposed unification scheme provides a powerful, exact, and flexible tool for modeling anisotropic compact stars, revealing nontrivial interplay between anisotropy, compactness, and tidal response—and demonstrates that not all isotropic seeds admit equally robust anisotropic generalizations under fundamental physical constraints.

Abstract: We present a unification scheme for anisotropic compact stars generated via minimal geometric deformation (MGD), built on a two-parameter seed family $ν(r)=\text{Ln}[C(1+ar^{2n})^{m}]$ whose radial metric potential $μ(r)=e^{-λ(r)}$ is fixed in closed form by the isotropy condition. At $n=1$ the family reproduces Tolman IV, Korkina--Orlyanskii, Heintzmann IIa, and Durgapal IV--V as the particular cases $m=1,2,3,4,5$. The key result is a single closure of the MGD $θ$-sector, through one source function $g(r)$, that integrates the decoupler equation in closed form, in terms of the Gauss hypergeometric function ${}_2F_1$, for \emph{arbitrary} $(n,m)$: the anisotropic extension of the $n=1$ family is thus obtained from one master formula. We derive the effective anisotropic matter content, the Darmois--Israel matching to the exterior Schwarzschild vacuum, and the full set of physical acceptability criteria constraining the parameter space, and map the admissible region in compactness and decoupling strength explicitly for the case $n=1$: Tolman IV and Korkina--Orlyanskii admit physically consistent anisotropic deformations at arbitrarily high compactness, while Heintzmann IIa and Durgapal IV--V are bounded by a compactness ceiling beyond which no value of the decoupling parameter restores causality. Using the same closed-form matter content, we compute the quadrupolar tidal Love number and dimensionless tidal deformability across the admissible region, finding that the decoupling systematically enhances both relative to the isotropic seed at fixed compactness, with a sharp rise near the causal ceiling in the Heintzmann and Durgapal branches.

</details>


### [3] [A Primer on Spacetime Singularities II: Spatially Homogeneous Cosmologies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25112)
*Jean-Pierre Luminet*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper, the second part of a review on spacetime singularities in General Relativity, analyzes how singularities manifest in spatially homogeneous cosmological models, distinguishing matter singularities from non-scalar (e.g., whimper) and kinematic singularities, and situating classical results (e.g., Ellis–King) within modern rigorous developments (e.g., curvature blow-up in Bianchi VIII/IX, dipole cosmology).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically examine the concrete realization and typology of spacetime singularities—beyond abstract existence proofs—in spatially homogeneous cosmological solutions of Einstein's equations, bridging foundational theory with dynamical and geometric insights.

Method: Geometric analysis of spatial homogeneity and Bianchi classification; derivation and analysis of Einstein field equations for orthogonal and tilted perfect fluid models; application of Raychaudhuri equation to establish finite-time boundary formation; classification of singularities by curvature, density, fluid tilt, and Cauchy horizon structure; critical reinterpretation of Ellis–King framework using modern results (e.g., rigorous curvature-blow-up theorems, geodesic completeness criteria, dipole cosmology).

Result: Spatially homogeneous cosmologies host a rich hierarchy of singular boundaries—including matter (curvature/density-divergent), whimper (finite-curvature, Cauchy-horizon-associated), and kinematic (geodesically complete but tilt-divergent) singularities—demonstrating that singularity structure is far more nuanced than previously assumed from classical analyses alone.

Conclusion: Even under the high symmetry of spatial homogeneity, singularities exhibit diverse geometric, dynamical, and causal characters; this necessitates refined classification schemes and motivates deeper dynamical study (deferred to Primer III), reinforcing that singularity theory remains an active, multifaceted domain at the interface of geometry, physics, and mathematical relativity.

Abstract: This article is the second part of a review devoted to spacetime singularities in classical General Relativity. While Primer I developed the mathematical framework for defining, classifying, and proving the existence of singularities, the present work examines their concrete realization in spatially homogeneous cosmological models. After reviewing the geometrical foundations of homogeneity and the Bianchi classification, we derive the field equations for orthogonal and tilted perfect fluid cosmologies and use the Kasner and Bianchi I solutions as elementary prototypes of anisotropic singular behaviour. Raychaudhuri focusing provides general conditions under which the homogeneous Cauchy development reaches a finite proper time boundary. We then distinguish matter singularities, characterized by divergent density and Ricci curvature, from more subtle non-scalar or whimper singularities associated with Cauchy horizons and extreme fluid tilt. The classical Ellis-King analysis is reconsidered in the light of later developments, including rigorous curvature-blow-up results for Bianchi VIII and IX models, kinematic singularities in geodesically complete tilted spacetimes, and the recent reappearance of whimper behaviour in dipole cosmology. The resulting picture shows that even spatially homogeneous cosmologies exhibit a remarkably rich hierarchy of singular boundaries. The detailed dynamical approach to these boundaries - Hamiltonian cosmology, Kasner transitions, Mixmaster dynamics and the BKL conjecture - is reserved for Primer III.

</details>


### [4] [Generalized Regular Black Holes with Tunable Cores and Geodesic Properties](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25113)
*Hassan Hassanabadi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a family of regular, spherically symmetric black-hole geometries parameterized by a discrete core index n, regularization scale a, and deformation parameter η; it systematically analyzes their horizon structure, thermodynamics (temperature, entropy, first law), null/timelike geodesics (photon sphere, shadow radius, ISCO, MBO), and weak lensing—unifying Bardeen and Reissner–Nordström-like features in a tunable framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a flexible, physically motivated family of regular black-hole spacetimes that interpolate between known regular models (e.g., Bardeen) and asymptotically Reissner–Nordström behavior, enabling systematic study of how core regularity and exterior charge-like deformations jointly influence thermodynamic and geodesic observables.

Method: Constructs static, spherically symmetric metrics from a tunable, positive normalized effective density profile; derives exact and perturbative solutions for horizon structure, Hawking temperature, entropy, radial thermodynamic identity, null geodesics (including photon sphere and shadow radius), timelike circular orbits (ISCO, MBO), and weak lensing via asymptotic metric expansion.

Result: Obtains analytic and implicit expressions for horizon radii, extremality condition, Hawking temperature, entropy, photon sphere radius, shadow radius (with exact Bardeen/Schwarzschild limits and weak-η approximations), orbital/instability timescales via angular frequency and Lyapunov exponent, and ISCO/MBO locations; shows n controls central curvature depletion (de Sitter → Minkowski), while η induces RN-like asymptotics.

Conclusion: The proposed family provides a unified, parametric framework to disentangle effects of core regularity (governed by n and a) and asymptotic electromagnetic-like deformation (governed by η), revealing distinct imprints on thermodynamics and geodesic observables—crucial for testing regular black-hole phenomenology against EHT and gravitational-wave data.

Abstract: We introduce a family of static and spherically symmetric regular black-hole geometries characterized by a discrete core index $n$, a regularization scale $a$, and a dimensionless deformation parameter $η$. The construction starts from a positive, normalized effective density profile with a tunable central behavior. The lowest member contains the Bardeen geometry as a limiting case, whereas a nonzero deformation parameter generates a Reissner--Nordström-like asymptotic correction. The parameter $n$ controls the order of central depletion: the lowest member possesses a de Sitter-type core, whereas higher members approach a Minkowski-like center with vanishing density and curvature. We determine the horizon structure, extremality condition, Hawking temperature, entropy, and the horizon thermodynamic identity implied by the radial Einstein equation. Null geodesics are analyzed to obtain the photon sphere, critical impact parameter, and shadow radius, including analytic weak-deformation approximations, the exact Schwarzschild and Bardeen limits, and comparison with the leading Reissner--Nordström-like behavior. The photon-orbit angular frequency and Lyapunov exponent are then used to separate orbital and instability timescales; their ratio provides information not already contained in the shadow radius. Weak gravitational lensing and magnification are discussed through a controlled sector-isolated expansion of the asymptotic metric. We further study timelike geodesics, circular motion, the marginally bound orbit, and the innermost stable circular orbit, deriving exact implicit relations and perturbative expressions. The model provides a unified framework for investigating how a tunable regular core and an RN-like exterior deformation affect the thermodynamic and geodesic properties of regular black holes.

</details>


### [5] [Event Detection and Finite-Grid Effects at an Imposed Absorbing Boundary in Newtonian Black Hole Orbit Integrations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25137)
*Gregory M Davis*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper examines how numerical implementation of absorbing boundaries affects classification of black-hole orbits, showing that discrete boundary evaluation—especially at t=0—strongly influences inferred critical periapsis, and that apparent thresholds may reflect numerical artifacts rather than true dynamical behavior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand whether numerically observed critical periapsis values in orbit classification stem from genuine physical/dynamical thresholds or from artifacts introduced by how absorbing boundaries are discretely evaluated in simulations.

Method: Numerical integration of massless particle trajectories around a Newtonian point mass; initialization at periapsis; use of an absorbing boundary at rsink = 10⁴RS; systematic variation of temporal grid resolution and timing of boundary evaluation (including vs. excluding t = 0); comparison against analytic Keplerian expectation for classification.

Result: Omitting boundary evaluation at t = 0 leads to misclassification of particles already inside rsink; including t = 0 yields exact agreement with analytic classification across all grids; as grid resolution increases, the numerically inferred critical periapsis converges toward rsink, confirming dependence on both boundary placement and discretization.

Conclusion: Apparent critical periapsis in orbit classification is not solely determined by dynamics but is sensitive to numerical implementation—particularly boundary evaluation strategy—highlighting the necessity of distinguishing numerical artifacts from intrinsic dynamical thresholds.

Abstract: Numerical classification of black-hole orbits can depend on how prescribed inner capture or absorbing boundaries are implemented. This study investigated how discrete evaluation of such a boundary affects orbit classification. Massless particles were integrated about an isolated Newtonian point mass and initialized at periapsis with an absorbing boundary at rsink = 104RS. Because the Keplerian periapsis q is constant, the expected classification can be analytically determined. A classifier omitting evaluation at initialization can miss particles that already satisfy the removal criterion, but evaluation at t = 0 reproduced the analytic classification on every sampled grid. As the grid resolution increased, the inferred critical periapsis approached the imposed sink radius. These results demonstrate that an apparent critical periapsis can depend on both the imposed boundary and its numerical evaluation, emphasizing the need to distinguish numerical effects from genuine dynamical thresholds.

</details>


### [6] [The BTZ black hole, the Third Law and gravitational collapse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25218)
*Aidan M. McSharry,Harvey S. Reall,Jorge E. Santos*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper proves a Third Law of black hole mechanics for the BTZ black hole in 3D gravity with matter satisfying the dominant energy condition, showing that finite-time evolution to extremal BTZ is forbidden under generic initial data with trapped surfaces—except in gravitational collapse of a massless scalar field, which reaches extremality in finite time due to sector mismatch (Neveu-Schwarz vs. Ramond). The proof uses quasilocal energy/angular momentum and spinorial BPS inequality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish whether a Third Law—preventing finite-time approach to extremality—holds for the BTZ black hole in 3D gravity with physically reasonable matter, and to resolve apparent contradictions with known collapse solutions.

Method: Defines quasilocal energy and angular momentum; employs spinorial techniques to derive a BPS inequality saturated by extremal BTZ; analyzes boundary conditions, sectors (Neveu-Schwarz vs. Ramond), and asymptotic behavior of collapsing solutions.

Result: A rigorous Third Law is proven: no finite-time evolution from trapped-surface initial data can yield an event horizon identical to extremal BTZ for any asymptotic boundary conditions—yet massless scalar collapse *does* form extremal BTZ in finite time, because it resides in the Neveu-Schwarz sector while extremal BTZ is supersymmetric only in the Ramond sector.

Conclusion: The Third Law holds for generic physical evolutions constrained by the dominant energy condition and sector-consistent supersymmetry, but apparent violations arise from sector mismatches; thus, extremality can be reached in finite time only when supersymmetry is not required to be preserved along the evolution.

Abstract: We prove that a Third Law of black hole mechanics holds for the BTZ black hole in 3d gravity coupled to matter obeying the dominant energy condition. This law states that initial data containing a trapped surface cannot evolve in finite time to a black hole that coincides with extremal BTZ on the event horizon, for any choice of boundary conditions at infinity. The result is proved by defining a quasilocal energy and angular momentum and using spinorial methods to establish a BPS inequality that is saturated by the extremal BTZ solution. Nevertheless, we show that gravitational collapse of a massless scalar field can result in the formation of an extremal BTZ black hole in finite time. The difference between these results arises because gravitational collapse occurs in the Neveu-Schwarz sector, whereas extremal BTZ is supersymmetric in the Ramond sector. Solutions that settle down to extremal BTZ in infinite time along the event horizon are also discussed.

</details>


### [7] [Gravity-Informed Neural Networks for Post-Newtonian Binary Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25222)
*Gabriele Barbagallo,Javier Matulich*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Introduces GravINNs, a physics-informed neural network framework that embeds post-Newtonian equations of motion to model conservative and dissipative binary orbital dynamics—achieving high accuracy without trajectory supervision in the dissipative regime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a flexible, accurate, and computationally efficient surrogate for post-Newtonian (PN) binary orbital dynamics—both conservative and dissipative—without relying on large datasets of numerical integrations or supervised trajectory data.

Method: Proposes gravity-informed neural networks (GravINNs) that embed PN equations of motion as soft constraints during training; implements three tiers: (1) single-orbit surrogates for conservative dynamics, (2) a parametric 4D model (initial separation, radial/tangential momenta, symmetric mass ratio), and (3) extension to 2.5PN radiation-reaction (dissipative) dynamics without dissipative reference trajectories.

Result: GravINNs achieve high accuracy across diverse orbital configurations; the parametric model generalizes over orbit families; the 2.5PN extension correctly captures secular energy loss and orbital decay without using dissipative ground-truth trajectories during training.

Conclusion: Physics-informed neural networks can robustly and efficiently represent both conservative and dissipative post-Newtonian dynamics, offering a promising path toward fast, interpretable, and generalizable gravitational waveform surrogates.

Abstract: We introduce a gravity-informed neural-network (GravINNs) framework for learning post-Newtonian binary orbital dynamics. The method incorporates the equations of motion directly into the training phase and is developed at three complementary levels. First, we construct single-orbit surrogates for conservative post-Newtonian dynamics and assess their accuracy across different orbital configurations. We then extend the approach to a parametric model spanning a four-dimensional parameter space defined by the initial separation, radial and tangential momenta, and symmetric mass ratio, allowing a single trained network to represent entire families of orbits and replace repeated numerical integrations. Finally, we consider dissipative dynamics by including the leading radiation-reaction contribution at $2.5$PN order. In this regime, the network accurately reproduces both the orbital evolution and the associated secular energy loss without using dissipative reference trajectories in the training. These results show that physics-informed neural-networks (PINNs) can provide accurate and flexible representations of both conservative and dissipative post-Newtonian dynamics. The complete implementation and the models used in this work are publicly available through the \texttt{GravINNs} repository.

</details>


### [8] [Gravitational wave signatures of subsolar neutron star formation in collapsar disks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25225)
*Vishal Baibhav,Valerio De Luca,Loris Del Grosso,Emanuele Berti,Brian D. Metzger,Lam Hui*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates gravitational-wave signatures of subsolar-mass neutron star binaries formed in collapsar accretion disks, identifying distinctive environmental imprints—orbital Doppler modulation, gas-driven migration, and tidal interactions—that distinguish them from other subsolar merger channels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify unique gravitational-wave signatures that can distinguish subsolar-mass neutron star binaries formed in collapsar accretion disks from those formed via other astrophysical channels, enabling tests of the collapsar formation pathway if such mergers are observed.

Method: Waveform modeling incorporating relativistic orbital motion around a central black hole, gas-driven migration in a neutrino-cooled accretion disk, and tidal effects during inspiral; analysis of detectability and parameter estimation with current and next-generation gravitational-wave detectors.

Result: Collapsar-formed subsolar binaries produce unique waveform features—including characteristic phase modulations and mass-dependent environmental imprints—that are distinguishable from field or dynamical-formation channels; detection prospects are favorable for current (e.g., LIGO/Virgo/KAGRA) and future (e.g., Cosmic Explorer, Einstein Telescope) observatories.

Conclusion: Gravitational-wave observations of subsolar-mass compact binary mergers can serve as a probe of collapsar environments, provided waveform models include disk-specific dynamics; discovery would provide direct evidence for neutron star formation in stellar collapse disks and constrain angular momentum transport and fragmentation physics.

Abstract: Standard models for stellar core collapse predict the birth of neutron stars with masses exceeding a solar mass. In the collapsar model, a rapidly rotating massive star collapses to a black hole surrounded by a dense, neutrino-cooled accretion disk, whose outer regions may in some cases fragment into neutron-rich clumps that collapse to form subsolar-mass neutron stars. These disk-formed objects can then merge either with one another or, following possibly hierarchical evolution, with the central black hole. In this work, we characterize the gravitational-wave signatures of these binaries, focusing on distinctive features of the collapsar environment: Doppler and other phase modulations from orbital motion about the central black hole, gas-driven migration in the disk, and tidal interactions during inspiral. We show that the combination of anomalously low component masses with these environmental imprints produces unique waveform signatures that can distinguish the collapsar channel from other subsolar merger scenarios. Current and next-generation detectors have good prospects for identifying the intrinsic properties of such objects and, if subsolar mergers are discovered, for testing and constraining the collapsar formation pathway.

</details>


### [9] [Accurate Black Hole Quasinormal Modes, Regge Poles, and Greybody Factors from Light Ring Ladder Symmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25228)
*Rajes Ghosh,David Pereñiguez,Jaime Redondo-Yuste,Emanuele Berti*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper reveals a hidden ladder symmetry in black hole perturbation equations in the high-frequency limit, mapping quasinormal modes, Regge poles, and greybody factors onto an anharmonic oscillator problem—enabling highly accurate analytical computation (up to 18th order in inverse angular momentum) for Schwarzschild black holes and offering a scalable framework for rotating black holes and modified gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a systematic, convergent analytical framework for computing black hole response functions—quasinormal modes, Regge poles, and greybody factors—in the high-frequency (eikonal) regime, overcoming limitations of existing approximation schemes.

Method: Exploits the null-geodesic correspondence near the light ring in the high-frequency limit; identifies a ladder operator symmetry in the radial Teukolsky (or Regge–Wheeler/Zerilli) equation; reformulates the problem as quantum-mechanical anharmonic oscillator perturbation theory in 1/ℓ; implements high-order WKB-like expansion and resummation techniques (e.g., Padé, Borel).

Result: Analytical expressions for quasinormal modes, Regge poles, and greybody factors of Schwarzschild black holes up to O(ℓ⁻¹⁸); demonstrated superior convergence vs. standard WKB or continued-fraction methods; resummation improves accuracy—especially for greybody factors at low frequencies; framework generalizable to Kerr and alternative gravity models.

Conclusion: The ladder-symmetry-based anharmonic oscillator framework provides a unified, efficient, and high-precision method for black hole scattering and decay properties in the eikonal limit, with broad applicability beyond Schwarzschild spacetime.

Abstract: The characteristic oscillations of a black hole can be understood in terms of null rays trapped near its unstable light ring, with this correspondence becoming exact in the high-frequency limit. In this regime, we uncover a hidden ladder symmetry in the perturbation equations, which maps the calculation of quasinormal modes, Regge poles, and greybody factors for a spherically symmetric black hole onto the quantum-mechanical problem of an anharmonic oscillator. This provides a systematic, efficient framework for computing the black hole response, with improved convergence properties compared with existing approximation schemes. As an explicit application, we analytically compute all three quantities for a Schwarzschild black hole through eighteenth order in inverse angular momentum, obtaining highly accurate results. We explore different resummation schemes and find that they can further improve the accuracy of the Regge poles and, particularly, the greybody factors. Our framework can be readily extended to rotating black holes and to modified theories of gravity.

</details>


### [10] [Higher derivative Hořava-Lifshitz fermions: from superconductivity to atomic spectroscopy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25236)
*A. A. Araújo Filho,K. E. L. de Farias,N. Heidari,M. Paganelly,A. F. Santos*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies an isotropic Hořava-Lifshitz fermion theory with a dimension-six spatial derivative term (z=2 Lifshitz scaling), analyzing its consequences for superconductivity (BCS/NJL pairing, gap equation, Meissner effect), scattering (exponentially damped oscillatory potential, finite cross sections), and atomic spectroscopy (hydrogenic level shifts, constraints on HL couplings from muonic atoms).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore physical implications of Lorentz-violating Hořava-Lifshitz fermion theories with z=2 anisotropic scaling in the UV, particularly how modified dispersion relations affect condensed matter phenomena (superconductivity), scattering physics, and precision atomic spectroscopy.

Method: Uses derivative expansion valid for z=2 Hořava-Lifshitz fermions; applies BCS/NJL mean-field theory for pairing; derives effective potential from scalar pole denominator as exchange kernel; computes Born approximation scattering amplitudes; analytically evaluates hydrogenic energy level shifts induced by the HL interaction.

Result: 1) Modified quasiparticle spectra, finite-temperature gap equation, and gauge-invariant stability criterion for superconductivity; 2) Exponentially damped oscillatory massive potential yielding finite forward/total cross sections and non-Rutherford momentum transfer behavior; 3) Closed-form expressions for 1S, 2S, 2P level shifts, providing spectroscopic bounds on HL coupling product via muonic atom data.

Conclusion: The z=2 Hořava-Lifshitz fermion sector produces distinct, testable signatures across multiple domains: it alters superconducting mechanisms, yields finite cross sections incompatible with standard Rutherford scattering, and induces measurable atomic level shifts—especially in muonic atoms—providing direct experimental constraints on the underlying Lorentz-violating couplings.

Abstract: We investigate an isotropic Hořava-Lifshitz fermion theory supplemented by a dimension-six spatial derivative operator, preserving first order time evolution while modifying the ultraviolet dispersion relation. Within the regime of validity of the derivative expansion, we develop three complementary consequences of the $z=2$ sector. First, a Bardeen-Cooper-Schrieffer/Nambu-Jona-Lasinio (BCS/NJL) mean field treatment of fermion pairing yields the particle and antiparticle quasiparticle spectra, the finite temperature gap equation, and the gauge invariant criterion for a stable superconducting phase. The modified dispersion alters both the density of states governing the pairing scale and the electromagnetic vertices entering the Meissner response. Second, using the scalar pole denominator as a minimal spin independent exchange kernel, we derive an exponentially damped and oscillatory massive potential and obtain the corresponding Born scattering amplitudes. The quartic momentum dependence renders both the forward and total cross sections finite and produces a large transfer behavior distinct from Rutherford scattering. Finally, we evaluate the induced shifts of the hydrogenic $1S$, $2S$, and $2P$ levels in closed form, retaining the full dependence on the interaction range. In other words, these latter results gives direct spectroscopic constraints on the product of nuclear and leptonic couplings, with muonic atoms, showing the respective sensitivity to the short distance Hořava-Lifshitz sector.

</details>


### [11] [Frozen-In Gravitational Fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25240)
*Felipe A. Asenjo,Maricarmen A. Winkler,Luca Comisso*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces gravitational field connections—topologically conserved two-surfaces and field lines—in general relativity, enabled by a gravitational analog of the ideal Ohm condition; it derives conserved gravitational magnetic flux and gravitational helicity, establishing topological constraints on spacetime evolution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand persistent geometric and topological structures arising in the nonlinear evolution of spacetime governed by the Einstein field equations.

Method: Reformulates the Einstein equations in analogy with nonlinear electrodynamics of continuous media; identifies gravitational field connections via an ideal Ohm-type condition; derives conservation laws using differential-geometric and topological analysis.

Result: Discovery of gravitational frozen-in field connections, conserved gravitational magnetic flux, and a topologically interpretable conserved gravitational helicity.

Conclusion: General relativity admits intrinsic topological invariants—gravitational flux and helicity—that constrain admissible spacetime evolutions and provide an organizing principle for its nonlinear dynamics.

Abstract: Spacetime can undergo complex nonlinear evolution, as governed by the Einstein field equations, and a central challenge is to understand the geometric structures that arise, persist, and interact throughout its evolution. Using a formulation of Einstein equations that parallels nonlinear electrodynamics of continuous media, we show that general relativity admits gravitational field connections---two-surfaces and associated field lines whose connectivity is maintained by the spacetime dynamics. This gravitational frozen-in behavior is enabled by an ideal Ohm-type condition for the gravitational field. We further show that the same framework naturally leads to a conserved ``gravitational magnetic'' flux. A conserved gravitational helicity also emerges, with a clear topological interpretation in terms of gravitational field-line structures. These results identify well-defined topological constraints on admissible spacetime evolution and provide an organizing principle underlying the nonlinear dynamics of spacetime.

</details>


### [12] [Reconnection of Gravitational Fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25241)
*Luca Comisso,Felipe A. Asenjo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a theory of gravitational reconnection—defined as a change in connectivity of gravitational field sheets due to breakdown of ideal (frozen-in) evolution—in tetrad-based general relativity, quantifies its rate, and analyzes its effects on energy-momentum exchange and gravitational helicity evolution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand consequences of non-ideal gravitational evolution—i.e., departures from the frozen-in condition for gravitational field sheets—in dynamical spacetimes, analogous to magnetic reconnection in plasma physics.

Method: Uses a tetrad formulation of general relativity; introduces an ideal Ohm-type condition for the gravitational field; derives quantitative reconnection rates; analyzes energy-momentum transfer and gravitational helicity evolution under non-ideal conditions.

Result: Derives explicit expressions for gravitational reconnection rate; demonstrates that reconnection mediates energy-momentum exchange between gravity and matter; shows it drives changes in gravitational helicity; establishes a quantitative framework for gravitational connectivity changes.

Conclusion: Gravitational reconnection is a well-defined, measurable process in non-ideal GR that fundamentally alters spacetime dynamics, offering new tools to study strong-field, time-dependent gravitational phenomena.

Abstract: In a tetrad formulation of general relativity, an ideal Ohm-type condition for the gravitational field implies the conservation of gravitational fluxes and the preservation of the connectivity of gravitational field sheets. We investigate the consequences of departures from ideal gravitational evolution by developing a theory of gravitational reconnection, defined as a change in the connectivity of gravitational field sheets arising from the breakdown of the ideal frozen-in evolution of the gravitational field. We derive quantitative measures of the gravitational reconnection rate, show that gravitational reconnection alters the energy-momentum exchange between the gravitational and matter sectors, and determine how it modifies the evolution of gravitational helicity. Our results provide a quantitative framework for describing changes in gravitational connectivity and their consequences in dynamical spacetimes.

</details>


### [13] [Kerr-Degenerate Shadows and Distinct Strong-Deflection Lensing in Rotating Hayward-like and Bardeen-like Geometries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25258)
*Chen-Hung Hsiao,Limei Yuan,Yidun Wan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper compares rotating Hayward-like and Bardeen-like black hole geometries within a Kerr-like axisymmetric framework, showing identical shadow boundaries but distinguishable strong-deflection lensing signatures—particularly in time delays, image separations, and magnifications—that depend on the regularity length ℓ and areal-radius profile, enabling potential observational discrimination from Kerr and between the two regular models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test whether regular black hole models (Hayward-like and Bardeen-like) can be observationally distinguished from Kerr and from each other using gravitational lensing—especially in light of EHT shadow observations and future high-precision strong-deflection measurements.

Method: Analyzes rotating geometries constructed via a common Kerr-like ansatz differing only in their areal-radius functions R_i(ρ); computes horizon structure, curvature invariants, Komar charges, energy condition violations (via Einstein tensor as effective stress-energy), photon orbits, black hole shadows, and equatorial strong-deflection limit (SDL) observables—including impact parameter u_m^+, deflection angle coefficients \bar a_+, \bar b_+, image separation s^+, relative magnitude r_{mag}^+, and subleading time-delay ΔT_{2,1}.

Result: Shadow boundaries are identical to Kerr (same mass, spin, inclination); thus EHT shadow data for M87* and Sgr A* do not constrain the regularity length ℓ. However, prograde equatorial SDL observables—\bar a_+, \bar b_+, s^+, r_{mag}^+, and ΔT_{2,1}—depend explicitly on ℓ and the areal-radius profile, breaking degeneracy with Kerr and between Hayward-like and Bardeen-like models—even in the one-horizon regime lacking an inner horizon.

Conclusion: While shadows cannot discriminate these regular geometries from Kerr, high-precision strong-deflection lensing measurements—especially time delays and higher-order image properties—offer viable observational pathways to test and distinguish Hayward-like and Bardeen-like rotating black holes from Kerr and from each other.

Abstract: We study and compare rotating Hayward-like and Bardeen-like geometries within a common Kerr-like axisymmetric ansatz, with the models distinguished by their areal-radius functions $R_i(ρ)$. On the adopted radial branches, the positive minimum areal radius excludes the Kerr ring locus, and the curvature invariants examined here remain finite. We identify the parameter domains containing two, one, or no horizons. The two geometries have the same asymptotic Komar charges but different finite-radius values. If we treat the Einstein tensor as an effective stress tensor, then for every nonzero regularity length $\ell$, this ansatz violates the null and weak energy conditions, as demonstrated by the equatorial radial-null contraction. For the complete photon family on a black-hole branch, the shadow boundary is identical to that of Kerr at the same mass, spin, and observer inclination, and is also identical for the two regular geometries. The corresponding area-equivalent shadow diameters for M87* and Sgr~A* are consistent with the approximate Event Horizon Telescope (EHT)-based intervals adopted here, and we do not obtain further constraints on $\ell$. By contrast, the prograde equatorial strong-deflection limit (SDL) calculations are only partly degenerate. At fixed spin, $u_m^+$ and $θ_\infty^+$ are common to Kerr and the two regular geometries, whereas $\bar a_+$, $\bar b_+$, the image separation, the relative magnitude, and the subleading time-delay correction depend on $\ell$ and on the areal-radius profile. Within the stated strong-deflection approximation, future measurements of $ΔT_{2,1}$, $s^+$, and $r_{\rm mag}^+$ may help test these rotating geometries and distinguish the Hayward-like and Bardeen-like predictions from Kerr, including in the one-horizon parameter domain where the adopted branch contains no Kerr-like inner horizon.

</details>


### [14] [Circular acceleration in Minkowski spacetime: thermality versus finite size](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25277)
*Cameron R D Bunney,Jorma Louko*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how spatial confinement (a circular boundary) and ambient temperature affect the response of an Unruh-DeWitt detector in uniform circular motion in 2+1D Minkowski spacetime, deriving analytic results in the large-boundary limit and identifying thermally enhanced resonance peaks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Experimental interest in observing the circular-motion analogue of the Unruh effect—particularly in analogue spacetime systems—necessitates understanding how realistic conditions like spatial boundaries and ambient thermal fields modify the detector response.

Method: Analytic computation of the transition rate (response function) of an Unruh-DeWitt detector on a circular orbit coupled to a massless scalar field in 2+1D Minkowski spacetime, with the field confined by a circular boundary and prepared in either vacuum or a thermal state; analysis focuses on the large-boundary regime.

Result: The detector response exhibits resonance peaks dependent on orbital speed, acceleration, and excitation energy; these peaks are analytically characterized and become more pronounced when the field is in a thermal state rather than vacuum.

Conclusion: Spatial confinement and ambient temperature significantly alter the operational definition of effective temperature for circularly accelerated detectors; thermal background enhances resonant features, implying that experiments must account for both boundary effects and ambient temperature to isolate Unruh-like signatures.

Abstract: The Unruh effect predicts that a uniformly linearly accelerated observer with proper acceleration $a$ reacts to the Minkowski vacuum through excitations and de-excitations with the characteristics of a thermal state at temperature $T_U=a/(2π)$. An observer in uniform circular motion will experience similar excitations and de-excitations that we may use to operationally define an effective temperature, which however depends not only on the acceleration but also on the orbital speed and excitation energy. Motivated by the experimental interest in the circular motion Unruh effect, we investigate how spatial confinement modifies the response of an Unruh-DeWitt detector in $2+1$ Minkowski spacetime. We consider a massless scalar field confined within a circular boundary prepared in either the vacuum or a thermal state, probed by an Unruh-DeWitt detector on a circular orbit, a setting that describes proposed analogue spacetime systems for testing the effect, and in which both a boundary and an ambient temperature will necessarily be present. We establish analytic results for the detector response in the large-boundary regime and identify resonance peaks, which are more prominent when the field has an ambient temperature.

</details>


### [15] [Black Hole Perturbation Toolkit: Low frequency and post-Newtonian expansions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25281)
*Jakob Neef,Chris Kavanagh,Adrian Ottewill*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces enhanced weak-field analytic capabilities in the BHPT's Teukolsky and SpinWeightedSpheroidalHarmonics packages, enabling high-precision post-Newtonian (PN) and low-frequency/PM expansions of key quantities—MST coefficients, renormalized angular momentum, radial function amplitudes, phase shifts, fluxes, and self-force data—for generic spin weight s and (ℓ,m) modes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable high-order post-Newtonian self-force calculations and provide widely applicable low-frequency analytic expansions for black hole perturbation theory, especially for small mass-ratio binary waveform modeling.

Method: Development and integration of new symbolic and semi-analytic algorithms into the BHPT packages to compute PN/PM expansions of Teukolsky equation solutions, MST coefficients, radial functions, phase shifts, energy/angular momentum fluxes, and self-force-related quantities; supporting arbitrary spin weight s and (ℓ,m) modes.

Result: State-of-the-art precision PN/PM expansions of MST coefficients, renormalized angular momentum, radial amplitudes, phase shifts, homogeneous/inhomogeneous radial functions, gravitational wave fluxes (infinity/horizon), and self-force tools—all computable on standard laptops; implemented in Teukolsky v.1.2 and SpinWeightedSpheroidalHarmonics v.1.1.

Conclusion: The updated BHPT packages significantly advance analytic capabilities for black hole perturbation theory, enabling efficient, high-order PN self-force computations and providing foundational low-frequency expansions critical for next-generation gravitational waveform models.

Abstract: The Teukolsky and SpinWeightedSpheroidalHarmonics packages of the black hole perturbation toolkit (BHPT) are one of the main public libraries to compute numerical solutions to the Teukolsky equation and related quantities. This work is a companion paper presenting their new and improved weak-field analytic functionality focusing on post-Newtonian expansions. The Teukolsky package now allows users to independently extract multiple useful quantities, such as the so called MST coefficients, renormalized angular momentum, amplitudes of the radial functions or the phase shift as a low frequency/PM expansion. It also provides PN expansions of the homogeneous radial functions or - for circular orbits in Kerr - inhomogeneous amplitudes, as well as energy and angular momentum fluxes at infinity and the horizon. All quantities can be computed to state of the art precision on regular laptops. It also provides a suite of tools for post-Newtonian self force computations and updates the analytic functionality of the SpinWeightedSpheroidalHarmonics package. Computations are possible both for specific and generic spin weight $s$ and harmonic mode numbers $\ell$ and $m$. This package paves the way for intensive high-order post-Newtonian self-force calculations at the forefront of waveform modelling of small mass ratio black hole binaries, as well as giving widely useful low-frequency expansions for black hole perturbation theory. All functions described in this paper are available in BHPT versions Teukolsky v.1.2 and SpinWeightedSpheroidalHarmonics v.1.1.

</details>


### [16] [Thin-Shell Black Bounce](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25354)
*Leandro A. Lessa,Renan B. Magalhães,Gonzalo J. Olmo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper identifies a thin-shell structure at the minimal-area bounce of black-bounce spacetimes sourced by anisotropic fluids, shows it arises from metric degeneracy and distributional curvature, constructs new static spherically symmetric solutions supported by delta-function thin shells violating energy conditions, uses Lorentzian/Gaussian smeared energy densities inspired by quantum gravity minimum-length scales, and analyzes thermodynamics of resulting regular black holes or wormholes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the origin of the minimal-area bounce in recently proposed black-bounce solutions, reveal its hidden thin-shell nature due to metric degeneracy, and construct physically motivated models incorporating quantum-gravitational minimum-length effects via smeared matter profiles.

Method: Analytical general relativity: identification of metric degeneracy and distributional curvature at the bounce; junction condition analysis revealing delta-function stress-energy; construction of new exact solutions using Lorentzian and Gaussian smeared energy density profiles as effective quantum-gravity-inspired sources; thermodynamic analysis via horizon geometry and Smarr-like relations.

Result: Demonstration that the bounce is necessarily associated with a thin shell of exotic matter violating energy conditions; derivation of new families of static, spherically symmetric black-bounce solutions (regular black holes or traversable wormholes) supported by smeared quantum-gravitational sources; full thermodynamic characterization including temperature, entropy, and first law.

Conclusion: The minimal-area bounce in black-bounce geometries generically implies a topologically nontrivial thin-shell structure enforced by metric degeneracy and distributional curvature; viable realizations require quantum-gravity-motivated smearing of matter, yielding physically distinct compact objects whose thermodynamics reflects their underlying geometric and matter structure.

Abstract: A recently proposed class of black-bounce solutions sourced by anisotropic fluids, whose exterior reproduces the Reissner-Nordström geometry all the way down to a minimal-area bounce, was put forward by Lessa and Olmo. In this work, we show that this solution conceals a thin-shell structure precisely at the bounce. Motivated by this observation, we construct a new class of black-bounce solutions with static, spherical symmetry featuring the emergence of a minimal-area bounce supported by a thin shell. This nontrivial topological structure is shown to arise from the degeneracy of the metric at the bounce, where the inverse metric becomes ill-defined, leading to curvature tensors with distributional discontinuities precisely at that point. As a consequence, a delta-function thin shell of energy-condition-violating matter is necessarily present at the bounce. To generate such solutions, we draw on proposals from quantum gravity that predict the existence of a fundamental minimum length scale, employing energy density profiles that encode these quantum-gravitational effects in an effective manner, replacing the point-like description of matter at minimal-length scales with a smeared one. We consider two well-motivated profiles, of Lorentzian and Gaussian type, to generate a novel class of solutions exhibiting the features of either regular black holes or wormholes, whose center conceals a thin-shell bounce. In addition, we investigate the thermodynamics of these compact objects.

</details>


### [17] [Light Cone Thermodynamics: Quasi-Local Formalism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25477)
*Matheus G. Barbosa*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a quasi-local thermodynamic formalism for general spacetimes by extending the Bondi-Sachs framework to past light cones, demonstrates quasi-local Hawking radiation under mild conditions, proposes an irreversible inequality as a gravitational analogue of the second law, and constructs a postulational thermodynamic framework incorporating spacetime dynamics—enabling rest-mass density definition purely from thermodynamic variables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a quasi-local thermodynamic description of gravity, motivated by deep connections between general relativity and thermodynamics (e.g., black hole thermodynamics), and to generalize classical thermodynamics to include spacetime dynamics.

Method: Generalizes the Bondi-Sachs formalism to past light cones of arbitrary worldlines; derives quasi-local energy via Landau-Lifshitz pseudotensor; analyzes Hawking radiation emission in selected examples; identifies and interprets an 'irreversible inequality' as a candidate second-law analogue.

Result: Demonstrates quasi-local Hawking radiation in generic settings; establishes an irreversible inequality satisfied in typical gravitational systems; constructs a postulational thermodynamic framework unifying spacetime dynamics and thermodynamics; defines rest-mass density solely from thermodynamic variables, decoupling it from particle-number evolution.

Conclusion: A consistent quasi-local thermodynamic formalism for general relativity is achievable; it extends classical thermodynamics to gravitational systems, provides physical meaning to local energy and entropy-like quantities, and suggests that spacetime dynamics can be fundamentally thermodynamic—even without horizons.

Abstract: Inspired by black hole thermodynamics and other results suggesting that general relativity has some intrinsic connection with thermodynamics, this work aims to establish a quasi-local thermodynamic formalism in general spacetimes. With this purpose, we generalize the Bondi-Sachs formalism to past light cones associated with an arbitrary worldline. From this generalization, we demonstrate the emission of Hawking radiation in a quasi-local context through a few examples, suggesting that it may occur under mild conditions. In addition, we argue that an "irreversible inequality" is satisfied in typical gravitational systems, indicating that an analogue of the second law may be established. Together with a definition of internal energy derived from the Landau-Lifshitz pseudotensor, these results are used to construct a postulational framework that generalizes classical thermodynamics in order to include the dynamics of spacetime. As a consequence, it is possible to define the rest-mass density from thermodynamic variables, decoupling it from the dynamics of constituent numbers.

</details>


### [18] [Operator-Level Quantum-Classical Correspondence in Relativistic Quantum Theory and Curved Spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25360)
*Pankaj Sheoran,Gopal Kashyap,Sanjay Siwach*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends a quantum-classical correspondence from non-relativistic quantum mechanics to quantum field theory and linearized gravity: Heisenberg equations of motion for quantum fields (scalar, electromagnetic, Yang–Mills, gravitational) reproduce their classical field equations *exactly* at the operator level—without invoking ℏ→0—and generalize to curved spacetime and geodesic motion in the semiclassical limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the recently discovered operator-level correspondence between quantum dynamics and Newtonian mechanics for non-relativistic particles generalizes to relativistic quantum fields and gravity.

Method: Systematic derivation of Heisenberg equations of motion for quantum field operators (Klein–Gordon, Maxwell, Yang–Mills, linearized metric perturbations) in flat and curved spacetimes; comparison with classical field equations; analysis of semiclassical limit for particle dynamics in curved spacetime.

Result: Heisenberg equations for quantum field operators identically match classical field equations (including covariant forms in curved spacetime) without ℏ→0 approximation; linearized Einstein equations emerge operatorially for metric perturbations; geodesic motion arises semiclassically from Heisenberg evolution of position/momentum operators.

Conclusion: The quantum-classical correspondence at the level of equations of motion is robust and universal across quantum mechanics, QFT, and linearized quantum gravity—suggesting a deeper structural alignment between quantum operator dynamics and classical field/particle dynamics, independent of the classical limit.

Abstract: In a recent work \cite{Shaikh:2026nsi}, it was shown that for a non-relativistic particle, the operator equations of motion can be cast in the same differential form as Newton's equation of motion. This establishes a correspondence between quantum operator dynamics and classical mechanics at the level of the equations of motion. In this work, we investigate whether this correspondence extends to quantum field theory and linearized gravity. We explicitly show that the Heisenberg equations of motion for quantum field operators reproduce the corresponding classical equations of motion for a broad class of field theories. The Klein-Gordon equation, Maxwell's equations, Yang-Mills equations, and linearized Einstein equations are shown to arise at the operator level without taking the limit $\hbar\rightarrow0$. We then extend the analysis to curved spacetime and show that the quantum field operators satisfy the corresponding covariant field equations at the operator level. In the weak-field approximation, we show that the Heisenberg evolution of the metric-perturbations reproduces the linearized Einstein equations at the operator level. For a quantum particle in curved spacetime, the Heisenberg equations for position and momentum reproduce classical geodesic motion in the semiclassical limit, thereby extending the operator-level quantum-classical correspondence from fields to particle dynamics.

</details>


### [19] [Cosmology in sixth-order higher-derivative theories of gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25387)
*Carlos Aguilar,Manuel Asorey,Diego Muñoz,Miguel Pardina*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies cosmological implications of higher-derivative gravity theories (quadratic and six-derivative curvature invariants), analyzing background solutions, stability of perturbations, and inflationary observables—finding singularity-free but tensor-unstable backgrounds, scalar super-horizon instabilities in dS, and controlled EFT corrections to Starobinsky inflation predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how higher-derivative gravitational corrections (beyond GR) affect cosmological evolution, singularity avoidance, perturbative stability, and inflationary predictions—particularly whether they yield viable, stable, and observationally distinguishable alternatives to standard inflation.

Method: Analyzes homogeneous and isotropic cosmological solutions in both Jordan and Einstein frames; derives and solves linearized equations for tensor and scalar perturbations; identifies stability criteria via propagator pole structure and coupling relations; applies effective field theory (EFT) techniques to treat six-derivative operators as small corrections around the Starobinsky background and computes modified primordial power spectra.

Result: The theory admits expanding, recollapsing, and non-singular bouncing FLRW solutions; singularity avoidance is enabled by new higher-derivative degrees of freedom evading Hawking–Penrose theorems; tensor perturbations are analytically stable on generic backgrounds but unstable on singularity-free ones; scalar perturbations exhibit super-horizon instabilities in de Sitter; sixth-order corrections yield controlled, EFT-consistent modifications to scalar spectral index n_s and tensor-to-scalar ratio r.

Conclusion: While higher-derivative gravity offers singularity resolution and a UV-motivated extension of inflation, background stability and scalar instability constraints limit viability; however, the six-derivative EFT framework around Starobinsky inflation provides a consistent and phenomenologically testable route to probe quantum gravitational corrections via CMB observables.

Abstract: We analyze the cosmological implications of theories of gravity with quadratic curvature invariants $R^2$ and $C^{μνρσ}C_{μνρσ}$, and six-derivative operators $R\Box R$ and $C^{μνρσ}\Box C_{μνρσ}$. We first analyze homogeneous and isotropic cosmological solutions, showing that the theory admits expanding, recollapsing and non-singular bouncing solutions. We clarify in the Einstein-frame formulation the mechanism that circumvents the Hawking--Penrose singularity theorems, and identify the role played by the additional higher-derivative degrees of freedom in that mechanism. We show that the complete set of linear equations governing tensor perturbations on standard backgrounds admit analytically stable solutions. The analysis reveals that the stability properties are determined by the pole structure of the propagator and by specific relations among the higher-derivative couplings, while the singularity-free backgrounds are found to be unstable against tensor perturbations. For scalar cosmological perturbations, the higher-derivative theories presents super-horizon scalar instabilities in de Sitter spacetimes for both quadratic and sixth-order gravity. To extract inflationary observables, we adopt an EFT approach to the sixth-order theory, treating these terms as perturbative corrections around the Starobinsky background. Within this framework, we compute the modification of the primordial scalar and tensor power spectra, obtaining predictions for the scalar spectral index and tensor-to-scalar ratio. We find that sixth-order operators produce controlled deviations from the Starobinsky model while preserving the EFT expansion, thereby providing a consistent phenomenological framework to probe ultraviolet gravitational corrections through cosmological observables.

</details>


### [20] [A Systematic Study of Resonance-Driven Flux Modifications in Extreme-Mass-Ratio Inspirals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25392)
*Edoardo Levati,Lennox S. Keeble,Alejandro Cárdenas-Avendaño*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes resonance-induced corrections to energy, angular momentum, and Carter constant fluxes for extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs) using frequency-domain Teukolsky equation solutions via pybhpt; it derives an analytical selection rule for radial-polar phase dependence and analyzes how resonance coefficients depend on black-hole spin, eccentricity, and inclination—especially for 3:2 and 2:1 resonances.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Transient orbital resonances in EMRIs induce phase-dependent corrections that can significantly alter long-term orbital evolution and gravitational-wave signals; accurate modeling of these effects is essential for LISA data analysis and strong-field gravity tests.

Method: Solves the Teukolsky equation in the frequency domain using the public code pybhpt; coherently combines degenerate radial and polar harmonics at resonance; analytically derives a selection rule for radial-polar phase dependence; computes resonance coefficients across a broad parameter space including spin, eccentricity, and inclination.

Result: Quantified resonance-induced modifications to all three fundamental fluxes (energy, axial angular momentum, Carter constant); derived a new analytical selection rule; mapped coefficient dependence on black-hole spin, eccentricity, and inclination for key resonances (3:2, 2:1); produced the largest set of Teukolsky-based resonance coefficients to date.

Conclusion: Resonance coefficients are governed by a competition between symmetry-induced cancellations and orbital phase-space sampling efficiency; the computed coefficients provide critical input for high-accuracy EMRI waveform models incorporating transient resonances.

Abstract: Transient orbital resonances can introduce phase-dependent corrections to the evolution of extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs), potentially altering their long-term dynamics and emitted gravitational-wave signals. In this work, we quantify the resonance-induced modifications to the energy, axial angular momentum, and Carter constant fluxes and compute the corresponding resonance coefficients across a broad region of the orbital parameter space. Using the publicly available \texttt{pybhpt} code, we solve the Teukolsky equation in the frequency domain to coherently combine the degenerate radial and polar harmonics that arise at resonance. We analytically derive a selection rule governing the relative radial-polar phase dependence of the resonant flux modifications. We argue that the relative strengths of different resonances reflect a balance between symmetry-induced cancellations and the degree to which the resonant orbit samples the underlying two-dimensional orbital phase space. For the dynamically important $3{:}2$ and $2{:}1$ resonances, we also characterize how the resonance coefficients vary with the primary black-hole spin, orbital eccentricity and inclination. Our results constitute the largest set of Teukolsky-based resonance coefficients calculated to date and provide essential input for future studies of transient orbital resonances in EMRIs.

</details>


### [21] [Analytical and spectral characterization of floral patterns in higher-order polynomial pp-waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25459)
*F. L. Carneiro,H. P. de Carvalho,L. A. Cabral,M. P. Lobo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how particle rings deform under higher-order polynomial pp-wave perturbations, showing that transverse tidal eigendirections induce m-fold symmetric stretching/compression sectors; geodesic integration and Fourier analysis confirm dominant n=m angular response, with m=4 'loops' arising from geometric folding—not harmonic mixing—and temporal pulse moments separating displacement vs. velocity memory effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the geometric and dynamical imprint of higher-order pp-wave spacetime perturbations on test particle configurations—specifically, how tidal forces encoded in polynomial modes deform circular particle rings and whether nonlinear or harmonic effects dominate the response.

Method: Analytical derivation of weak-pulse first-order ring deformation using linearized geodesic deviation; direct numerical integration of timelike geodesics for m = 2, 3, 4; Fourier decomposition of ring shape evolution; analysis of temporal pulse moments to disentangle displacement and velocity memory effects.

Result: The ring deformation exhibits exact m-fold discrete symmetry aligned with tidal eigendirections; Fourier spectra show >95% power in the n = m mode; loops observed at m = 4 are due to folding of the fundamental m = 4 mode, not n = 8 harmonics; displacement and velocity memory are distinguishable via pulse time moments but preserve the same angular mode signature.

Conclusion: Higher-order polynomial pp-waves induce clean, mode-selective floral deformations in particle rings governed solely by the wave’s angular order m; harmonic generation is negligible, confirming the dominance of linear tidal geometry over nonlinear coupling—even for strongly folded configurations—and reinforcing the role of pp-wave memory effects as angular-mode-preserving phenomena.

Abstract: We investigate analytically and numerically the floral deformation of particle rings induced by higher-order polynomial modes of pp-waves. The transverse tidal eigendirections determine $m$ interlaced sectors of radial stretching and compression, while a weak-pulse solution provides the complete first-order response of the ring. Direct geodesic integration for $m=2,3,4$ confirms the predicted orientation and discrete symmetry. A Fourier analysis shows that the response remains overwhelmingly dominated by the angular mode $n=m$; in particular, the loops found for $m=4$ arise from geometrical folding of the fundamental mode rather than strong harmonic mixing. The temporal moments of the pulse further distinguish displacement from velocity memory without changing this angular signature.

</details>


### [22] [Quadratic Gravitational-Wave Scattering by Kerr Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25522)
*Lennox S. Keeble,Hengrui Zhu,Lawrence E. Kidder,Harald P. Pfeiffer,Mark A. Scheel*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates second-order perturbations of Kerr black holes under monochromatic gravitational wave incidence, focusing on quadratic coupling to (4,4) daughter modes; it reveals spin- and frequency-dependent scattering-state nonlinearities beyond quasinormal modes, validates results with semi-analytic Teukolsky calculations, and establishes foundations for second-order radiative perturbation theory in strong-field gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To advance precision gravitational-wave modeling and strong-field gravity tests by characterizing the full second-order retarded response of Kerr black holes—including real-frequency scattering states—not just quasinormal mode couplings, which are insufficient for generic radiative perturbations.

Method: Numerical relativity simulations of nearly monochromatic (ℓ,m)=(2,±2) incident gravitational waves on Kerr black holes (spin a ≤ 0.95); measurement of complex self-coupling to (ℓ,m)=(4,4) daughter mode at twice the parent frequency; complementary semi-analytic second-order Teukolsky equation solutions.

Result: Observed strong low-frequency suppression and opposite spin dependence for prograde/retrograde scattering; identification of a daughter-mode resonance tracking the (4,4,0) QNM; in-mode coupling near (2,2,0) QNM grows with spin—unlike decaying quadratic QNM coupling—demonstrating dependence on full parent scattering state; excellent agreement between numerical relativity and semi-analytic Teukolsky results.

Conclusion: The nonlinear second-order response of Kerr black holes is governed by the complete parent scattering state—not just its frequency—and cannot be reduced to QNM-only interactions; this work provides a validated framework for generic homogeneous second-order radiative perturbations, enabling progress in dynamical tides, near-extremal dynamics, and gravitational self-force theory.

Abstract: Second-order black-hole perturbation theory will be an important component of precision gravitational-wave modeling and tests of strong-field gravity with next-generation detectors. Beyond quasinormal modes (QNMs), whose quadratic interactions have been the focus of recent work in black-hole spectroscopy, a generic retarded solution consists of a continuum of real-frequency scattering states whose nonlinear interactions in Kerr remain comparatively less explored. We use numerical relativity to study the quadratic response of Kerr black holes to nearly monochromatic $(\ell,m)=(2,\pm2)$ incident gravitational waves, measuring the complex self-coupling to the outgoing $(\ell,m)=(4,4)$ daughter at twice the parent frequency for black-hole spins up to $a=0.95$. At low frequencies, the coupling is strongly suppressed and exhibits opposite spin dependence for prograde and retrograde scattering. At higher prograde frequencies, we identify a daughter-mode resonance whose frequency and width extracted from the nonlinear response track the fundamental $(\ell,m,n)=(4,4,0)$ QNM. Near the fundamental $(2,2,0)$ QNM frequency, the in-mode coupling grows with spin, in contrast to the decreasing quadratic QNM coupling, demonstrating that the nonlinear response depends on the full parent scattering state rather than on its frequency alone. A complementary semi-analytic second-order Teukolsky calculation reproduces the nonlinear response from our numerical relativity simulations. Our numerical-relativity results and their semi-analytic extension provide a basis for generic homogeneous radiative perturbations of Kerr at second order, with applications to dynamical tides, near-extremal dynamics, and second-order gravitational self-force theory.

</details>


### [23] [Axial Perturbations of Charged Kiselev Black Holes in Rastall Gravity: Matter Response and Quasinormal Modes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25565)
*Yan Wang,Yun-tao Gu,Wen-Di Guo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies axial gravitoelectromagnetic quasinormal modes (QNMs) of a charged black hole in Rastall gravity surrounded by Kiselev-type anisotropic dust, deriving coupled Schrödinger-type perturbation equations; it finds that full decoupling is obstructed by combined Rastall and matter effects, but a truncated 'frozen-source' model yields sub-percent agreement in fundamental frequencies; numerical Chebyshev pseudospectral results show nonmonotonic dependence of QNM frequencies and damping on Rastall coupling γ, charge Q, and surrounding-matter strength.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how modifications of gravity (Rastall theory) and external matter fields (Kiselev-type anisotropic dust) jointly affect the stability and spectral structure of charged black holes via their axial gravitoelectromagnetic quasinormal modes.

Method: Linearized axial perturbation analysis yielding two coupled Schrödinger-type equations under a closure prescription neglecting anisotropy-direction perturbations; algebraic decoupling analysis in limiting cases; introduction of an auxiliary 'frozen-source' truncation for comparison; high-precision Chebyshev pseudospectral numerical computation validated against Reissner–Nordström limit and spectral convergence tests.

Result: Full r-independent decoupling fails when both Rastall modification and surrounding matter are present; the matter-response system exhibits one-way coupling for axial dipole, unlike the two-way coupling in the truncated reference; fundamental QNM frequencies between the two systems differ by <1%; increasing Rastall coupling γ lowers both oscillation frequency and damping rate for small γ, but frequency rises and damping becomes nonmonotonic for large γ; larger black-hole charge increases oscillation frequency; stronger surrounding-matter contributions increase mode lifetime (reduce |Im ω|); all fundamental axial modes are damped (Im ω < 0).

Conclusion: The interplay between Rastall gravity and Kiselev-type surrounding matter qualitatively alters the coupling structure of axial perturbations, yet quantitative QNM predictions remain robust under reasonable approximations; the system remains stable (no growing modes), with parameter-dependent trends in frequency and damping revealing distinct gravitational and environmental imprints on black-hole ringdown signatures.

Abstract: We investigate axial gravitoelectromagnetic quasinormal modes of a charged black hole surrounded by dust-like Kiselev-type anisotropic matter in Rastall gravity. Under a closure prescription that neglects perturbations of the anisotropy direction, the system reduces to two coupled Schrödinger-type equations. These equations admit an $r$-independent algebraic decoupling in the Reissner--Nordström and general-relativistic dust-like limits, but such a decoupling is generically obstructed when both the Rastall modification and the surrounding-matter contribution are present. For comparison, we introduce an auxiliary frozen-source truncation, whose potential matrix differs from the matter-response matrix in only two entries. For the axial dipole, the matter-response system has one-way coupling, whereas the truncated reference system has two-way coupling. Nevertheless, representative fundamental frequencies of the matter-response system and the reference system differ only at the sub-percent level. A Chebyshev pseudospectral calculation, validated through the Reissner--Nordström limit and spectral-convergence tests, shows that increasing the dimensionless Rastall coupling $γ$ lowers both the oscillation frequency and the damping rate in the small-$γ$ region. In the large-$γ$ region, the oscillation frequency rises, whereas the damping rate varies nonmonotonically. A larger black-hole charge generally raises the oscillation frequency, whereas stronger surrounding-matter contributions tend to produce longer-lived modes. All surveyed fundamental axial modes satisfy $\operatorname{Im}ω<0$.

</details>


### [24] [Lensing Signatures of a Primary Hair Black Hole Solution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25667)
*Rahul Das,Umananda Dev Goswami*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies weak and strong gravitational lensing by a 'primary hair' black hole derived via gravitational decoupling from Schwarzschild geometry, using Gibbons-Werner (weak field) and Bozza (strong field) formalisms; finds α dominates leading-order deflection, Q induces repulsive gravity, computes relativistic image observables for Sgr A*, and constrains Q using EHT shadow data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe novel black hole geometries beyond GR by analyzing lensing signatures of a primary hair BH generated via gravitational decoupling—specifically, to assess how the hair parameter Q and coupling constant α modify lensing observables relative to Schwarzschild, and to constrain Q using EHT shadow measurements of Sgr A*.
