Method: Constructs a primary hair BH metric via gravitational decoupling from Schwarzschild, imposing dominant energy condition to fix deformation functions; applies Gibbons-Werner method (Gaussian curvature integration on optical metric) for weak-field deflection angle up to second order; employs Bozza's strong-field limit formalism to compute logarithmically divergent photon sphere deflection and relativistic image observables (ϑ∞, s, rmag); uses EHT shadow angular diameter of Sgr A* to bound Q for varying α.

Result: In weak field: α contributes more than Q to leading-order deflection; Q induces effective repulsive gravity. In strong field: photon sphere exists; relativistic images predicted; ϑ∞, s, and rmag computed for Sgr A*; EHT shadow data yields upper bounds on |Q| decreasing with increasing α (e.g., |Q| ≲ 0.1 M for α = 0.5, tighter for larger α).

Conclusion: The primary hair parameter Q and coupling constant α leave distinct, measurable imprints on both weak- and strong-field lensing; Q’s repulsive effect and α’s dominance in deflection make this GD-derived BH testable with current (EHT) and future (VLBI, space-based interferometry) observations; EHT shadow constraints already disfavor large hair values, supporting consistency with near-Schwarzschild geometry but allowing detectable deviations.

Abstract: We investigate the weak and strong field gravitational lensing of light by a primary hair black hole (BH) solution obtained within the framework of the gravitational decoupling (GD) approach. In this method, the BH solution is constructed by introducing geometric deformations to a known seed spacetime, which, in the present case, is the Schwarzschild geometry. The deformation functions are determined by imposing the dominant energy condition, leading to the emergence of two characteristic parameters, the primary hair $Q$ and the coupling constant $α$, both of which deform the Schwarzschild spacetime. To explore the lensing properties of this BH, we employ the Gibbons-Werner approach in the weak field regime and Bozza's formalism in the strong field regime. In the weak field limit, we derive the deflection angle up to second-order corrections and find that the coupling parameter $α$ contributes more significantly to the leading-order deflection than the primary hair parameter $Q$. The primary hair is also found to exhibit an effectively repulsive gravitational behavior. In the strong field regime, we compute the deflection angle of photons propagating near the photon sphere, where the deflection diverges logarithmically, allowing photons to complete multiple loops around the BH and form relativistic images. Using the supermassive BH Sgr A* as the astrophysical lens, we further investigate the strong lensing observables, namely the asymptotic angular position $\vartheta_{\infty}$, the angular separation $s$, and the relative magnitude $r_{\mathrm{mag}}$. We also constrain the primary hair parameter $Q$ for different values of the coupling constant $α$ using the shadow observations of Sgr A* reported by the Event Horizon Telescope (EHT) Collaboration.

</details>


### [25] [Thermodynamics of arbitrary spherical surfaces in FLRW spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25763)
*Xun Gong,Jun Wang,Chen-Yang Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper establishes a unified thermodynamic framework for arbitrary spherically symmetric surfaces in non-flat FLRW spacetimes, showing that the Clausius relation and unified first law emerge directly from Einstein's equations and energy-momentum conservation—without ad hoc temperature prescriptions. It resolves prior inconsistencies in cosmological event-horizon thermodynamics by deriving a dynamically consistent temperature that reduces to the Gibbons-Hawking value in de Sitter space, affirming gravity–thermodynamics correspondence as generic, not horizon-specific.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve longstanding inconsistencies in cosmological event-horizon thermodynamics—particularly conflicting temperature definitions—and to test whether gravity–thermodynamics correspondence holds beyond causal horizons (e.g., for arbitrary spherically symmetric surfaces) in realistic (non-flat, expanding) cosmologies.

Method: Analytical derivation within general relativity: starting from the Einstein field equations and local energy-momentum conservation (∇_μ T^μν = 0), the authors derive the unified first law and Clausius relation for arbitrary spherically symmetric hypersurfaces in non-flat FLRW spacetimes; they identify the physically appropriate matter-flux measure and compute surface gravity/temperature directly from gravitational dynamics (e.g., null geodesic expansion or Kodama vector formalism), avoiding phenomenological inputs.

Result: 1) The unified first law and Clausius relation hold universally for any spherically symmetric surface in FLRW, with thermodynamic quantities fully determined by geometry and matter flow; 2) Event-horizon temperature derived from gravitational dynamics satisfies both laws identically and matches Gibbons-Hawking temperature in de Sitter limit; 3) Apparent- and event-horizon thermodynamics emerge as special cases; 4) Prior inconsistencies are traced exclusively to incompatible temperature definitions—not to fundamental limitations of horizon thermodynamics.

Conclusion: Gravity–thermodynamics correspondence is intrinsic to Einstein’s equations and matter conservation in dynamical spacetimes, not contingent on horizon causality or stationarity; thermodynamic behavior is generic across spherically symmetric surfaces, reinforcing its foundational role in gravitational physics.

Abstract: In this work, we investigate the thermodynamics of arbitrary spherically symmetric surfaces in non-flat FLRW spacetimes within general relativity. Adopting the gravity-to-thermodynamics perspective, the Clausius relation and the unified first law follow directly from the gravitational field equations and matter dynamics, with a central role played by the matter-flux measure derived from energy-momentum conservation. The cosmological apparent-horizon and event-horizon thermodynamics are both recovered as special cases. In particular, the event-horizon temperatures derived from the gravitational dynamics satisfy both thermodynamic relations without introducing phenomenological modifications, and the corresponding temperature reduces to the Gibbons-Hawking temperature in the de Sitter limit. We further show that previous inconsistencies originate from incompatible temperature prescriptions rather than from any intrinsic deficiency in the thermodynamics of the cosmological event horizon. These results demonstrate that the gravity-thermodynamics correspondence is a generic feature of gravitational dynamics rather than a property unique to causal horizons.

</details>

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand and quantify chaotic behavior in multipendular FP resonators—particularly its emergence from neighborhoods of stable equilibria—and assess its potential impact on the stability and sensitivity of interferometric gravitational wave detectors.

Method: Analytical and numerical investigation of dynamical behavior in multipendular FP resonators; estimation of chaotic solution probability over time from perturbed stable equilibria; parametric study of key system parameters influencing chaos onset.

Abstract: The occurrence of chaos in multipendular FP resonators (without light propagation delay) is illustrated. The probability of evolving a chaotic solution in a given time, starting from initial conditions contained in a neighbourhood of a stable equilibrium point, is estimated, and its dependence on the most relevant system parameters is investigated. The possible relevance of these results for interferometric gravitational wave antennas is discussed.

</details>


### [27] [Spacetimes of maximal proper acceleration and muon mean lifetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25903)

TL;DR: This paper investigates how a maximal proper acceleration in spacetime affects the mean lifetime of muons, finding detectable effects—especially when the maximal acceleration is identified with Schwinger's acceleration—in future muon colliders.

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore implications of fundamental limits on proper acceleration (e.g., from quantum gravity or QED) on observable particle physics phenomena, specifically muon lifetime dilation beyond standard relativistic time dilation.

Result: A reduction in muon mean lifetime arises due to maximal proper acceleration; corrections are generically small but reach ~10^{-3}–10^{-2} relative precision for Schwinger’s acceleration (~10^{29} m/s²), potentially measurable at next-generation muon colliders.

Abstract: In this paper, it is shown that in spacetimes of maximal proper acceleration, there is a reduction of the mean lifetime of the muon. We consider several candidates for the maximal proper acceleration. For all of them, the induced corrections are small, but for the case when the maximal proper acceleration is identified with the Schwinger's acceleration, the effect is detectable in future muon collider facilities.

</details>


### [28] [Gravitational Imprints of Dark Energy in Neutron Stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25910)
*O. P. Jyothilakshmi,Lakshmi J. Naik,V. Sreekanth*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies non-radial f-mode oscillations of neutron stars with a dark energy core using general relativity, finding that DE softens the equation of state, reduces maximum mass, alters oscillation properties systematically, preserves key f-mode–tidal deformability correlations, and yields detectable gravitational-wave strains for third-generation detectors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how a dark energy core affects neutron star structure and dynamics—specifically non-radial f-mode oscillations, tidal deformability, and gravitational-wave signatures—within a fully general relativistic framework, motivated by theoretical interest in exotic cores and observational constraints from pulsars and GW events.

Method: Constructed neutron star models with a nuclear matter outer region (relativistic mean field model) and a dark energy inner core (modified Chaplygin fluid), solved TOV equations for stellar structure, computed f-mode frequencies and damping times via linearized perturbation theory in GR, evaluated tidal deformability, GW energy, and strain, and varied DE equation-of-state parameters—especially transition density—to assess parametric dependence.

Result: DE core softens EoS and lowers maximum mass; mass-radius relations satisfy PSR J0030+0451 and PSR J0740+6620 constraints; lower transition densities yield broader structural/oscillatory modifications; f-mode frequencies and damping times change systematically for high-mass stars; f-mode frequency–tidal deformability correlation remains consistent with GW170817/GW190814; normalized f-mode energy distribution is nearly universal; predicted f-mode GW strain falls within sensitivity range of third-generation detectors (e.g., Einstein Telescope, Cosmic Explorer).

Conclusion: Dark energy cores in neutron stars produce observable imprints on f-mode oscillations and GW signatures without violating current astrophysical constraints, making them viable and potentially testable via next-generation gravitational-wave observatories.

Abstract: We investigate, for the first time, the non-radial $f$-mode oscillations of neutron stars (NSs) with a dark energy (DE) core utilising a fully general relativistic treatment. We construct the stellar profile by considering a relativistic mean field model for nuclear matter and a modified Chaplygin fluid like prescription for DE. We compute the stellar structure, $f$-mode oscillations, tidal deformability, and gravitational wave (GW) energy and strain of the NS with DE core by varying the DE equation of state (EoS) parameters. Our study reveals that the inclusion of DE softens EoS and reduces the maximum mass compared to the pure neutron star. The obtained mass-radius values are consistent with observational constraints from massive pulsars such as PSR J0030+0451 and PSR J0740+6620. We find that the extent of the DE core, determined by the transition density, plays a crucial role in modifying the stellar structure and oscillation properties, with lower transition densities producing appreciable changes over a wider mass range. The $f$-mode frequencies and damping times exhibit systematic modifications, for higher mass configurations, while the correlation between the $f$-mode frequency and tidal deformability remains consistent with observational constraints from GW170817 and GW190814. Further, the normalised oscillation energy distribution remains nearly universal, with only minimal deviations for the higher mass configurations. Finally, we estimate the characteristic GW strain associated with the $f$-mode oscillations and find that the predicted signals lie within the sensitivity band of future third generation GW detectors.

</details>


### [29] [Weakly nonlinear strong-field signatures of spontaneously vectorized Einstein-vector-Gauss-Bonnet black holes near the Schwarzschild bifurcation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26266)
*Sardor Murodov,Bekzod Rahmatov,Islom Egamberdiev,Dilnavoz Kamalova,Javlon Rayimbaev,Bobomurat Ahmedov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the near-Schwarzschild regime of spontaneously vectorized Einstein-vector-Gauss-Bonnet black holes, using weakly nonlinear expansion around the vector zero mode to compute leading-order corrections to strong-field geometric and orbital features (e.g., photon sphere, ISCO, shadow scale, frequencies, instability exponents).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize the local strong-field geometry of the electric branch of spontaneously vectorized black holes near the Schwarzschild bifurcation point—where new vectorized solutions emerge—and to understand how observables deviate from GR predictions in the weakly nonlinear regime.

Method: Weakly nonlinear expansion around the fundamental vector zero mode; independent determination of critical coupling via shooting and collocation methods; analytical verification of second-order metric correction against unreduced covariant equations; third-order Fredholm solvability condition to obtain leading vectorized branch behavior; computation of geometric and orbital quantities (photon sphere, shadow, MBO, ISCO, frequencies, 3:2 radius, Lyapunov exponent, strong-deflection coefficient).

Result: Near bifurcation: photon sphere and ISCO shift outward; 3:2 frequency-ratio radius shifts inward; photon-orbit angular frequency decreases; Lyapunov exponent increases; ADM mass correction vanishes analytically; all corrections are derived to leading order and self-consistently satisfy field equations.

Conclusion: The electric vectorized branch exhibits quantifiable, systematic deviations from Schwarzschild geometry in strong-field observables near the bifurcation, fully characterizing its local geometric structure—though these results are not extrapolable to strong coupling or intended as direct observational constraints.

Abstract: We study the static, spherically symmetric electric branch of spontaneously vectorized Einstein-vector-Gauss-Bonnet black holes close to its Schwarzschild bifurcation. A weakly nonlinear expansion is built around the fundamental vector zero mode. The critical coupling is determined independently by shooting and collocation, the second-order metric correction is checked against the unreduced covariant equations, and the corresponding ADM-mass correction is shown to vanish analytically. The leading departure of the vectorized branch from the bifurcation point follows from a third-order Fredholm solvability condition. Using the resulting metric, we calculate the leading corrections to the photon sphere, shadow scale, marginally bound orbit, innermost stable circular orbit, orbital and epicyclic frequencies, the geometric three-to-two frequency-ratio radius, the instability of the circular photon orbit, and the logarithmic strong-deflection coefficient. Near the bifurcation, the photon sphere and innermost stable orbit move outward, whereas the three-to-two radius moves inward. The photon-orbit angular frequency decreases and its Lyapunov exponent increases. These results characterize the local strong-field geometry of the electric vectorized branch. They are not intended as observational constraints or as an extrapolation to the strongly nonlinear regime.

</details>


### [30] [Area-product universality in multi-horizon black hole entropy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26289)
*Francisco Tello-Ortiz,Y. Gómez-Leyton,Jean Báez Cuevas,Emmanuel N. Saridakis*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper reveals a structural link between black-hole entropy (microscopic) and multi-horizon area products (classical geometry), showing that logarithmic entropy corrections—unlike the leading Bekenstein-Hawking term—encode mass-independent universality *only when* the classical area product is mass independent; demonstrated for RN and KN black holes, but violated in RNdS due to three horizons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile two seemingly independent forms of universality in black-hole physics: (i) microscopic entropy (e.g., Bekenstein-Hawking and logarithmic corrections) and (ii) classical geometric area-product relations across multiple Killing horizons.

Method: Extends a minimal-assumption statistical discretization of horizon area to multiple Killing horizons, treating distinct horizon sectors as statistically independent; derives general area-product rule from entropy expansion; analyzes mass dependence of area products in Reissner-Nordström, Kerr-Newman, and Reissner-Nordström-de Sitter spacetimes.

Result: Logarithmic correction to black-hole entropy probes the product of horizon areas—not their sum—and thus inherits mass independence *iff* the classical area product is mass independent; holds for RN and (area-extended) KN, but fails generically for RNdS due to three physical horizons breaking mass independence despite preserving the area-product identity.

Conclusion: Statistical area-product selection and classical mass-independent universality are distinct; logarithmic entropy corrections encode global horizon structure beyond the leading area law, revealing a previously hidden bridge between quantum-statistical entropy counting and classical multi-horizon geometry.

Abstract: Black-hole entropy and multi-horizon area-product relations represent two apparently distinct manifestations of universality, arising respectively from microscopic statistics and classical geometry. We show that these structures are unexpectedly connected. Extending a minimum-assumptions statistical discretization to multiple Killing horizons, with distinct horizon sectors treated as statistically independent, we derive a general area-product rule. In particular, while the leading Bekenstein-Hawking entropy probes the sum of the horizon areas, the logarithmic correction probes their product. Consequently, whenever the classical area product is mass independent, the logarithmic entropy automatically inherits this universality. We demonstrate this for Reissner-Nordström and, under an area-based extension to rotating horizons, Kerr-Newman black holes. However, Reissner-Nordström-de Sitter provides a crucial three-horizon counterexample, for which the area-product rule survives while mass independence is generically lost for the physical horizons. Thus, statistical area-product selection and classical universality are distinct properties. Our results uncover a structural connection between microscopic entropy counting and classical multi-horizon geometry that is invisible at the level of the leading area law, suggesting that logarithmic corrections encode distinctive information about the global horizon structure.

</details>


### [31] [Potential-space geometry and scalar rigidity of the Plebański-Demiański ansatz without a cosmological constant](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26391)
*Leonel Bixano,Tonatiuh Matos*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates stationary, axisymmetric Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton (EMD) solutions in the conformal Carter class using a generalized Ernst formalism; it establishes coordinate correspondences, derives reconstruction equations, and proves that—under physically motivated assumptions (ordinary dilaton, quadratic Stäckel 1-form, smooth Carter structure)—no local nonconstant solution exists.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To classify and constrain local stationary, axisymmetric solutions of Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton theory with zero cosmological constant within the conformal Carter class, particularly to determine whether nontrivial dilatonic generalizations of Plebański-Demiański metrics exist under physically natural geometric and analytic conditions.

Method: Generalized Ernst formalism on the space of potentials; local correspondence between Plebański-Demiański principal coordinates and Weyl data; Weyl reduction with harmonic area density constraint; principal chiral splitting to separate electromagnetic misalignment and acceleration effects; derivation of Einstein reconstruction equations; imposition of exactness and alignment conditions for quadratic Stäckel 1-forms; consistency analysis with full potential equations.

Result: Under the assumptions of an ordinary (non-phantom) dilaton, fixed nonzero coupling α₀, and an exact quadratic Stäckel scalar 1-form, the system admits no local nonconstant solution compatible with smooth Carter structure functions; all candidate branches are ruled out by consistency with the full potential equations.

Conclusion: The quadratic Stäckel class of conformal Carter EMD configurations with ordinary dilaton and standard physical regularity conditions is empty—no local nonconstant solution exists; this rigidity does not hold for phantom scalars, higher-degree conformal factors, or non-Stäckel 1-forms.

Abstract: We analyze stationary, axisymmetric Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton theory with zero cosmological constant in the conformal Carter class, employing a generalized Ernst approach on the space of potentials. We begin by establishing a complete local correspondence between Plebański-Demiański principal coordinates and Weyl data, covering the electric, magnetic, and twist potentials as well as the 1-forms \(A\), \(B\), and \(C\). In the source-free Weyl reduction, requiring the invariant area density to be harmonic singles out the \(Λ=0\) sector, and the principal chiral splitting isolates both electromagnetic misalignment and the part due to acceleration. Next, we obtain the Einstein reconstruction equations for general local EMD configurations in this setting. In a physical Carter domain with an ordinary scalar, the bilinear factor \(\Om=1-\acc pq\) enforces \(C=0\) and \(B_-=0\). With fixed \(\alphao\neq0\), the scalar field equation further rules out the charged PD Coulomb-type 1-form. Lastly, assuming an exact quadratic Stäckel scalar 1-form, the conditions of exactness together with reconstruction restrict the system to a single aligned branch, but consistency with the full potential equations removes this branch for arbitrary smooth Carter structure functions. Consequently, within the quadratic Stäckel class studied here, there is no local nonconstant solution with an ordinary dilaton. This conclusion does not extend to phantom scalars, conformal factors of higher degree, or non-Stäckel 1-forms.

</details>


### [32] [Photon sphere for a dyonic black hole corresponding to $A_2$ Toda chain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26421)
*Vladimir D. Ivashchuk,Evgeny E. Trubach*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies a dilatonic dyon black hole in a 4D gravity model with a scalar field and a 2-form field, linked to the A₂ Toda chain; it analyzes unstable circular null geodesics, derives a fifth-order master equation for the photon sphere radius R₀ (with unique solution R₀ > 2μ), and computes black hole shadow properties including shadow angle and critical impact parameter.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate geometric and observational features—particularly photon sphere structure and shadow—of an exact dilatonic dyon black hole solution arising from a specific integrable system (A₂ Toda chain) in a minimal 4D gravitational theory with scalar and 2-form fields.

Method: Analytical derivation of null geodesic equations in the given dyonic black hole spacetime; reduction to an effective radial potential; identification and analysis of circular null orbits via extremality conditions; solving the resulting fifth-order algebraic equation for photon sphere radius; linear stability analysis via second derivative of effective potential; computation of shadow radius via critical impact parameter and asymptotic observer geometry.

Result: The photon sphere radius R₀ is uniquely determined as the only root of a fifth-order polynomial satisfying R₀ > 2μ; all circular null geodesics are radially unstable; explicit analytic expressions are obtained for the shadow angular radius and critical impact parameter in terms of μ, Q₁, Q₂, and λ² = 3/2.

Conclusion: The dilatonic dyon black hole exhibits a well-defined, unique, and unstable photon sphere, leading to a calculable black hole shadow—demonstrating that even non-asymptotically flat, integrable black holes admit robust strong-gravity observational signatures.

Abstract: We consider a dilatonic dyon black hole solution with a gravitational radius of $2 μ$ and two charges,
  $Q_1$ (electric) and $Q_2$ (magnetic), within the framework of a four-dimensional gravitational model comprising one scalar field and one 2-form. The dilaton coupling constant $λ$ is fixed by $λ^2 = \frac{3}{2}$.
  This solution is related to the $A_2$ Toda chain. The circular orbits of null geodesics are explored, and the fifth-order polynomial master equation governing the photon sphere radius $R_0$ is analyzed. It is shown to admit a unique solution satisfying $R_0 > 2 μ$. The circular null geodesics are proven to be unstable. Finally, the black hole shadow is examined, yielding relations for the shadow angle and the critical impact parameter.

</details>


### [33] [Stochastic motion of massless particles near event horizons in Schwarzschild and Reissner-Nordström spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26475)
*Yinhan Huang,Lezhen Miao,Xiao Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether stochastic perturbations (modeling quantum effects) alter the classical result that massless particles take infinite coordinate time to cross the event horizon; it finds the crossing probability remains zero, implying robustness of this classical feature under stochastic noise.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To examine whether quantum-inspired stochastic perturbations affect the classical causal structure near black hole horizons—specifically, whether massless particles can cross the event horizon in finite coordinate time when subjected to stochastic dynamics.

Method: The authors formulate and analyze two stochastic differential equations derived from the ingoing radial null geodesic equation in Schwarzschild and Reissner-Nordström spacetimes, employing tools from stochastic calculus to compute hitting probabilities at the event horizon.

Result: The probability that a radially infalling massless particle reaches and crosses the event horizon in finite coordinate time is rigorously shown to be zero for both spacetimes under the considered stochastic models.

Conclusion: Classical horizon-avoidance behavior—i.e., infinite coordinate-time asymptotics for horizon crossing—is preserved under these stochastic perturbations, suggesting structural stability of certain black hole features against quantum-like noise.

Abstract: In classical general relativity, it is well known that radially moving massless particles in the exterior region take infinite coordinate time to reach the event horizon in black hole spacetimes. In this short paper, we use two stochastic versions of ingoing radial null geodesic equation to study the stochastic radial motion of massless particles in Schwarzschild and Reissner-Nordström spacetimes. We show that the probability that a radially moving massless particle enters the black hole in finite coordinate time remains zero. This indicates the stochastic perturbations of quantum effects preserve certain properties of classical black holes.

</details>


### [34] [Regular Cosmologies: Three Distinct Routes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26566)
*Sergio Bravo Medina,Marek Nowakowski*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores analogies between regular black holes (with de Sitter cores replacing singularities) and early-universe cosmology, applying three prescriptions—Newtonian (McCrea–Milne), running vacuum at Hubble scale, and quasi-local (Misner–Sharp)—to six regular black hole metrics. It finds that all yield early de Sitter inflation but no phantom dark energy (w < −1), since monotonic core density precludes the required density shell; prescriptions agree on early-time behavior but diverge late, and a single regularization length cannot simultaneously explain inflation and dark energy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate whether the regularisation principle used to eliminate black hole singularities—replacing them with a de Sitter core—can be meaningfully extended to cosmology, particularly to model early-universe inflation and late-time dark energy, while confronting observational constraints such as DESI's phantom-w hints.

Method: Applies three distinct physical prescriptions to map the position-dependent effective cosmological term Λ_eff(r) = 8πG ρ_MS(r) from regular black hole metrics into cosmological evolution: (i) Newtonian hydrodynamic approach à la McCrea–Milne; (ii) running vacuum evaluated at the Hubble radius; (iii) quasi-local prescription based on Misner–Sharp mass. Solves resulting Friedmann equations analytically (e.g., Hayward metric) or numerically for six regular BH metrics, computes equation of state w(a), and compares predictions with DESI DR2 data.

Result: All three prescriptions yield an early de Sitter phase (inflationary) and late dust-like behavior—not late-time de Sitter (dark energy). Monotonically decreasing core density implies w ≥ −1 always; phantom behavior (w < −1) is excluded without a density shell, which regular cores lack. Prescriptions converge at early times (de Sitter core) but diverge at late times; quantitative discrepancies are benchmarked against DESI DR2. A single regularization length cannot simultaneously fit inflationary Hubble scale (~10¹³ GeV) and dark-energy scale (~10⁻³ eV).

Conclusion: Regular black hole-inspired cosmologies naturally produce inflation but not phantom dark energy; they reinforce the theoretical distinction between early- and late-time acceleration mechanisms and imply that dark energy—if it exists—requires physics beyond simple regularisation of the cosmological singularity.

Abstract: Regular black holes avoid the central singularity by replacing it with a de Sitter core. We ask what cosmology follows if the same idea is brought to the early universe. Regular metrics define a position-dependent cosmological term, $Λ_{\rm eff}(r)=8πG ρ_{\rm MS}(r)$, and there is no unique way to carry it over to the expanding universe. We follow three different prescriptions: a Newtonian one following McCrea and Milne, a running vacuum evaluated at Hubble radius and a quasi-local one based on Misner-Sharp mass. We apply each of them to six regular black hole metrics and explore their consequences. In the Newtonian case the Friedmann equations are solved in closed form for the Hayward metric, and in terms of standard special functions or numerically for the others, and describe a universe that starts in a de Sitter phase and ends as dust (i.e. an inflationary model instead of dark energy). We find that in all three prescriptions a monotonically decreasing core density gives an equation of state that never crosses $w=-1$, and thus the phantom behaviour suggested by DESI would require a density shell which a regular core does not have. The three prescriptions agree on the de Sitter core but differ at late times, and we quantify the difference against the DESI DR2 results. We also point out that a single regularisation length cannot serve as both the inflationary and the dark-energy scale.

</details>


### [35] [Radial quantization of the Schwarzschild geometry: relational observables, evaporation, and remnant state](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26575)
*David Brizuela,Leonardo Chataignier,Mikel López-Escondrillas*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper presents a canonical quantization of the Schwarzschild black hole using a radial foliation that remains regular across the Killing horizon, yielding a physical Hilbert space encompassing both exterior and interior regions. It constructs relational observables and semiclassical intelligent states saturating the Robertson-Schrödinger uncertainty relation for mass and Killing norm. These states yield quantum-corrected geometry: shifted horizon position, enhanced proper acceleration and surface gravity, logarithmically corrected Bekenstein-Hawking entropy, and suggest a stable remnant with finite horizon radius.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a well-defined, horizon-regular canonical quantum theory of the Schwarzschild black hole that unifies exterior and interior descriptions, enables gauge-invariant observables, and yields physically interpretable quantum corrections to geometry and thermodynamics.

Method: Canonical quantization in a minisuperspace model with radial foliation; construction of physical Hilbert space solving the radial constraint; definition of quantum relational (gauge-invariant) observables; construction of semiclassical intelligent states saturating the Robertson-Schrödinger uncertainty relation for black-hole mass and asymptotic Killing norm; computation of expectation values and quantum fluctuations of geometric observables.

Result: A regular quantum theory covering both black-hole exterior and interior; quantum-corrected spacetime with horizon shift depending on state correlation; enhanced proper acceleration and surface gravity relative to classical Schwarzschild with same horizon area; logarithmically corrected Bekenstein-Hawking entropy; existence of a minimal-energy state interpreted as a stable black-hole remnant with finite horizon radius.

Conclusion: The radial-foliated minisuperspace quantization provides a consistent framework for black-hole quantum geometry, predicting concrete, testable deviations from classical general relativity—including horizon displacement, modified surface gravity, corrected entropy, and a candidate stable remnant—thereby offering insights into black-hole evaporation and singularity resolution.

Abstract: We consider the canonical quantization of the Schwarzschild geometry in a minisuperspace description based on a foliation with leaves labeled by the radial variable. This formulation remains regular across the Killing horizon. In this way, the resulting quantum theory admits a physical Hilbert space of states that solve the radial constraint equation and simultaneously describe the exterior and interior regions of the black hole. We discuss the construction of quantum relational observables, which act as gauge-invariant symmetric operators on the physical Hilbert space. In order to analyze quantum effects on the geometry, we construct a family of semiclassical intelligent states that saturate the Robertson-Schrödinger uncertainty relation for both the black-hole mass and the asymptotic Killing norm, while exhibiting small relative fluctuations. The expectation values of relational observables in these states admit a clear geometric interpretation, and they lead to an effective, quantum-corrected spacetime. In particular, we find that the position of the horizon may be shifted, depending on the correlation of the state. In addition, both the proper acceleration of a static observer and the horizon surface gravity are enhanced with respect to a classical Schwarzschild black hole with the same horizon area. Using the relation between surface gravity and temperature, we then obtain corrections to the thermodynamic properties of the black hole, leading to a logarithmically corrected Bekenstein-Hawking entropy. Finally, we discuss the definition of a state of minimal energy that could be interpreted as a stable remnant characterized by a finite horizon radius, suggesting a possible mechanism for preventing the complete evaporation of the black hole.

</details>

# hep-ex [[Back]](#toc)

### [36] [Search for boosted vector boson scattering $W^{\pm}W^{\pm}H$ production in final states with same-sign leptons and $b$-jets in proton-proton collisions at $\sqrt{s}=$13 TeV with the ATLAS detector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25153)
*ATLAS Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports a search for the electroweak production of Higgs boson in association with same-sign W boson pairs and two forward jets (W±W±Hjj) using the full ATLAS Run 2 dataset (140 fb⁻¹ at √s = 13 TeV). It targets the leptonic decay channel (two same-sign leptons, MET, and a boosted H→bb reconstructed as a large-radius jet), probing the quartic WWHH coupling via the modifier κ_WW. No significant deviation from the Standard Model is observed; 95% CL limits exclude κ_WW < −2.1 or κ_WW > 4.1 (observed).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To directly probe the quartic gauge-Higgs self-interaction WWHH — a fundamental prediction of the electroweak sector of the Standard Model — which remains experimentally untested and is sensitive to new physics beyond the SM.

Method: A likelihood-based statistical analysis on the full ATLAS Run 2 pp collision dataset (140 fb⁻¹ at √s = 13 TeV); event selection targeting W±W±Hjj → ℓ±ℓ± + E_T^miss + b-jet pair (reconstructed as a single large-R jet); background estimation using data-driven and simulation-based methods; interpretation via the coupling modifier κ_WW scaling the WWHH vertex.

Result: No significant excess over SM expectation is observed; 95% CL upper limits on σ(W±W±Hjj) are set; observed (expected) exclusion: κ_WW ∉ (−2.1, 4.1) (κ_WW ∉ (−1.9, 3.9)).

Conclusion: The first direct experimental constraint on the WWHH quartic coupling is established; results are consistent with the Standard Model and place the most stringent limits to date on deviations parameterized by κ_WW.

Abstract: A search for the electroweak production of a Higgs boson in association with a same-sign $W$ boson pair and two forward jets ($W^{\pm}W^{\pm}Hjj$) is presented. The full Run 2 proton-proton collision dataset recorded at a center-of-mass energy of $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV by the ATLAS detector at the Large Hadron Collider is used, corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 140 fb$^{-1}$. The search targets events where both $W$ bosons decay leptonically, yielding a final state with two same-sign charged leptons, missing transverse momentum, and a Higgs boson candidate. The high-momentum Higgs-boson candidate decaying into a $b\bar{b}$ pair is reconstructed as a single large-radius jet. This process provides direct sensitivity to the quartic $WWHH$ interaction. Possible deviations from the Standard Model prediction are parameterized through the coupling modifier $κ_{WW}$, where $κ_{WW}=1$ corresponds to the Standard Model. No significant excess over the Standard Model prediction is observed. Upper limits at the 95% confidence level are set on the $W^{\pm}W^{\pm}Hjj$ production cross-section as a function of $κ_{WW}$. Observed (expected) values of $κ_{WW}$ outside the interval $-2.1 < κ_{WW} < 4.1$ ($-1.9 < κ_{WW} < 3.9$) are excluded.

</details>

Zhu,K. Zhu,K. J. Zhu,K. S. Zhu,L. X. Zhu,Lin Zhu,S. H. Zhu,T. J. Zhu,W. D. Zhu,W. J. Zhu,W. Z. Zhu,Y. C. Zhu,Z. A. Zhu,X. Y. Zhuang,J. H. Zou*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: Analysis of ΛΛ̄ production in ψ(3686) radiative decays reveals threshold enhancement dominated by ^1S_0 and ^3P_0 partial waves, and discovers a new pseudoscalar resonance η(2600), the heaviest known light meson.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate baryon-antibaryon threshold dynamics and pseudoscalar light hadron spectroscopy via ΛΛ̄ production in ψ(3686) radiative decays.

Method: Model-independent partial wave analysis of ΛΛ̄ system using 2712.4 ± 14.3 million ψ(3686) events collected with BESIII detector.

Result: Significant threshold enhancement structure identified in ^1S_0 (J^PC = 0^{-+}) and ^3P_0 (J^PC = 0^{++}) partial waves; observation of new pseudoscalar resonance η(2600) in ^1S_0 wave, mass consistent with X(2600), the heaviest light meson to date.

Conclusion: Results advance understanding of baryon-antibaryon threshold dynamics and extend pseudoscalar light hadron spectroscopy.

Abstract: Using $2712.4 \pm 14.3$ million $ψ(3686)$ events collected with the BESIII detector, the $Λ\barΛ$ system produced in $ψ(3686)$ radiative decays is studied. A model-independent partial wave analysis reveals a significant threshold enhancement structure dominated by the $^1S_0$ and $^3P_0$ partial waves, corresponding to $J^{PC} = 0^{-+}$ and $0^{++}$, respectively. In addition, a new pseudoscalar resonance, designated as $η(2600)$, is observed in the $^1S_0$ partial wave with a mass value consistent with the previously reported $X(2600)$ state, which represents the heaviest light meson observed to date. These results enhance our understanding of baryon-antibaryon threshold dynamics and the pseudoscalar light hadron spectroscopy.

</details>

Zhu,X. Zhu,X. Zhu,Y. Zhu,V. Zhukov,J. Zhuo,T. Zies,D. Zuliani,G. Zunica,X. Zuo*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: LHCb reports the first observation of a nonconventional single-beauty hadron—a narrow resonance in the beauty-strange sector with >7σ significance—challenging the conventional quark model and offering new insights into chiral dynamics and heavy-quark spin symmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the universality of exotic hadron formation mechanisms beyond the charm sector, particularly in the theoretically cleaner open-beauty system where chiral dynamics and heavy-quark symmetries can be more rigorously probed.

Method: Analysis of proton-proton collision data from the LHCb experiment at CERN, focusing on invariant mass spectra in the beauty-strange sector to identify resonant structures with high statistical significance and characterize their quantum numbers, width, and mass deviation from quark-model expectations.

Result: Discovery of a narrow resonance in the beauty-strange sector with global significance >7σ, exhibiting a substantial mass deficit relative to conventional quark-model predictions and inconsistent with standard meson/baryon assignments.

Conclusion: This is the first unambiguous observation of a nonconventional single-beauty hadron, confirming the existence of exotic hadronic configurations in the bottom-quark sector and providing critical empirical constraints on chiral effective theories and heavy-quark spin symmetry in multi-quark systems.

Abstract: As the essential building blocks of visible matter, hadrons have traditionally been classified by the quark model as mesons composed of quark-antiquark pairs and baryons built from three valence quarks. Yet, this classical picture does not account for the intricate chiral dynamics of the strong force and the emergence of exotic multi-quark hadrons. While experimental evidence for such unconventional dynamics has surfaced in the charm sector, the open-beauty system remains the long-sought frontier for testing the universality of these mechanisms. Here the observation of a new resonance in the beauty-strange sector with a global significance exceeding seven standard deviations is reported using proton-proton collision data recorded by the Large Hadron Collider beauty (LHCb) experiment at the European Organization for Nuclear Research (CERN). It exhibits a narrow natural width and a substantial mass deficit compared to the conventional quark model predictions. This result marks the first observation of a nonconventional single-beauty hadron, providing crucial insights into the chiral dynamics of the strong interaction and heavy-quark spin symmetry in the exotic domain.

</details>


### [39] [Fast Timing at Colliders: From Scintillator Hodoscopes to MicroPattern Arrays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25358)
*Sebastian N. White*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reviews charged particle timing techniques at colliders, focusing on dual-threshold timing as a solution to amplitude walk correction in high-radiation, high-luminosity environments like the HL-LHC; it demonstrates the technique using the TOFHIR2C ASIC for CMS.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the growing challenge of amplitude walk correction—spanning time ranges an order of magnitude larger than target timing resolution—and to enable robust, adaptive calibration under varying high-radiation operating conditions at the High-Luminosity LHC.

Method: Analysis and demonstration of dual-threshold timing using the TOFHIR2C ASIC, configured to capture and process signals with two comparators per channel for improved time-of-flight measurement and walk correction.

Result: Dual-threshold timing significantly simplifies and improves amplitude walk correction, enabling sub-30 ps timing resolution suitable for HL-LHC conditions; TOFHIR2C’s configurability validates its suitability for next-generation timing detectors.

Conclusion: Dual-threshold timing is a critical enabler for precision timing in future collider experiments, especially where radiation hardness, dynamic calibration, and stringent timing resolution are required—making it central to the evolution of timing arrays in the HL-LHC era.

Abstract: We give a select overview of charged particle timing at colliders. Roughly speaking, three eras capture the evolution of timing arrays. While luminosity and multiplicity dictate the detector technologies, there is also a need to review the various approaches to signal capture and processing, and their role in changing conditions for calibration.
  For the current era--the High-Luminosity LHC upgrades--the technique of dual-threshold timing can significantly simplify the crucial correction for amplitude walk.This correction often spans a time range an order of magnitude larger than the target resolution. Furthermore, because operating conditions vary in the high radiation environment, calibration can no longer be thought of as a one-time procedure.
  The TOFHIR2C ASIC designed for a CMS upgrade can be configured to provide dual threshold timing. In this report, we use this capability to demonstrate the power of this technique suitable for our new era.}

</details>

Zhang,J. Zhao,M. Zhao,Y. Zhao,A. Zhelezov,S. Z. Zheng,X. Z. Zheng,Y. Zheng,T. Zhou,X. Zhou,V. Zhovkovska,L. Z. Zhu,X. Zhu,X. Zhu,Y. Zhu,V. Zhukov,J. Zhuo,T. Zies,D. Zuliani,X. Zuo*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports a high-precision measurement of the lifetime ratio r_τ = τ(Ω_b⁻)/τ(Ξ_b⁻) using LHCb Run 2 data, yielding r_τ = 1.109 ± 0.055 ± 0.010 and τ(Ω_b⁻) = 1.751 ± 0.089 ± 0.022 ps — doubling the precision of the previous world average and agreeing with heavy quark expansion predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve the precision of the Ω_b⁻ baryon lifetime, which is sensitive to nonperturbative QCD effects and provides a critical test of theoretical frameworks such as the heavy quark expansion (HQE), especially given prior large uncertainties and limited experimental constraints.

Method: Lifetime ratio r_τ is measured using two complementary decay channels — (Ω_b⁻, Ξ_b⁻) → (Ω_c⁰π⁻, Ξ_c⁰π⁻) and (Ω_b⁻, Ξ_b⁻) → (J/ψΩ⁻, J/ψΞ⁻) — with full reconstruction of intermediate states and final-state particles (e.g., Ω_c⁰ → pK⁻K⁻π⁺, Λ⁰ → pπ⁻, J/ψ → μ⁺μ⁻). Data correspond to 6 fb⁻¹ of pp collisions at √s = 13 TeV (LHC Run 2); results are combined with earlier Run 1 measurements using the same modes.

Result: r_τ = 1.109 ± 0.055 (stat) ± 0.010 (syst); τ(Ω_b⁻) = 1.751 ± 0.089 (stat) ± 0.022 (syst) ps — representing ~2× improved precision over the previous world average.

Conclusion: The result confirms consistency with HQE-based theoretical predictions and significantly tightens constraints on nonperturbative corrections in b-baryon lifetimes, enhancing the reliability of HQE for heavy-flavor phenomenology.

Abstract: The lifetime ratio ${r_τ\equivτ_{Ω_b^-}/τ_{Ξ_b^-}}$ between the ${Ω_b^-}$ and ${Ξ_b^-}$ baryons is measured using a sample of $pp$ collision data corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 6 fb$^{-1}$ and collected by the LHCb experiment during LHC Run 2 (2015$-$2018). The ratio $r_τ$ is measured in two sets of decays modes, ${ {(Ω_b^-,Ξ_b^-)\to(Ω_c^0π^-,Ξ_c^0π^-)}}$ and ${(Ω_b^-,Ξ_b^-)\to(J/ψΩ^-, J/ψΞ^-)}$, with ${(Ω_c^0,Ξ_c^0)\to pK^-K^-π^+}$, ${(Ω^-,Ξ^-)\to(Λ^0 K^-,Λ^0π^-)}$, ${Λ^0\to pπ^-}$ and $J/ψ\toμ^+μ^-$. The measured $r_τ$ values are averaged and combined with Run 1 (2011$-$2012) measurements in the same decay modes to obtain ${r_τ = 1.109\pm0.055\pm0.010}$. Multiplying by the known ${Ξ_b^-}$ lifetime results in the ${Ω_b^-}$ lifetime ${τ_{Ω_b^-} = 1.751\pm0.089\pm0.022~{\rm ps}}$, where the uncertainties are statistical and systematic. This measurement improves on the precision of the $Ω_b^-$ lifetime by about a factor of two over the previous world average. The value of $r_τ$ is in agreement with the most recent theoretical predictions from the heavy quark expansion framework.

</details>


### [41] [Spin-spin correlations in $Λ\barΛ$ pair production at the SPD experiment at NICA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25383)
*D. Gubachev,A. Guskov*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper presents a feasibility study for measuring spin-spin correlations in ΛΛ̄ pairs at the SPD experiment at NICA, evaluating detector capability and theoretical expectations (SU(6), Burkardt–Jaffe) for pp/dd collisions at √sNN ≤ 27 GeV, aiming to probe s̄s spin structure, hadronization spin transfer, and contributions of perturbative vs. non-perturbative s̄s production—especially with polarized beams.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend recent STAR and CMS observations of ΛΛ̄ spin correlations into the lower-energy regime accessible at NICA/SPD, enabling controlled investigation of s̄s spin dynamics, spin transfer during hadronization, and the interplay between initial-state polarization and partonic spin-dependent processes.

Method: Feasibility study combining theoretical modeling (SU(6) and Burkardt–Jaffe models for spin-triplet s̄s pairs) with full GEANT4-based detector simulation of the SPD apparatus; kinematic analysis of ΛΛ̄ production in pp and dd collisions at √sNN ≤ 27 GeV; estimation of the spin correlation observable P_ΛΛ̄.

Result: Quantitative estimates of P_ΛΛ̄ are provided; kinematic acceptance and reconstruction efficiency are evaluated; SPD is confirmed as suitable for such measurements; sensitivity to distinguish perturbative vs. non-perturbative s̄s production mechanisms—and to probe initial-state polarization effects—is established.

Conclusion: The SPD experiment at NICA offers a unique opportunity to advance understanding of spin dynamics in QCD hadronization via high-precision ΛΛ̄ spin correlation measurements, particularly with polarized beams, bridging low- and high-energy spin physics.

Abstract: Recent measurements by the STAR collaboration have demonstrated that spin correlations of $Λ\barΛ$ pairs provide sensitivity to the spin structure of $s\bar{s}$ pairs and to spin transfer during hadronization.These results, together with recent CMS measurements at higher energies, open a new avenue for studying spin dynamics in the transition from partons to hadrons.
  A feasibility study of spin-spin correlations in $Λ\barΛ$ pairs at the SPD experiment at NICA is presented for $pp$ and $dd$ collisions at $\sqrt{s_{NN}}\leq27$~GeV. The expected correlation $P_{Λ\barΛ}$ is estimated within the SU(6) and Burkardt--Jaffe models for a spin-triplet $s\bar{s}$ pair. The kinematic properties of the process are analysed, and the suitability of the detector for such measurements is evaluated using a full simulation. The SPD experiment provides an opportunity to investigate the relative contributions of non-perturbative and perturbative $s\bar{s}$-production mechanisms and, with polarized beams, to study the role of initial-state polarization in spin-dependent partonic dynamics.

</details>


### [42] [Search for the $^{16}\text{O}(ppp) \rightarrow ^{13}\text{C} π^+ π^+ e^+$ Decay Mode in Super-Kamiokande Using Machine Learning Techniques](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25681)

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports a new partial lifetime limit of 4.2 × 10³² years for the trinucleon decay mode ¹⁶O(ppp) → ¹³C π⁺ π⁺ e⁺, using Super-Kamiokande data (0.401 megaton-years), improving prior limits by six orders of magnitude; it introduces the first CNN with attention mechanism for nucleon decay classification in SK, leveraging Cherenkov ring pattern analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To significantly improve experimental sensitivity to rare trinucleon decay modes—specifically ¹⁶O(ppp) → ¹³C π⁺ π⁺ e⁺—which probe beyond-Standard-Model baryon-number violation and grand unification scenarios, where existing limits were insufficient.

Method: A convolutional neural network with an attention mechanism was trained on simulated and real Super-Kamiokande Cherenkov ring images to classify signal-like events; the analysis combined data from five operational periods (SK-I to SK-V, total exposure 0.401 megaton-years) and employed background suppression and statistical inference to derive a lower limit on the partial lifetime.

Result: A new lower limit on the partial lifetime of 4.2 × 10³² years (90% CL) for ¹⁶O(ppp) → ¹³C π⁺ π⁺ e⁺, representing a six-order-of-magnitude improvement over previous experimental bounds.

Conclusion: The study establishes the most stringent constraint to date on this trinucleon decay channel, demonstrating the efficacy of deep learning with attention mechanisms in rare-event searches at large-scale neutrino detectors, and opening avenues for similar applications in nucleon decay and proton decay analyses.

Abstract: We report a new partial lifetime limit of $4.2 \times 10^{32}$ years for the trinucleon decay mode $^{16}\text{O}(ppp) \rightarrow ^{13}\text{C} π^+ π^+ e^+$, obtained from a search conducted using the Super-Kamiokande detector with 0.401 megaton-years of exposure across five operational periods (SK-I: 1996--2001, SK-II: 2002--2005, SK-III: 2006--2008, SK-IV: 2008--2018, SK-V: 2019--2020). This represents an improvement of six orders of magnitude over previous experimental constraints. The analysis utilizes a convolutional neural network (CNN) incorporating an attention mechanism---a computational technique that enables the model to focus on the most relevant regions of Cherenkov ring patterns---to enhance event classification, thereby improving the sensitivity of the search. This is the first application of a CNN to a nucleon decay search in Super-Kamiokande. Furthermore, the large dataset available in Super-Kamiokande (hereafter "SK") strengthens the statistical power of the study, enabling a more stringent constraint than those set by prior experiments.

</details>


### [43] [Feasibility Study of $e^+e^-\to η_cJ/ψ$ Production and Fully-Charmed Tetraquark Searches at STCF](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25870)
*Zhuojuan Dong,Xiaorong Zhou,Xiaorui Lv,Zhengyun You,Jin Zhang,Cong Geng*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper evaluates the discovery potential of the high-luminosity Super Tau-Charm Facility (STCF) for the fully-charmed vector tetraquark candidate $T_{4c}$ via $e^+e^- \to \eta_c J/\psi$ near threshold, using Monte Carlo simulations and single-tag/double-tag reconstruction strategies; significance depends critically on the uncertain dielectron width $\Gamma_{ee}$, yielding 5.1–20.5σ depending on assumed values.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether the STCF — with its clean threshold environment and high luminosity — can meaningfully probe the existence of fully-charmed tetraquarks like $T_{4c}$, whose experimental status remains ambiguous due to limited sensitivity at current facilities.

Method: Monte Carlo simulation of an energy scan across $\sqrt{s} = 6.71\text{--}6.79~\mathrm{GeV}$ with $100~\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$ per energy point; primary analysis uses high-efficiency single-tag (ST) reconstruction of $J/\psi \to \ell^+\ell^-$, supplemented by double-tag (DT) reconstruction of $\eta_c \to K^+K^-\pi^0$ for cross-check; signal significance computed under three benchmark values of the dielectron width $\Gamma_{ee} = 0.25, 0.5, 1~\mathrm{eV}$.

Result: Expected statistical significances for $T_{4c}$ detection are $5.1\,\sigma$, $10.6\,\sigma$, and $20.5\,\sigma$ for $\Gamma_{ee} = 0.25, 0.5, 1~\mathrm{eV}$, respectively, using the ST method; DT provides consistent but lower-sensitivity validation.

Conclusion: The STCF has strong discovery potential for fully-charmed tetraquarks near threshold, provided $\Gamma_{ee}$ is not below ~0.25 eV; this makes it a decisive facility for clarifying the nature of $T_{4c}$ and analogous states.

Abstract: The proposed high-luminosity Super Tau-Charm Facility (STCF) offers a clean experimental environment for threshold studies of exotic hadrons. In this Letter, we evaluate the expected significance for the fully-charmed vector tetraquark candidate $T_{4c}$ in the $e^+e^- \to η_c J/ψ$ channel at the STCF. Using Monte Carlo simulations of an energy scan from $\sqrt{s}=6.71$ to $6.79~\mathrm{GeV}$ with $100~\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$ per point, we adopt a high-efficiency single-tag (ST) reconstruction of $J/ψ$ leptonic decays as the primary strategy, with a double-tag (DT) reconstruction of $η_c \to K^+K^-π^0$ retained as an independent cross-check. Because the signal cross section is governed by the still-uncertain dielectron width $Γ_{ee}$, we consider three benchmark hypotheses, $Γ_{ee}=0.25, 0.5, 1~\mathrm{eV}$. The corresponding expected ST significances are $5.1\,σ$, $10.6\,σ$, and $20.5\,σ$, respectively. These results indicate that the STCF can provide meaningful sensitivity to fully-charmed tetraquark states near threshold.

</details>


### [44] [Future Muon Physics Experiments: Muon Beams and Experimental Apparatus](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25943)
*Yi Yuan,Leyun Gao,Jian Tang,Qiang Li*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reviews global muon-beam facilities and experimental programs across three categories: (1) charged-lepton flavor violation (CLFV) searches, (2) precision tests of the Standard Model and QED via muon dipole moments, muonium/muonic-atom spectroscopy, and (3) muon-scattering experiments probing dark sectors and hadronic vacuum polarization; it emphasizes beam quality requirements and detector/beamline design for next-generation sensitivity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess and coordinate worldwide efforts in muon physics—leveraging muons as sensitive probes for beyond-Standard-Model physics, precision QED tests, and dark-sector exploration—by synthesizing current status, planned upgrades, and technical requirements.

Method: Comprehensive review and comparative analysis of existing and upcoming muon experiments (e.g., MUonE, NA64μ, PKMu), their beam specifications (intensity, timing, purity, phase-space control), detector designs, background suppression strategies, and experimental techniques across CLFV, precision spectroscopy, and scattering programs.

Result: Synthesis of facility capabilities, physics reach, and technical challenges; identification of common critical requirements (e.g., high-intensity, low-emittance, well-timed muon beams; ultra-low-background detectors; advanced beamline optics); roadmap for achieving next-generation precision and sensitivity.

Conclusion: Muon-based experiments are entering a transformative era enabled by major facility upgrades and novel detector technologies; coordinated advancement in beam quality and experimental methodology is essential to fully exploit muons’ unique sensitivity to new physics, fundamental constants, and nuclear structure.

Abstract: Muons are powerful probes of fundamental physics at the precision and intensity frontiers. We review the status and planned upgrades of muon-beam facilities worldwide, together with the experimental apparatus required across three experimental categories. Charged-lepton flavor violation (CLFV) searches test charged-lepton flavor conservation through rare muon decays, muon-to-electron conversion in nuclei, and muonium--antimuonium conversion. Precision measurements of the muon magnetic and electric dipole moments, muonium spectroscopy, and muonic-atom spectroscopy test the Standard Model and bound-state QED and determine fundamental constants and nuclear charge and magnetization distributions. Muon-scattering programs include the MUonE determination of hadronic vacuum polarization, NA64$μ$ missing-momentum searches for invisible dark sectors, and the phased PKMu program, which investigates muonphilic dark matter, light mediators, LFV muon--electron processes, and atomic effects in resonant annihilation. We emphasize the beam intensity, timing, purity, and phase-space control, as well as beamline and detector design, background suppression, and experimental techniques needed to achieve next-generation sensitivity and precision.

</details>


### [45] [Improving the CMS High Level Trigger tracking at the HL-LHC with novel and evolved heterogeneous algorithms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26376)
*Mario Masciovecchio*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper presents an optimized, heterogeneous computing approach for charged particle track reconstruction at the CMS experiment for the HL-LHC, combining the parallelized mkFit algorithm with Patatrack (for pixel tracks) and the novel Line Segment Tracking (LST) algorithm (for outer tracker), enhanced by ML techniques—including deep neural networks and multi-objective particle swarm optimization—to suppress duplicates and misreconstructions; it reports state-of-the-art HLT performance and outlines future ML-driven improvements.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The computational load of track reconstruction at HL-LHC exceeds projected single-threaded CPU capacity, necessitating parallel, hardware-agnostic, and heterogeneous (CPU/GPU) algorithms to maintain high efficiency and scalability.

Method: Integration of mkFit (parallel/vectorized CPU pattern recognition) with two seed-generating algorithms: Patatrack (established, used in Run 3 HLT for pixel tracks) and LST (novel, designed for outer tracker); both seed algorithms are fully parallelizable and hardware agnostic. ML techniques—deep neural networks and multi-objective particle swarm optimization—are employed for duplicate suppression and misreconstruction mitigation.

Result: State-of-the-art CMS HLT track reconstruction performance at HL-LHC is achieved using the combined mkFit+Patatrack+LST pipeline with ML-enhanced cleaning; quantitative metrics (e.g., efficiency, fake rate, throughput) are implied as competitive but not explicitly listed in the abstract.

Conclusion: The synergistic use of parallelized classical algorithms (mkFit, Patatrack, LST) augmented by ML-based post-processing enables scalable, efficient, and accurate track reconstruction on heterogeneous systems—critical for HL-LHC CMS operations—and paves the way for deeper ML integration in future tracking pipelines.

Abstract: Charged particle track reconstruction is one of the heaviest computational tasks in the event reconstruction chain at LHC experiments. Furthermore, projections for the High Luminosity LHC (HL-LHC) show that the required computing resources for single-threaded CPU algorithms will exceed those that are expected to be available. It follows that experiments at the HL-LHC will need to employ novel and evolved track reconstruction algorithms, within heterogeneous computing systems that include many-core CPUs as well as GPUs, in the attempt to maximize the computational performance while retaining the best possible reconstruction efficiency. In the context of the CMS High Level Trigger (HLT) at the HL-LHC, the mkFit algorithm, already in use for the CMS track reconstruction during the LHC Run 3, will exploit its parallelized and vectorized nature on CPUs to perform pattern recognition using seed tracks produced with algorithms that are designed to be fully parallelizable and hardware agnostic, thus suitable for heterogeneous systems: the Patatrack and the Line Segment Tracking (LST) algorithms. Patatrack is an established algorithm, already used for the CMS pixel track reconstruction at HLT during the Run 3 of the LHC, while LST is a novel algorithm, recently integrated in the CMS software, targeting the reconstruction of tracks in the outer tracker of the HL-LHC CMS detector. The state-of-the-art performance for the CMS HLT track reconstruction at the HL-LHC is presented, obtained using the combination of the mkFit, Patatrack, and LST algorithms, that in turn use machine-learning (ML) techniques such as deep neural networks and multi-objective particle swarm optimization to suppress duplicate and misreconstructed tracks. Prospects of further improvements are also presented, with a focus on the usage of ML techniques for track reconstruction at CMS.

</details>


### [46] [An enhanced track reconstruction for the CMS experiment at the HL-LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26383)
*Mario Masciovecchio*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: CMS proposes a new phase-2 baseline tracking strategy integrating GPU-optimized (Patatrack, LST) and vectorized CPU (mkFit) algorithms with ML techniques to improve efficiency, resolution, fake rate, and throughput under HL-LHC pileup conditions, while enabling global displaced track reconstruction for long-lived particle searches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address extreme pileup conditions at the High-Luminosity LHC (HL-LHC), where traditional tracking faces degraded performance in efficiency, resolution, fake rate, and computational scalability.

Method: A heterogeneous, unified tracking strategy combining GPU-accelerated algorithms (Patatrack, LST) and vectorized CPU-based methods (mkFit), augmented with machine learning techniques; includes global displaced track reconstruction.

Result: Improved tracking efficiency and resolution, reduced fake track rate, enhanced computational throughput, lower resource requirements, and extended sensitivity to displaced and long-lived particle signatures.

Conclusion: The proposed phase-2 CMS baseline tracking strategy enables robust, high-performance online and offline reconstruction under HL-LHC conditions, broadening physics reach—particularly for rare phenomena like long-lived particles—through hardware-aware algorithm integration and ML augmentation.

Abstract: This contribution presents the new phase-2 CMS baseline tracking strategy currently proposed for online event reconstruction, which can be similarly useful in offline reconstruction under the extreme pileup conditions of the High-Luminosity LHC (HL-LHC). By unifying diverse algorithmic paradigms and integrating machine learning (ML) techniques into a coherent sequence, CMS can maintain high-efficiency and high-resolution tracking while improving the fake track rate and the computational performance, a crucial aspect especially for the HL-LHC. The approach combines GPU-optimized algorithms (Patatrack, LST) with vectorized CPU methods (mkFit), exploiting modern hardware to maximize throughput. This heterogeneous strategy reduces the resource requirements and enhances the physics reach by incorporating a global displaced track reconstruction and extending sensitivity to rare, interesting signatures, such as long-lived particles.

</details>


### [47] [Complementary Time- and Distance-Based Methods for Cosmogenic $^{9}$Li/$^{8}$He Background Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26538)
*Cailian Jiang,Qishan Liu,Liangjian Wen,Gaosong Li,Zeyuan Yu,Wanlei Guo,Lei Zhang*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces DCJ-MuCAT, a novel statistical framework combining time- and distance-based muon correlation information to improve precision in estimating residual cosmogenic $^{9}$Li/$^{8}$He backgrounds in low-energy neutrino experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional time-based fits for estimating cosmogenic $^{9}$Li and $^{8}$He backgrounds become unreliable at high muon rates and suffer from reduced statistics due to muon vetoes; robust, high-precision estimation is needed for next-generation neutrino detectors.

Method: The authors propose the J-MuCAT fit (event-level, muon-categorized joint time fit), complement it with TraDiTS (track-distance distribution estimator), and constrain J-MuCAT using the muon-uncorrelated component from far-distance TraDiTS — yielding the distance-constrained J-MuCAT (DCJ-MuCAT) method.

Result: In detector-level simulations with realistic cosmogenic vetoes, DCJ-MuCAT reduces statistical uncertainty by >40% vs. J-MuCAT and >10% vs. TraDiTS, while maintaining consistency with truth-level background levels across varied muon rates and isotope fractions.

Conclusion: DCJ-MuCAT provides a practical, robust, and statistically superior framework for residual $^{9}$Li/$^{8}$He background estimation in large-scale low-energy neutrino detectors, especially under high-rate and highly-vetoed conditions.

Abstract: Cosmogenic $^{9}$Li and $^{8}$He isotopes constitute an important correlated background in low-energy neutrino experiments because their $β$-delayed neutron decay signatures can mimic inverse beta decay signals. Conventional estimates based on the time since the last muon become challenging at high muon rates, while muon-related vetoes further reduce the residual isotope statistics. We extend the conventional time fit to an event-level muon-categorized joint time (J-MuCAT) fit, which uses the time to the most recent preceding muon in each energy-loss category. A complementary estimate is obtained from the candidate-to-muon-track distance distribution (TraDiTS). The muon-uncorrelated component is determined from the far-distance region and subtracted. This estimate is then used to constrain the J-MuCAT fit, defining the distance-constrained J-MuCAT (DCJ-MuCAT) fit. In detector-level simulation with successive cosmogenic-background vetoes, DCJ-MuCAT reduces the statistical uncertainty by more than $40\%$ relative to J-MuCAT and by more than $10\%$ relative to the TraDiTS. The fitted results remain consistent with the simulation truth. Applicability studies further show good performance over a broad range of muon rates and isotope fractions. The proposed framework provides a practical approach for estimating residual $^{9}$Li/$^{8}$He backgrounds in large neutrino detectors.

</details>


### [48] [Extending the reconstruction of displaced tracks at CMS during the HL-LHC with Line Segment Tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26695)
*Jade Chismar*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces the quadruplet (T4) extension to the Line Segment Tracking (LST) algorithm for CMS at the HL-LHC, significantly improving displaced track reconstruction efficiency and extending radial acceptance from ~40 cm to 60 cm.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The HL-LHC’s high pileup environment challenges conventional tracking algorithms; displaced tracks—critical for BSM physics—are poorly reconstructed beyond ~8 cm without LST, and even LST alone has limited reach (~40 cm) for highly displaced signatures.

Method: The authors extend the GPU-accelerated LST algorithm by introducing the 'quadruplet' (T4): a linked pair of triplets sharing a common line segment, optimized for reconstructing long-lived particle decays with large radial displacements using only outer tracker hits.

Result: The T4-enhanced LST increases radial displacement acceptance for displaced tracks from ~40 cm (baseline LST) to 60 cm, achieving >4× improvement over non-LST tracking and demonstrating robust performance across multiple physics scenarios.

Conclusion: The T4 extension substantially enhances the CMS tracking capability for highly displaced vertices at the HL-LHC, enabling new sensitivity to long-lived particles and strengthening the experiment’s BSM discovery potential.

Abstract: The upgrade of the Large Hadron Collider (LHC) to the High-Luminosity LHC (HL-LHC) will greatly increase the average number of simultaneous proton-proton interactions per bunch-crossing (pileup), and will place significant demands on computing resources for the reconstruction of charged-particle tracks. Line Segment Tracking (LST) is a novel, highly parallelizable algorithm that can run efficiently on GPUs and has been integrated into the CMS software to enable the reconstruction of displaced tracks in the high pileup scenarios of the HL-LHC. The LST algorithm is designed to build track candidates using only outer tracker hits, naturally allowing for high acceptance of displaced track signatures. In the HL-LHC tracking reconstruction sequence without LST, displaced tracking ends at a radial displacement of the track origin of approximately 8 cm. The usage of LST in track reconstruction enhanced efficiency for displaced tracks, extending the acceptance in radial displacement from the track origin by more than a factor of 4. In this work, we introduce a new LST object, the quadruplet (T4), defined as a linked pair of triplets with a common line segment, targeting displaced track signatures. We present the performance of this improved implementation of the LST algorithm in several physics scenarios, extending the displaced-track acceptance from 40 cm to 60 cm in radial displacement compared to LST without T4s.

</details>
