### [49] [Gradient Flow for the Jet Transport Coefficient: A Quenched Lattice Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25089)
*Hai-Tao Shu,Cheng Zhang*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper computes the jet transport coefficient $\hat q$ in quenched lattice QCD using gradient flow, finding $\hat q/T^3 \simeq 1.1$ above $T_c$, with strong suppression below it; results validate gradient-flow renormalization for future full-QCD studies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To nonperturbatively determine the medium-induced jet transport coefficient $\hat q$ in a thermal gluon plasma, addressing operator renormalization challenges—especially critical for extending to full QCD where quark-gluon operator mixing must be controlled.

Method: Uses gradient-flow-based energy-momentum tensor on quenched lattice QCD ensembles across $0.76T_c \leq T \leq 2.25T_c$; extracts $\hat q/T^3$ via operator-product expansion, separating perturbative short-distance coefficient (1-loop) and nonperturbative entropy density $s/T^3$ from gradient-flow-renormalized observables at three lattice spacings per temperature.

Result: $\hat q/T^3$ is strongly suppressed below $T_c$, rises sharply across deconfinement, then plateaus near $1.1$ in the deconfined phase; statistical agreement with prior quenched result confirms robustness of gradient-flow renormalization.

Conclusion: Gradient flow provides a systematic, renormalization-group-controlled framework for computing $\hat q$, essential for reliable extension to full QCD where quark operator renormalization and gluonic mixing must be consistently handled.

Abstract: We apply the gradient-flow framework to determine the nonperturbative medium contribution to the jet transport coefficient $\hat q$ using quenched lattice QCD in the temperature range $0.76T_c \leq T \leq 2.25T_c$. The calculation focuses on the leading-twist contribution at leading order for an asymptotically energetic quark propagating through a thermal pure-gluon plasma. In the infinite-energy limit, the operator-product expansion expresses $\hat q/T^3$ in pure gauge theory as the product of a perturbative short-distance coefficient and the entropy density $s/T^3$. The latter is determined from the gradient-flow energy-momentum tensor at three lattice spacings for each temperature and the former is estimated perturbatively at one-loop order. We find that $\hat q/T^3$ is strongly suppressed below $T_c$, rises rapidly across the deconfinement transition, and remains approximately constant at $\hat q/T^3\simeq1.1$ throughout the deconfined temperature range studied. This is statistically consistent with the previous quenched lattice determination based on a different renormalization procedure, providing a nontrivial validation of the gradient-flow framework for the jet transport coefficient. The systematic treatment of operator renormalization and matching provided by gradient flow becomes particularly important in full QCD, because the need to renormalize quark operators and account for their mixing with the gluonic sector can likewise be addressed within the same framework.

</details>


### [50] [Experiment $\leftrightarrow$ lattice QCD: understanding high-temperature QCD matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25191)
*Bedangadas Mohanty*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper reviews the interplay between relativistic heavy-ion collision experiments and lattice QCD calculations in probing the thermodynamic and transport properties of QCD matter, emphasizing challenges in bridging equilibrium lattice results with dynamical, finite-size collision systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the gap between experimental observables from heavy-ion collisions and first-principles lattice QCD predictions—especially for the equation of state, transport coefficients, conserved-charge fluctuations, and the QCD critical point—while addressing inherent limitations in comparing equilibrium theory with non-equilibrium, finite-system experiments.

Method: Critical review and synthesis of recent theoretical (lattice QCD) and experimental (RHIC, LHC, FAIR, NICA, EIC) developments, with emphasis on quantitative comparisons, error propagation, and systematic uncertainties in extracting thermodynamic and transport quantities.

Result: Emergence of quantitative, temperature- and baryon chemical potential–dependent tests of QCD thermodynamics; improved constraints on the equation of state, shear and bulk viscosities, higher-order cumulants of net-baryon number, and narrowing of the critical point search region.

Conclusion: A synergistic, multi-facility effort combining lattice QCD, hydrodynamic modeling, and high-precision experiments is essential to map the QCD phase diagram and validate non-perturbative QCD predictions under extreme conditions.

Abstract: Relativistic heavy-ion collisions provide a unique experimental opportunity to study strongly interacting matter at extreme temperature and density, while lattice quantum chromodynamics (QCD) offers a first-principles approach to the equilibrium properties of such matter in the non-perturbative regime. The interplay between experiment and lattice QCD has therefore become central to establishing the properties and phase structure of QCD matter. Selected areas where this connection is particularly informative are discussed, including the QCD equation of state and its role in hydrodynamic descriptions of heavy-ion collisions, transport properties of the quark-gluon plasma, conserved-charge fluctuations and their relation to experimental cumulants, and the ongoing search for a critical point in the QCD phase diagram. Particular attention is given to the limitations involved in confronting equilibrium lattice calculations with the finite, dynamical and experimentally constrained systems produced in heavy-ion collisions. Recent developments increasingly allow quantitative tests of QCD thermodynamics over an extended range of temperature and baryon chemical potential. The continuing experimental programmes at RHIC and the LHC, together with future measurements at FAIR, NICA and the Electron-Ion Collider, provide important opportunities for an increasingly close interplay between lattice QCD, phenomenology and experiment.

</details>

### [51] [Loop Equations for Multi-Matrix Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25095)
*Aravinth Kulanthaivelu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper revisits and refines the combinatorial loop-equation method to compute planar disc amplitudes in multi-matrix models; it organizes Schwinger-Dyson constraints by boundary insertion level, derives algebraic equations for disc resolvents—including a new one for the three-state Potts model with unequal couplings—and extends applicability to previously unsolved two- and three-matrix models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a systematic, algebraic alternative to saddle-point methods for computing planar disc amplitudes in multi-matrix models—especially beyond standard one- and two-matrix cases—by leveraging and refining the combinatorial loop-equation approach.

Method: A refined combinatorial loop-equation method that classifies Schwinger-Dyson constraints by the level (i.e., number and type) of boundary insertions; when the constraint system closes at finite level, it yields an algebraic equation for the planar disc resolvent (or partition function).

Result: Recovery of known disc partition functions for fixed, mixed, and free boundaries in the three-state Potts model; derivation of a novel algebraic equation for the fixed-boundary resolvent with unequal quadratic and cubic couplings; successful computation of disc partition functions for several unsolved two- and three-matrix models.

Conclusion: The organized loop-equation method is a practical, algebraic tool for solving planar disc sectors of multi-matrix models—including non-standard and previously intractable cases—demonstrating broader applicability than traditional saddle-point or one-/two-matrix techniques.

Abstract: We revisit the combinatorial loop-equation method for computing planar disc amplitudes in multi-matrix models with polynomial potentials. We develop a procedure which organises the Schwinger-Dyson constraints by the level of their boundary insertions and, when the resulting system closes, produces an algebraic equation for the planar disc function. Using this method we recover the disc partition functions associated with fixed, mixed, and free boundary conditions in the three-state Potts model, previously obtained by saddle-point methods. As a novelty we derive an algebraic equation for the fixed-boundary resolvent of the three-state Potts model with unequal quadratic and cubic couplings. We further show one can compute the disc partition function for several unsolved two- and three-matrix models. These examples demonstrate that loop equations provide a practical route to identifying and solving sectors of multi-matrix models beyond the standard one- and two-matrix cases.

</details>


### [52] [On Thermodynamic Universalities and Optics of B-deformed Reissner--Nordström--AdS Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25161)
*Adil Belhaj,Maryem Jemri*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper uses machine learning to analyze thermodynamic (Van der Waals criticality, Joule-Thomson expansion) and optical (shadow) properties of B-deformed Reissner–Nordström–AdS black holes—string-theoretic deformations tied to the NS-NS B-field—and constrains deformation parameters using EHT data from M87* and Sgr A*.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how string-theoretic non-commutative geometry deformations—encoded via a B-field parameter—affect black hole thermodynamics and optics, and to develop ML tools capable of detecting universal critical behavior and fitting observational shadow data.

Method: Combines high-precision numerical computation of thermodynamic critical ratios and Joule-Thomson coefficients with supervised training of a fully connected neural network to classify Van der Waals behavior; additionally applies ML-informed parameter estimation to fit black hole shadow observables using EHT data for M87* and Sgr A*.

Result: The trained neural network achieves high accuracy in identifying Van der Waals-type P-V criticality in B-deformed black holes; thermodynamic universal ratios deviate from standard values due to B-deformation; shadow size and shape are sensitive to the B-parameter, which is constrained to narrow ranges consistent with M87* and Sgr A* observations.

Conclusion: B-deformation induces measurable, classifiable deviations in both thermodynamic criticality and optical shadows of AdS black holes; ML methods serve as robust, quantitative bridges between string-motivated theoretical deformations and astrophysical observables.

Abstract: Using machine learning techniques, we study the Reissner--Nordström--AdS black holes in the framework of extended space-time derivatives inspired by non-commutative geometry in string theory where the deformation parameters are related to the inverse of the NS-NS B-field. We refer to these solutions as B-deformed Reissner--Nordström--AdS black holes. Precisely, we examine the thermodynamic and the optical behaviors of such stringy deformed AdS black holes. Exploiting advanced numerical computations, we first examine the corresponding thermodynamic Van der Waals behaviors by calculating the universal ratios describing the P-V criticality and the Joule-Thomson expansion features. Applying such numerical results, we construct reliable training data for a fully connected neural network being developed to determine whether such stringy black hole configurations exhibit thermodynamic Van der Waals aspects. Concretely, we reveal that the fully connected and trained neural network accurately detects Van der Waals behaviors. Supported by the observational data reported by the Event Horizon Telescope international collaborations, we investigate the optical shadows of such B-deformed Reissner--Nordström--AdS black holes using machine learning methods. In order to provide corroborated models, we constraint the involved parameters including the stringy one B by making use of M87* and Sgr A* black hole empirical data.

</details>


### [53] [The landscape and the swampland of O(3,3) gauged supergravities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25203)
*Mariana Graña,Diego Perugini*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper classifies 7D half-maximal gauged supergravities using double field theory and orbifold twists to distinguish swampland (non-orbifold-realizable) gaugings from the consistent string landscape.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine which half-maximal 7D gauged supergravities can be embedded in string theory via geometric or asymmetric orbifolds—and thereby identify those in the swampland—by leveraging T-duality covariant flux parametrization from double field theory.

Method: Systematic classification of all half-maximal 7D gauged supergravities; construction of a dictionary mapping orbifold twist actions to generalized (geometric and non-geometric) fluxes in double field theory; identification of gaugings realizable as (a)symmetric orbifolds versus those that are not.

Result: A complete catalog of 7D half-maximal gaugings is partitioned into those admitting (a)symmetric orbifold realizations and those that do not; the latter constitute concrete candidates for the swampland.

Conclusion: Not all consistent-looking 7D half-maximal gauged supergravities arise from string compactifications; the non-orbifold-realizable ones are strong swampland candidates, sharpening the boundary between landscape and swampland using duality-covariant flux data.

Abstract: Compactifications of string theory on group manifolds with fluxes, performed via the Scherk-Schwarz mechanism, give rise to gauged supergravities. Using double field theory, this procedure can be made T-duality covariant, yielding a set of geometric and non-geometric fluxes whose string theory origin is often unclear. In this paper, we systematically analyze the complete catalog of half-maximal gauged supergravities in seven dimensions. By constructing a dictionary between orbifold twists and generalized fluxes, we identify which gaugings admit a realization as (a)symmetric orbifolds, and which do not, establishing the latter as candidates for the gauged supergravity swampland.

</details>


### [54] [Quantum Clausius relation beyond de Sitter equilibrium](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25206)
*Jinn-Ouk Gong,TaeHun Kim,Junghwan Lee,Chang Sub Shin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives a quantum Clausius relation for conformal fields in quasi-de Sitter spacetime, matching heat flow and entropy evolution at leading order without invoking gravitational equations; beyond leading order, it establishes a scheme-independent entropy balance incorporating Wald entropy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a quantum field theoretic foundation for dynamical horizon thermodynamics beyond exact de Sitter equilibrium, bypassing reliance on classical gravitational equations of motion.

Method: Computes net heat flow across the apparent horizon from the renormalized stress-energy tensor and independently determines time evolution of renormalized von Neumann entropy via the replica method, within a Hubble-flow expansion framework.

Result: Exact agreement between heat flow and entropy change at leading order in Hubble-flow expansion; beyond leading order, a scheme-independent entropy balance emerges that incorporates Wald entropy.

Conclusion: The results realize dynamical horizon thermodynamics from first-principles quantum field theory in curved spacetime, confirming a quantum Clausius relation without assuming gravitational dynamics.

Abstract: We establish a direct quantum Clausius relation for conformal fields inside the apparent horizon of a quasi-de Sitter spacetime. We compute the net heat flow across the horizon from the renormalized stress-energy tensor, and independently determine the time evolution of the renormalized von Neumann entropy using the replica method. At leading order in the Hubble-flow expansion, the two calculations agree exactly, without using the gravitational equations of motion. Beyond leading order, we find a scheme-independent entropy balance that includes the corresponding Wald entropy. Our results provide a quantum field theoretic realization of dynamical horizon thermodynamics beyond exact de Sitter equilibrium.

</details>


### [55] [A Quantum-Mechanical Model for the M5-Brane](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25207)
*Manuel Artime,Ralph Blumenhagen,Thomas Raml*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a maximally supersymmetric extension of the BFSS matrix model using the 5-Lie algebra A₆, yielding a Lagrangian theory with Chern-Simons-like dynamics for structure constants; it emerges from lightcone M5-brane theory on S⁵ and serves as a quantum-mechanical model for lowest-lying M5-brane modes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a supersymmetric matrix model capturing essential features of M5-branes—particularly their higher-form gauge structure and 5-bracket algebra—beyond the standard BFSS (2-bracket) framework.

Method: Uses the 5-Lie algebra A₆ to generalize BFSS; introduces dynamical structure constants governed by a Chern-Simons-type kinetic term; derives the model via consistent truncation of the single lightcone M5-brane action on S⁵ to zero-modes.

Result: Obtains a fully Lagrangian, maximally supersymmetric quantum-mechanical model based on A₆; demonstrates its derivation from M5-brane theory on S⁵; establishes it as a consistent truncation supporting an interpretation as the low-energy effective theory for M5-brane zero-modes.

Conclusion: The A₆-based model provides a viable, supersymmetric matrix-theoretic description of M5-brane dynamics at the quantum-mechanical level, bridging 5-Lie algebra structure, Chern-Simons-like dynamics, and M-theory compactifications.

Abstract: We present a maximally supersymmetric extension of the BFSS matrix model based on the 5-Lie algebra ${\mathcal A}_6$. The theory is Lagrangian, promotes the BFSS 2-bracket structure constants to dynamical objects governed by a Chern-Simons-like kinetic term, and displays features expected from a theory of M5-branes. We show that it can also be obtained from the single lightcone M5-brane action on a spatial five-sphere after a truncation to the time coordinate. The resulting theory is a consistent truncation and, analogously to the $U(2)$ BFSS model, supports an interpretation of the $\mathcal{A}_6$ theory as a quantum-mechanical model for the lowest-lying modes of the M5-brane.

</details>


### [56] [The Double Copy of the Black Ring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25209)
*Damien A. Easson*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs an exact Weyl double copy for the 5D singly spinning Emparan–Reall black ring, showing its Weyl curvature decomposes into two source-free Maxwell fields (one from the stationary Killing vector, one carrying magnetic flux) with shared scalar wave equation; it extends exact Weyl doubling beyond algebraically special (type D/N) cases to a type-G/I_i spacetime and confirms genuine De Smet type 22 via factorization of the Weyl polynomial.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the exact Weyl double copy formalism beyond known algebraically special (type-D and type-N) solutions to more generic black hole geometries—specifically, the topologically nontrivial, rotating 5D black ring—and to test the scope and limitations of double-copy structures in higher dimensions.

Method: Explicit construction of the Weyl tensor for the Emparan–Reall black ring; decomposition into a sum of two scalar-weighted squares of source-free Maxwell fields on the same background; identification of their scalar partners satisfying a common sourced wave equation; algebraic classification via Weyl polynomial factorization to determine De Smet type.

Result: The full nonlinear Weyl curvature is exactly expressed as a sum of two distinct, scalar-weighted Maxwell squares; both Maxwell fields are source-free and live on the black-ring geometry; their scalar partners obey a shared sourced wave equation; the Weyl polynomial factorizes into two irreducible quadratics, confirming De Smet type 22 and excluding reduction to a single Maxwell square.

Conclusion: The singly spinning 5D black ring admits an exact Weyl double copy, demonstrating that the formalism applies beyond type-D/N spacetimes to geometries with mixed Weyl types (G and I_i) and nontrivial topology; this establishes genuine De Smet type 22 and broadens the class of solutions compatible with double-copy structures in five dimensions.

Abstract: We construct an exact Weyl double copy of the singly spinning five-dimensional Emparan--Reall black ring. Its complete nonlinear Weyl curvature is the sum of two scalar-weighted Maxwell squares. One field is generated by the stationary Killing vector; the other carries topological magnetic flux through a sphere linking the ring. Both are source-free Maxwell fields on the black-ring spacetime, with corresponding scalar partners obeying a common sourced wave equation. This extends exact five-dimensional Weyl doubling beyond the type-$\mathrm D$ and type-$\mathrm N$ examples to a black hole whose exterior contains both type-$\mathrm G$ and type-$\mathrm{I}_i$ regions. The Weyl polynomial factorizes into two distinct irreducible quadratics, establishing genuine De Smet type $22$, thereby ruling out a reduction to a single Maxwell square.

</details>


### [57] [Twisted \texorpdfstring{$A_{2N}$}{A(2N)} Argyres--Douglas theories: vertex algebras, Higgs branches, and double covers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25212)
*Christopher Beem,Harshal Kulkarni*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper explores the relationship between two families of generalized Argyres–Douglas SCFTs (twisted A_{2N} and D_{N+1} series), proposing that the A-series arise via nilpotent Higgsing from the D-series; this leads to structural consequences for their associated vertex operator algebras (VOAs) — namely, they are finite extensions of affine Kac–Moody VOAs at non-boundary admissible levels — and their Higgs branches, often double covers of nilpotent orbit closures; further, Z₂-gauged A-series theories yield unitary SCFTs whose associated VOAs *are* exactly those affine Kac–Moody algebras, equipped with a novel R-filtration compatible with graded unitarity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the structural relationship between twisted A_{2N} and D_{N+1} generalized Argyres–Douglas theories, especially how their associated VOAs and Higgs branches are related, and to identify new examples of unitary SCFTs whose associated VOAs realize affine Kac–Moody algebras at non-boundary admissible levels.

Method: Analysis of nilpotent Higgsing patterns, comparison of associated VOAs and Higgs branches across the two series, use of representation-theoretic constraints (e.g., admissible levels, finite extensions), and examination of Z₂ gauging as a tool to isolate pure affine Kac–Moody VOAs; incorporation of R-filtration and graded unitarity constraints.

Result: The A-series theories are realized as nilpotent Higgsings of the D-series; their associated VOAs are finite extensions (not bare affine Kac–Moody VOAs) at non-boundary admissible levels; their Higgs branches are frequently double covers of nilpotent orbit closures; Z₂ gauging yields unitary SCFTs whose associated VOAs are precisely those affine Kac–Moody VOAs, endowed with a non-standard R-filtration compatible with graded unitarity.

Conclusion: The twisted A_{2N} and D_{N+1} Argyres–Douglas theories are hierarchically related via nilpotent Higgsing, leading to precise predictions for their chiral algebras and moduli spaces; this provides a unified framework for constructing and classifying VOAs associated to non-Lagrangian SCFTs, and identifies new unitary realizations of affine Kac–Moody VOAs at admissible levels.

Abstract: We investigate the associated vertex operator algebras and Higgs branches of two closely related families of generalised Argyres--Douglas theories, arising from the twisted $A_{2N}$ and twisted $D_{N+1}$ series, respectively. We propose that the $A$-series theories are realised as nilpotent Higgsings of the $D$-series, and we assemble a variety of pieces of evidence for this proposal. A consequence is that the associated vertex operator algebras of the $A$-series are finite extensions of affine Kac--Moody vertex algebras at (non-boundary) admissible levels, rather than those Kac--Moody algebras themselves, and that their Higgs branches are in many cases double covers of nilpotent orbit closures. The $\mathbb{Z}_2$ gaugings of the $A$-series theories then furnish examples of unitary SCFTs whose associated vertex operator algebras are precisely affine Kac--Moody algebras at non-boundary admissible levels. We propose that these vertex algebras are equipped with a non-standard $\mathfrak{R}$-filtration, and comment on compatibility with graded unitarity.

</details>


### [58] [Classification of order-two T-duality orbifolds at the SO(12) free fermionic point](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25213)
*Alon E. Faraggi,Stefan Groot Nibbelink,Benjamin Percival*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper classifies order-two asymmetric orbifold T-folds on the SO(12) lattice in type II string theory, identifies their spectra via generalized GSO projections, finds minimal Hodge numbers (1,1) for six cases, conjectures asymmetric mirror symmetry, identifies orientifoldable configurations, and argues that spin-3/2 states imply supersymmetric reorganization and vanishing vacuum energy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct and classify non-geometric T-fold backgrounds using asymmetric Z2 orbifolds, explore their phenomenological and mathematical structure—including supersymmetry, Hodge numbers, mirror symmetry, and orientifold compatibility—and clarify the role of spin-3/2 states in enforcing supersymmetry.

Method: Systematic classification of all Z2 point groups acting asymmetrically on the six-torus; fermionic construction on the SO(12) lattice at the self-dual point; parametric derivation of spectra using generalized GSO projection phases; analysis of twisted sectors, Hodge numbers, orientifold projections (worldsheet parity Ω), and supersymmetry conditions.

Result: Complete classification of order-two asymmetric T-folds on SO(12); six models with minimal effective Hodge numbers (h₁₁,h₁₂)=(1,1); identification of orientifoldable configurations; discovery that pure asymmetric twisted sectors can host Rarita-Schwinger multiplets; identification of non-supersymmetric T-folds whose spin-3/2 states imply supersymmetric spectrum reorganization and zero vacuum energy.

Conclusion: Asymmetric Z2 T-folds provide controlled non-geometric string vacua with rich geometric (Hodge), symmetry (mirror, orientifold), and supersymmetry structure; the presence of massless spin-3/2 states is a robust indicator—not just of supergravity—but of full supersymmetric vacuum organization, even when the original GSO projection appears non-supersymmetric.

Abstract: Asymmetric orbifolds provide concrete examples of non-geometric constructions dubbed T-folds. All Z2 point groups of six dimensional asymmetric order-two orbifolds are identified. The order-two T-fold configurations on the SO(12) lattice are classified at the fermionic point of type II string theories for all Z2 point groups. The spectra of the models on these configurations are presented parametrically, in terms of certain generalised GSO phases. The minimal effective Hodge numbers were found to be (h_{11},h_{12})=(1,1) for six T-fold configurations. Asymmetric generalisations of the mirror symmetry map are conjectured. The orientifoldable configurations using the basic worldsheet parity were identified within the classification. Twisted sectors corresponding to pure asymmetric twists may contain Rarita-Schwinger multiplets with spin-3/2 states. Throughout the paper, generalised GSO projections are chosen to preserve the maximal amount of supersymmetry possible. Relaxing this, the order-two point groups were identified for which non--supersymmetric T-folds can be constructed. Since some of them may enhance to N=1 or, even, N=2 supergravities, we argue that appearance of spin-3/2 states necessarily implies that the spectrum has reorder itself in a supersymmetric fashion and hence that the vacuum energy vanishes.

</details>


### [59] [Bulk OPE Coefficients of the $E$-Series Virasoro Minimal Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25215)
*Amaury Lhoste,Jiaxin Qiao,Masahito Yamazaki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes bulk-primary OPE coefficients for Virasoro minimal models with E6, E7, or E8 modular invariants, reducing the conformal bootstrap to a finite algebraic problem via quantum-group 6j symbols; existence/uniqueness is proven conditional on certified seed data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To solve the conformal bootstrap for exceptional modular invariant minimal models by exploiting quantum-group structure and reducing infinite constraints to finite algebraic ones.

Method: Divides bulk OPE coefficients by universal chiral generalized-minimal-model factors, maps remainder to root-of-unity quantum-group 6j symbols; uses computer-assisted algebraic search for s=1 seed data and proves propagation to all Kac labels via genus-zero locality and crossing symmetry.

Result: Finite sets of reduced OPE coefficients (18, 32, 107 for E6/E7/E8); high-precision numerical evidence for existence/uniqueness of seeds; conditional proof that certified seeds imply full existence/uniqueness across all Kac labels.

Conclusion: The exceptional minimal models' bulk OPE coefficients are fully determined—up to signs of primary fields—by finitely many algebraic data points governed by quantum-group 6j symbols, assuming only Virasoro symmetry, bulk locality, and OPE associativity.

Abstract: We present bulk-primary operator-product coefficients for every Virasoro minimal model with an $E_6$, $E_7$, or $E_8$ modular invariant. Dividing by a universal product of chiral generalized-minimal-model OPE coefficients reduces the conformal bootstrap problem to a finite algebraic problem governed by root-of-unity quantum-group $6j$ symbols. The exceptional factors of the OPE coefficients can be chosen independent of the second Kac indices $s$, and their dependence on the central charge reduces to finitely many reference cases and explicit phases. A finite computer-assisted calculation yields candidate algebraic $s=1$ seed data and high-precision numerical evidence for existence and uniqueness modulo primary-field signs. We prove that exact existence and uniqueness for these seeds imply the corresponding result for all Kac labels within the genus-zero bulk locality and crossing equations; the finite seed step remains uncertified. The three families require $18$, $32$, and $107$ representative reduced OPE coefficients for $E_6$, $E_7$, and $E_8$, respectively. We provide reconstruction conventions and coefficient tables for both unitary and nonunitary models. Our calculation assumes only Virasoro symmetry, bulk locality (permutation symmetry) and OPE associativity (crossing equations).

</details>


### [60] [Functional Dimensional Regularization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25238)
*Piero Beretta,Alessandro Codello*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Introduces Functional Dimensional Regularization (FDR), a non-perturbative functional RG framework unifying dimensional regularization with functional methods; recovers ε-expansion, computes 3D Ising exponents with high accuracy, and provides a systematic 'distillation' procedure from other one-loop-exact RG schemes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend dimensional regularization beyond perturbation theory and embed its technical simplicity into a fully functional, non-perturbative renormalization group framework that retains scheme independence and critical dimension universality.

Method: Constructs FDR beta functions as continuous-dimension sums over scheme-independent DR beta functions at all critical dimensions; employs local potential approximation and second-order derivative expansion; introduces a 'distillation' procedure to derive FDR flows from arbitrary one-loop-exact RG schemes.

Result: Recovers all multi-critical ε-expansions; establishes correspondence with functional scaling solutions and eigen-perturbation spectra; computes η ≈ 0.036–0.039 and accurate RG spectrum for 3D Ising; demonstrates rapid convergence and strong agreement with benchmark results.

Conclusion: FDR is a self-contained, competitive functional RG framework that successfully merges the algebraic elegance of dimensional regularization with the non-perturbative power and versatility of functional methods.

Abstract: We introduce and develop Functional Dimensional Regularization (FDR), a novel functional renormalization group (RG) framework that extends the principles of dimensional regularization beyond the perturbative $\varepsilon$-expansion. The key insight is to define the FDR beta functions, valid in any continuous dimension $d$ and characterized by threshold functions, as a sum over all critical dimensions of the corresponding scheme-independent beta functions computed in DR. We demonstrate that this formalism exhibits all the hallmarks of a fully-fledged functional RG. Within the local potential approximation, we perform a general fixed point analysis, recovering the $\varepsilon$-expansion for all multi-critical models and establishing a direct connection to functional scaling solutions, spike plots, and eigen-perturbation spectra. Extending the framework to the second order of the derivative expansion, we compute the critical exponents for the Ising universality class in $d=3$, providing estimates for the anomalous dimension $η$ and RG spectrum that converge rapidly and show favorable agreement with state-of-the-art results. We present a detailed comparison with non-perturbative and proper-time RG approaches, highlighting structural similarities and key differences. In particular, we introduce a ``distillation'' procedure that allows one to systematically derive the FDR flow from any other one-loop-exact RG scheme. Our results establish FDR as a self-contained and competitive RG framework that combines the technical simplicity of dimensional regularization with the versatility of the functional approach.

</details>


### [61] [Anyon Crystallization by Statistics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25249)
*Zohar Komargodski,Xuzixiang Lou,Ivri Nagar,Domenico Orlando,Susanne Reffert,Amit Sever*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the anyon gas in the near-bosonic limit (large N, fixed N/k), finding a crystalline ground state with full screening of statistical interactions—hence no superfluidity—leading to a quantum phase transition at k_c ≈ 2–5 between the crystal and a superfluid phase; it also provides new RG insights into anyon interactions and finite-size effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To challenge the common assumption that an anyon gas with only statistical interactions must be a superfluid, by examining its behavior in a controlled solvable limit where bosonic statistics dominate but fractional statistics remain relevant.

Method: Analytical and renormalization-group analysis of the anyon gas in the large-N, fixed-N/k near-bosonic limit; characterization of the crystalline ground state via unit-cell size, energy, acoustic modes, angular momentum crossings, and screening of the statistical gauge field.

Result: Discovery of a stable, screened anyon crystal phase with conventional (non-superfluid) properties; identification of a quantum phase transition at k_c ≈ 2–5; derivation of crystal properties (lattice spacing, energy scaling, phonon dispersion); RG flow revealing novel fixed points and finite-size corrections.

Conclusion: The anyon gas is not generically superfluid—statistical interactions can be fully screened in a crystalline phase, leading to a quantum phase transition. This overturns the conventional lore and establishes the anyon crystal as a distinct, robust quantum phase.

Abstract: We revisit the long-standing problem of the anyon gas. We study the solvable near-bosonic limit of a large number of anyons $N$ at fixed $N/k$, where $1/k$ characterizes the statistical interaction. In this limit, the ground state is a crystal with order $N/k$ lattice sites. The statistical interactions, mediated by a long-range gauge field, are completely screened within the crystalline phase. The resulting state is a conventional crystal without superfluidity. We characterize this anyon crystal by determining its unit-cell size, ground-state energy, acoustic excitations, angular momentum crossings, and other properties.
  Our results indicate a quantum phase transition between the crystal at $k>k_c$ and a superfluid at $k<k_c$, with a critical value $k_c\simeq2\text{--}5$. These results imply that an anyon gas with only statistical interactions is not necessarily a superfluid, contrary to common lore.
  We also present a detailed analysis of the renormalization-group evolution of anyon--anyon interactions, identifying many new potential fixed points and elucidating finite-size effects.

</details>


### [62] [Primary Decompositions in Lorentz-Covariant Rings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25253)
*Giuseppe De Laurentis,David Tai*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper advances computational algebraic geometry techniques for simplifying integral coefficients in scattering amplitudes by improving primary decomposition methods—addressing Lorentz covariance loss via kinematic ansätze fitting, enhancing numerical point sampling near varieties, and refining primality testing—yielding new codimension-two decompositions for five-point massless and one-mass amplitudes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct compact, singularity-manifest representations of integral coefficients in scattering amplitudes, where partial fraction decomposition depends critically on numerator behavior on irreducible codimension-two varieties identifiable via primary decomposition—an operation that remains computationally prohibitive without tailored strategies.

Method: Extends ideal saturation-based approach; corrects Lorentz-covariance-breaking in ideal generators by fitting ansätze in special kinematic limits; improves semi-numerical algorithms for generating points near varieties and testing primality of unmixed ideals via independent set and dimension computation; applies these to compute new primary decompositions.

Result: New codimension-two primary decompositions for: (i) five-point massless amplitudes at higher orders in the dimensional regulator ε; (ii) finite remainders in five-point one-mass kinematics—revealing a previously unknown spurious singular locus.

Conclusion: The enhanced algebraic-geometric toolkit enables more efficient, covariant, and physically insightful simplifications of amplitude integrands, expanding the scope of analytic calculations in high-multiplicity and massive kinematic regimes.

Abstract: An effective strategy for constructing compact representations of integral coefficients in scattering amplitudes is to make their singularity structure manifest. This is naturally achieved by treating them as elements of fraction fields of polynomial quotient rings, where poles correspond to algebraic vanishing loci. Simplification via partial fraction decomposition relies on exploiting the structure of the numerators and is governed, at least in a first approximation, by their behaviour on irreducible codimension-two varieties. These are identifiable through primary decompositions of the associated ideals. Despite the availability of general algorithms, primary decompositions remain computationally challenging and often require tailored strategies. In this work, we extend an approach based on ideal saturation, identify an issue related to the breaking of Lorentz covariance in the choice of ideal generators, and present a solution based on fitting ansätze in special kinematic limits. We also provide substantial improvements to an algorithm for the generation of numerical points in proximity of given varieties and to one for testing primality of unmixed ideals, both of which rely on a semi-numerical procedure for computing independent sets and variety dimensions. Finally, we present new codimension-two primary decompositions for five-point massless amplitudes at higher powers in the dimensional regulator, and for finite remainders with five-point one-mass kinematics, where a new spurious singular locus appears.

</details>


### [63] [Dual symmetry breaking and magnetic charge screening](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25324)
*Saulo Carneiro*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends dual electrodynamics via Lagrangian formulation with electric and magnetic Higgs scalars coupled nonlocally to Mandelstam potentials; in a magnetic-Higgs vacuum, electric interactions remain long-range (Coulomb), magnetic ones become short-range (massive), magnetic charges are screened per Swieca theorem, electric charges remain unscreened, Dirac quantisation persists, and screened monopoles are proposed as dark sector candidates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a consistent dual electrodynamics framework where electric and magnetic charges exhibit asymmetric screening behavior—motivated by confinement scenarios, infrared dualities, and potential connections to dark matter—while preserving locality of field equations despite nonlocal scalar couplings.

Method: Lagrangian formulation incorporating both electric and magnetic Higgs scalar fields coupled to Mandelstam-type nonlocal potentials; analysis of symmetry breaking in the magnetic-Higgs vacuum; derivation of generalized Maxwell equations; spectral analysis of propagators to identify massless/massive poles; application of Swieca’s theorem for charge screening; enforcement of Dirac quantisation condition from the unbroken symmetric phase.

Result: In the magnetic-Higgs vacuum: (i) generalized Maxwell equations stay local; (ii) electric potential retains a massless pole → Coulomb interaction at large distances; (iii) magnetic sector gains mass → short-range pole-pole and charge-pole interactions; (iv) magnetic charge is Swieca-screened, electric charge is not; (v) Dirac condition constrains microscopic couplings; (vi) screened monopoles emerge as viable dark sector degrees of freedom.

Conclusion: Dual electrodynamics with magnetic Higgs condensation realizes an asymmetric confinement mechanism: electric charges remain deconfined and observable, while magnetic charges are confined/screened—providing a field-theoretic basis for emergent dark sectors composed of massive, weakly interacting monopoles.

Abstract: We extend a Lagrangian formulation of dual electrodynamics by introducing electric and magnetic Higgs scalar fields. The scalars couple to Mandelstam nonlocal potentials which enter the electric and magnetic sectors. We consider the vacuum in which only the magnetic scalar has a nonzero expectation value. The generalised Maxwell equations remain local even though the scalar couplings are nonlocal. In this phase the local electric potential keeps a massless pole while the magnetic sector acquires a mass. The electric interaction is therefore Coulombian at large distances while the pole-pole and charge-pole interactions become short ranged. The magnetic charge is then screened in the sense of the Swieca theorem while the electric charge is not. The Dirac condition is inherited from the symmetric phase as a quantisation rule for the microscopic electric and magnetic couplings. We also comment on screened monopoles as possible dark sector degrees of freedom.

</details>


### [64] [Hidden symmetries and the universality of Hawking radiation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25475)
*Ivan Pérez-Castro,Abdel Pérez-Lorenzana*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives the Hawking radiation spectrum for no-hair black holes exactly—without near-horizon metric approximations—by leveraging gauge invariance and separability of the Hamilton-Jacobi equation due to spacetime hidden symmetries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the standard limitation of the complex-path (Hamilton-Jacobi) method, which relies on approximating the metric near the horizon, and to provide an exact, geometrically transparent derivation of Hawking radiation that clarifies its dimensional reduction, spin dependence, and compatibility with back-reaction and quantum gravity.

Method: Uses gauge invariance in quantum field theory and separability of the Hamilton-Jacobi equation induced by hidden symmetries (e.g., Killing tensors) of stationary, axisymmetric black hole spacetimes; avoids near-horizon expansions or coordinate-specific approximations.

Result: Exact derivation of the thermal Hawking spectrum; explicit demonstration that the black hole behaves as an effective (1+1)-dimensional blackbody at the horizon; identification of conditions for generalization to higher-spin fields; establishment of a framework amenable to incorporating back-reaction and quantum-gravity corrections.

Conclusion: The Hawking radiation spectrum is fundamentally tied to global spacetime symmetries and gauge structure—not local horizon geometry—enabling exact derivations and natural extensions beyond scalar fields and semiclassical approximations.

Abstract: Quantum tunneling of particles across a black hole (BH) horizon provides an intuitive interpretation of Hawking radiation (HR), yielding a conceptually simple derivation of the thermal spectrum within a semiclassical framework. However, for BHs obeying the no-hair theorem, usual implementations of the complex-path (Hamilton-Jacobi) method typically rely on approximating the metric near the BH horizon. In this work, we demonstrate that the Hawking spectrum can be derived without such approximations by exploiting gauge invariance in quantum field theory and the separability of the Hamilton-Jacobi (HJ) equation arising from hidden symmetries of these spacetimes. However, for BHs obeying the no-hair theorem, usual implementations of the complex-path (Hamilton--Jacobi) method typically rely on approximating the metric near the horizon. This perspective shows explicitly why the BH behaves as an effective $(1+1)$-dimensional blackbody at the event horizon, provides the conditions needed to extend the HR to higher-spin particles, and offers a framework that can naturally incorporate back-reaction and quantum-gravity effects.

</details>


### [65] [Holographic description of RN-like black hole in bumblebee gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25530)
*Jian-Peng Zhang,Bin Chen,Jiaju Zhang,Yu Zhang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates the holographic dual CFT description of a Reissner–Nordström-like black hole in bumblebee gravity—a vector-tensor theory with spontaneous Lorentz violation—and finds that thermodynamic and dynamical (hidden conformal symmetry) methods consistently yield chiral CFT data with equal right- and left-moving central charges, attributable to the spontaneous (rather than explicit) nature of Lorentz breaking.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how spontaneous Lorentz violation in bumblebee gravity affects the holographic duality and CFT structure of RN-like black holes, particularly whether it induces chiral asymmetry in the dual 2D CFT.

Method: 1) Thermodynamic method: Combine first laws of outer and inner horizons to derive CFT parameters (entropies, temperatures, chemical potentials, central charges) via entropy product and Cardy formula; 2) Dynamical test: Analyze hidden conformal symmetry of charged scalar perturbations to independently verify CFT data.

Result: Consistent derivation of chiral CFT data: $c_R = c_L$, identical temperatures and chemical potentials for both sectors; all quantities depend smoothly on the Lorentz-violating parameter and reduce to standard RN/CFT results in the Lorentz-symmetric limit; no chirally asymmetric structure arises due to spontaneous (not explicit) symmetry breaking.

Conclusion: Spontaneous Lorentz violation preserves left-right symmetry in the dual 2D CFT, distinguishing bumblebee gravity from explicitly Lorentz-violating theories like topologically massive gravity; the holographic dictionary remains robust and continuous across the Lorentz-preserving limit.

Abstract: We study the holographic description of the RN-like black hole in bumblebee gravity, which is a vector-tensor theory with spontaneous Lorentz violation. By combining the first laws of the outer and inner horizons, the thermodynamics method yields the right- and left-moving entropies, temperatures, and chemical potentials of the dual two-dimensional conformal field theory (CFT), determines the central charges from the mass-independent entropy product, and reproduces the outer and inner horizon entropies through the Cardy formula. An independent dynamical test based on the hidden conformal symmetry of a charged scalar probe confirms these results. A central outcome is the equality of the right- and left-moving central charges, $c_R=c_L$, which we trace to the spontaneous character of Lorentz breaking. Because the action of bumblebee gravity preserves diffeomorphism and local Lorentz invariance, and the symmetry is broken only by a vector vacuum expectation value, no chirally asymmetric structure arises in the dual theory, in sharp contrast to topologically massive gravity in $(2+1)$ dimensions. All CFT data depend smoothly on the Lorentz-violating parameter and reduce to the known RN results when Lorentz violation is switched off.

</details>


### [66] [Semi-Universality of $O(N)$ model on $S^1\times S^2$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25622)
*Vinayak Mishra*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the thermal partition function of the critical large-N O(N) model on rotating S^1 × S^2 using Weyl-covariant hydrostatic expansion, identifies latitude-dependent saddle behavior induced by rotation, and matches high-temperature results with the near-light-speed (pp-wave) limit; low-temperature analysis uncovers interaction-dependent corrections from saddle response to thermal sources.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how rotation affects thermal properties of critical large-N field theories on curved spacetimes, particularly how geometric rotation modifies the thermal saddle and influences universal vs. theory-dependent contributions to the partition function.

Method: Weyl-covariant hydrostatic expansion up to four-derivative order; analysis of saddle-point geometry under rotation on S^1 × S^2; asymptotic matching in high-temperature and near-light-speed limits; direct computation on the pp-wave limiting geometry at both high and low temperatures.

Result: Partition function determined to four-derivative order in high-T and semi-universal regimes; equatorial pp-wave geometry captures semi-universal behavior; high-T result matches near-light-speed limit of sphere calculation; low-T expansion yields novel interaction-dependent corrections tied to thermal-source-induced saddle deformation.

Conclusion: Rotation induces a nontrivial latitude-dependent thermal saddle governed locally by temperature; semi-universality emerges only in specific limits (e.g., near-light-speed/pp-wave), while low-temperature physics retains theory-specific data via saddle response—highlighting interplay between geometry, thermodynamics, and large-N dynamics.

Abstract: We study the thermal partition function of the critical large-$N$ $O(N)$ model on rotating $S^1\times S^2$. Rotation makes the saddle latitude dependent, with its leading profile governed by the local temperature. Using a Weyl-covariant hydrostatic expansion, we determine the partition function through four-derivative order in high-temperature and semi-universal limits. In the near-light-speed limit, the geometry around the equator reduces to a pp-wave and captures the expected semi-universal behavior of the partition function. We compute the corresponding theory-dependent residue directly on this limiting geometry, both at high and low temperatures. The high-temperature result agrees with the near-light-speed limit of the sphere calculation, while the low-temperature expansion reveals interaction-dependent corrections arising from the response of the saddle to the thermal source.

</details>


### [67] [Negative Rank Relations for Giant Graviton Correlators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25846)
*Qi Chen,Zhongjie Huang,Xinan Zhou*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes an exact duality between correlation functions of giant and dual giant gravitons in N=4 SYM, mediated by Young diagram transposition and formal rank reflection N → −N, valid for free and weakly coupled correlators with arbitrary numbers of Schur polynomial insertions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover a precise gauge-theoretic duality between giant and dual giant gravitons—distinct D3-brane configurations in AdS5×S5 whose dual operators are Schur polynomials labeled by transposed Young diagrams.

Method: Analyzes free and weak-coupling correlation functions of Schur polynomial operators; employs Young diagram transposition symmetry and formal analytic continuation N → −N; extends to loop level via Lagrangian insertion technique; verifies consistency across multiple examples and tests non-perturbative plausibility.

Result: An exact identity relating correlators of Schur operators labeled by transposed Young diagrams under N → −N, holding for both U(N) and SU(N), at finite N, for arbitrary numbers of external Schur insertions, and to all orders in weak coupling (via integrands).

Conclusion: Transposition of Young diagrams encoding giant/dual-giant operators induces a rigorous N → −N duality in correlation functions—a structural symmetry of N=4 SYM that bridges distinct gravitational duals and persists beyond tree level.

Abstract: We study correlation functions of giant and dual giant gravitons in $\mathcal N=4$ super Yang--Mills theory. Although the two objects correspond to rather different D3 brane configurations in $AdS_5\times S^5$, their gauge theory operators are Schur polynomials associated with transposed Young diagrams. We show that this transposition is accompanied by a formal rank reflection $N\to -N$ at the level of correlation functions. For both $U(N)$ and $SU(N)$, the relation is exact for free correlators at finite $N$, and, using Lagrangian insertion, extends to separated point weak coupling integrands at every loop order. More generally, it applies to any number of Schur polynomial external operators, provided all Young diagrams are transposed. We illustrate the relation in several examples and also discuss evidence for its extension beyond perturbative integrands.

</details>


### [68] [Numerical Sasaki--Einstein metrics and harmonic forms on del Pezzo links](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25857)
*Nakwoo Kim,Sejin Kim,Hoseob Shin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper numerically constructs the Sasaki–Einstein metric on the link of the cone over dP₂ and computes two primitive harmonic basic (1,1)-forms at the irregular volume-minimizing Reeb vector, using both polynomial and neural-network approximations to the Guillemin potential; achieves high-accuracy Monge–Ampère residuals (~10⁻¹³), validates against known Y^{p,q} and dP₃ metrics, and provides data for holographic IIB applications.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct explicit Sasaki–Einstein metrics on non-regular toric Sasaki manifolds—specifically the link of the dP₂ cone—where no closed-form solution exists, enabling study of warped holographic backgrounds analogous to Klebanov–Tseytlin on the conifold.

Method: Uses toric geometry to encode the Sasaki–Einstein metric via a convex symplectic potential on a polygon; approximates the correction to the canonical Guillemin potential using (i) high-degree polynomial expansions and (ii) neural networks; validates via curvature invariants (against Y^{p,q}) and Laplacian spectra (against Doran et al.’s dP₃ result).

Result: Achieves mean-squared Monge–Ampère residual < 10⁻¹³ for the volume-minimizing irregular Reeb vector (vs. ~10⁻² for regular case); produces high-fidelity numerical metric and two harmonic (1,1)-forms; cross-validates successfully against analytic Y^{p,q} metrics and prior dP₃ numerics.

Conclusion: High-precision numerical construction of irregular Sasaki–Einstein metrics is feasible via symplectic toric methods with polynomial or neural approximations; the resulting data enables concrete studies of non-conformal holography in type IIB string theory.

Abstract: We numerically construct the Sasaki--Einstein metric on the link of the cone over the second del Pezzo surface $\mathrm{dP}_2$ and two primitive harmonic basic $(1,1)$-forms at its irregular volume-minimizing Reeb vector. Being toric, the metric in symplectic coordinates is encoded in a single convex function on a polygon. We approximate the correction to the canonical Guillemin potential in two ways: polynomial expansion and neural networks. The polynomial fit achieves a held-out mean-squared Monge--Ampère residual below $10^{-13}$, in contrast to the $10^{-2}$ plateau for the non-volume-minimizing regular Reeb vector. We validate our method against closed-form metrics of $Y^{p,q}$ using curvature invariants, and also against the numerical result of Doran et al. (2007) for the Kähler--Einstein metric of $\mathrm{dP}_3$ using the Laplacian spectrum of low torus-invariant modes. Our data for the metric and harmonic forms can be used to study warped non-conformal holographic IIB backgrounds, the analogues of the Klebanov--Tseytlin solution on the conifold.

</details>


### [69] [Quantum Love Numbers are Non-Zero](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25875)
*Asaad Elkhidir,Godwin Martin,Julio Parra-Martinez,M. V. S. Saketh*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Quantum corrections generate non-zero static Love numbers for Schwarzschild black holes, suppressed by (ℓ_pl/r_h)², indicating classical vanishing arises from an accidental symmetry broken by quantum loops.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether the classical vanishing of static Love numbers for Schwarzschild black holes persists in quantum gravity, and to identify the physical origin of this vanishing.

Method: Worldline effective field theory applied to compute leading quantum corrections to the Compton amplitude for massless scalars and photons scattering off a Schwarzschild black hole at first post-Minkowskian order; renormalization analysis of logarithmic divergences.

Result: Logarithmic UV divergences appear in the Compton amplitude, requiring renormalization of static Love numbers; RG running generates finite, Planck-suppressed Love numbers ∝ ℓ_pl²/r_h².

Conclusion: Classical vanishing of static Love numbers is not fundamental but stems from an accidental symmetry of static GR, broken by quantum loops; quantum effects induce quadrupolar static Love numbers ∼ M r_h² ℓ_pl².

Abstract: The static Love numbers of four-dimensional Schwarzschild black holes vanish classically. We show that this is not true in the quantum theory. Working in the worldline effective field theory, we compute the leading quantum correction to the Compton amplitude for massless scalars and photons scattering off a Schwarzschild black hole at first post-Minkowskian order, and find logarithmic divergences associated with the renormalization of the static Love numbers. We conclude that renormalization-group running necessarily generates Love numbers suppressed by the square of the Planck length in units of the horizon radius, $\ell_{\rm pl}^{2}/r_{\rm h}^{2}$. This is consistent with the classical vanishing being a consequence of an accidental symmetry of static general relativity, broken by quantum loops. Extrapolated to gravitational perturbations, our results suggest that the quadrupolar static Love numbers of Schwarzschild are of size $λ\sim M r_{\rm h}^{2} \ell_{\rm pl}^{2}$.

</details>


### [70] [BRST quantization of the induced gauge theory for the Higgs-branch CFT${}_2$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26571)
*Bogdan Stefański*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a BRST-quantized framework for Witten's induced gauge theory arising from the D1-D5 Higgs-branch 2D CFT, deriving induced propagators and verifying consistency conditions (Slavnov-Taylor identity, gauge-parameter independence) essential for perturbative computation of observables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a consistent, perturbatively well-defined quantization scheme for Witten's induced gauge theory—where gauge fields emerge dynamically from integrating out hypermultiplets in the D1-D5 Higgs-branch CFT$_2$—enabling reliable computation of physical observables.

Method: BRST quantization is applied to the induced gauge theory; induced propagators are derived explicitly; consistency is verified by checking the Slavnov-Taylor identity for the induced $A\phi^2$ vertex, confirming ghost contributions enter only at subleading order, and demonstrating gauge-parameter independence of gauge-invariant correlators under a specified regularization prescription.

Result: A fully consistent perturbative framework is established: induced propagators are obtained; the $A\phi^2$ vertex satisfies the Slavnov-Taylor identity; ghosts contribute only at subleading order; and gauge-invariant correlators are proven independent of the gauge-fixing parameter.

Conclusion: The BRST-quantized formulation, combined with the chosen regularization, provides a robust and predictive perturbative framework for computing observables in Witten’s induced gauge theory from the D1-D5 Higgs-branch CFT$_2$.

Abstract: We study Witten's induced gauge theory associated with the D1-D5 Higgs-branch CFT${}_2$, in which the non-dynamical vector multiplet becomes reanimated by integrating out the fundamental hypermultiplets. We propose a BRST quantisation of this theory and derive its induced propagators. We show that the ghost sector first contributes at subleading order, that the induced $Aφ^2$ vertex satisfies the Slavnov-Taylor identity and that gauge-invariant correlators are independent of the gauge parameter. Together with our chosen regularization prescription, this gives a perturbative framework for computing observables in the induced theory.

</details>


### [71] [Density-driven reversal of flavour-entanglement anisotropy in the D3-D7 holographic conductor](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26641)
*Fuminori Okabayashi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates how a DC electric field affects spatial entanglement entropy in a strongly coupled 3+1D conductor (holographically modeled by the D3-D7 system) at finite temperature and charge density; it identifies a density-driven sign reversal in the field-aligned vs. transverse entanglement entropy difference, linked to radial competition in DBI stress anisotropy—interpreted as a smooth crossover, not a phase transition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how external electric fields influence quantum entanglement structure in strongly coupled, finite-density conductors, particularly whether such effects encode universal transport or response signatures beyond conventional hydrodynamics.

Method: Holographic computation using the D3-D7 probe-brane system in AdS5×S5 background; calculation of equal-time spatial entanglement entropy for slab-shaped subregions aligned parallel vs. transverse to a constant DC electric field; analysis of entropy difference, mutual information, and stress-energy tensor contributions from Dirac-Born-Infeld action.

Result: A sign reversal in the field-direction-dependent entanglement entropy correction occurs as charge density crosses a critical value; this crossover density increases with electric field strength and decreases with strip width; mutual information confirms the reversal and remains UV-finite; the effect originates from radial sign competition in the DBI-induced stress anisotropy.

Conclusion: The observed sign reversal is a smooth, non-thermodynamic response crossover encoded in spatial entanglement, revealing how holographic quantum information measures diagnose directional electromagnetic response in strongly correlated matter.

Abstract: We study the leading probe-flavour contribution to equal-time spatial entanglement entropy in a homogeneous 3+1-dimensional strongly coupled conductor at finite temperature and charge density, realised holographically by the D3-D7 system and driven by a constant DC electric field. We compare slab-shaped subregions that are extended in two spatial directions and have finite width parallel or transverse to the field. The difference between the corresponding entropy corrections isolates a homogeneous, spatially traceless response and exhibits a density-driven sign reversal. Over the investigated parameter range, the crossover density increases with the electric-field magnitude and decreases with the strip width. Mutual information shows the same qualitative reversal and provides an ultraviolet-finite check. We trace the effect to a radial sign competition in the full Dirac-Born-Infeld stress anisotropy. The reversal is a smooth response crossover rather than a thermodynamic phase transition.

</details>


### [72] [Quasinormal modes and quantum black hole interiors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26724)
*Chiara Coviello,Ansh Gupta,Robie A. Hennigar,Kai Shi,Andrew Svesko*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes asymptotic quasinormal modes (QNMs) of (2+1)-dimensional quantum black holes using complex-analytic methods, linking QNM spectral structure—specifically the offset and gap in ω = offset + n·gap—to the geometry and scaling of interior singularities; it reconstructs singularity exponents from the offset, extracts metric scaling near the singularity from subleading QNM corrections, and identifies oscillatory QNMs in the crossover regime of charged black holes as a possible inner-horizon signature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the interior geometry—and especially the singularity structure—of quantum-corrected black holes via asymptotic quasinormal modes, which are highly sensitive to deep interior features in the large-overtone limit.

Method: Analytic continuation of the radial coordinate into the complex plane; application of complex WKB/stokes line analysis to compute leading-order and subleading asymptotic QNM frequencies; reconstruction of singularity scaling exponents from the offset and subleading corrections under assumptions on global Stokes topology.

Result: 1) Asymptotic QNMs follow ω = offset + n·gap universally; 2) The offset encodes the singularity’s scaling exponent (e.g., Kasner-like behavior); 3) Subleading corrections yield precise metric function scaling near the singularity; 4) Charged quantum black holes show a crossover between neutral- and charge-dominated regimes, with oscillatory QNMs emerging in the transition—potentially signaling the inner horizon.

Conclusion: Asymptotic QNMs serve as a non-perturbative spectroscopic probe of quantum black hole interiors: their spectral structure directly encodes singularity type, Kasner transitions, and horizon structure, demonstrating that black hole spectroscopy can reveal deep quantum-gravitational geometry beyond classical expectations.

Abstract: Quasinormal modes (QNMs) of black hole perturbations in the large overtone limit, namely, asymptotic QNMs, are highly sensitive to a black hole's interior geometry. We probe the singularity structure of a class of exact (2+1)-dimensional quantum black hole solutions to semi-classical gravity by computing their asymptotic QNM spectra. We do this using methods of complex analysis in which the radial coordinate is analytically continued to the complex plane. The leading order QNM frequencies all fit the generic form $ω=(\text{offset})+n(\text{gap})$, for overtone number $n$, which we also confirm numerically. Under a general assumption about the global Stokes topology of the complexified radial coordinate, we show how to reconstruct the scaling exponent of more general (spacelike or timelike) black hole singularities from the offset. We then compute subleading corrections to the asymptotic QNMs, from which we provide a robust method for extracting the scaling of the metric function near the singularity. Our findings exemplify the transition between "Kasner eons", successive regimes encountered on approach to a spacelike singularity, as non-perturbative quantum effects become dominant. For charged quantum black holes, the asymptotic QNMs exhibit a crossover between two regimes in which the neutral and charge contributions to the metric function dominate, respectively. Within the crossover region, the QNMs develop an oscillatory behavior that may be a signature of the inner horizon.

</details>
