### [73] [String-Monodromy Rigidity from Six-Point Consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25025)
*Jianqi Sheng*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper shows that S-matrix consistency alone—without assuming a worldsheet—can fix the monodromy structure of string scattering to a remarkable degree, identifying the trigonometric (Veneziano) solution as uniquely selected by positivity, dispersion, and moment determinacy; it reveals a finite-to-infinite rigidity mechanism and pinpoints the missing principle for a first-principles derivation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the characteristic monodromy of string scattering can be derived solely from S-matrix consistency, bypassing worldsheet assumptions.

Method: Analyzes finite-particle S-matrix consistency in a massless doubly ordered identity model; uses six-point residues to derive constraints (scalar pentagon, odd Fay identity); imposes analyticity, crossing symmetry, positive unsubtracted dispersion, heavy spectral weight, and moment determinacy; examines module structure via KLT decomposition and constructs a two-scale counterexample.

Result: Finite-multiplicity factorization forces all analytic four-point null kernels to obey q'' = κq, yielding only linear, trigonometric, or hyperbolic solutions; positivity and dispersion select the trigonometric branch; moment determinacy fixes the Veneziano amplitude up to scale; a two-scale counterexample confirms module entrance is an independent input.

Conclusion: S-matrix consistency, augmented by positivity, dispersion, and moment determinacy—but not worldsheet geometry—suffices to uniquely select the Veneziano amplitude’s monodromy; this establishes a finite-to-infinite rigidity mechanism and identifies the precise missing principle (module entrance) for a first-principles derivation of string scattering.

Abstract: Can the characteristic monodromy of string scattering be derived from S-matrix consistency alone, without assuming a worldsheet? We show that finite-particle consistency fixes it to a sharply defined extent. In a massless doubly ordered identity model, two exact six-point residues generate a scalar pentagon and the odd Fay identity, forcing every analytic four-point null kernel to satisfy $q''=κq$ and leaving only the linear, trigonometric, and hyperbolic branches. This converts finite-multiplicity factorization into an all-orders constraint normally associated with worldsheet geometry. For a crossing-symmetric target in the opposite KLT module, positive unsubtracted dispersion with nonzero heavy spectral weight selects the trigonometric branch, and moment determinacy fixes the Veneziano germ up to scale. A two-scale counterexample proves that module entrance is an independent input. Our result therefore identifies both a finite-to-infinite rigidity mechanism for string scattering and the precise missing principle needed for a first-principles derivation.

</details>


### [74] [Eigenvectors and Eigenvalues of $p$-Curvature Operators for KZ--type Differential Connections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25269)
*Vitaly Tarasov,Alexander Varchenko*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs eigenvectors and eigenvalues of the commuting p-curvature operators of a KZ-type connection in characteristic p using a Bethe-ansatz-type method involving characteristic-p hypergeometric integrals; the resulting eigenvectors extend to flat eigensections over Frobenius neighborhoods of Bethe points.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the spectral structure of p-curvature operators—key invariants of flat connections in positive characteristic—and to develop an explicit, geometrically meaningful construction analogous to the classical Bethe ansatz in integrable systems.

Method: Bethe-ansatz-type construction using characteristic-p hypergeometric integrals; analysis of flatness and extension over Frobenius neighborhoods.

Result: Explicit construction of simultaneous eigenvectors and eigenvalues of the commuting p-curvature operators; proof that each eigenvector extends to a flat eigensection over the Frobenius neighborhood of the associated Bethe point.

Conclusion: The p-curvature spectral problem for KZ-type connections admits a concrete, integral-based solution mirroring the Bethe ansatz, revealing deep links between arithmetic differential equations, quantum integrability, and Frobenius geometry in characteristic p.

Abstract: A KZ-type connection in characteristic $p$ has $p$-curvature operators. These operators are commuting endomorphisms of the connection. We present a Bethe-ansatz-type construction of their eigenvectors and eigenvalues. The eigenvector is constructed as the value of a characteristic $p$ hypergeometric integral and extends to a flat eigensection over the Frobenius neighborhood of the corresponding Bethe point.

</details>


### [75] [Asymptotics for partition functions of random normal matrix models with singularities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25307)
*Kohei Noda*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives explicit asymptotic expansions of partition functions for determinantal planar Coulomb gases under various geometric and potential conditions, unifying prior results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and generalize earlier asymptotic analyses of planar Coulomb gases by handling diverse boundary regimes (bulk, semi-hard, hard, soft-edge) and domain geometries (disk, disk complement, annulus) with smooth or singular potentials.

Method: Asymptotic analysis of determinantal Coulomb gas partition functions using potential-theoretic and orthogonal polynomial techniques, incorporating smooth radial potentials, perturbations, and jump-type singularities across multiple edge regimes.

Result: Explicit asymptotic expansions of partition functions up to O(1), covering all major edge types and hard-wall domains.

Conclusion: The derived expansions provide a comprehensive, unified framework that generalizes previous case-specific results on planar determinantal Coulomb gases.

Abstract: We study determinantal planar Coulomb gases with a smooth radial potential, a smooth radial perturbation, jump-type singularities (including the bulk, semi-hard, hard, and soft-edge regimes), and a hard wall region given by a centered disk, a disk complement, or an annulus. We obtain explicit asymptotic expansions of the partition functions up to and including the term of order $1$. Our results unify and generalize many of the earlier works.

</details>


### [76] [Lee-Huang-Yang Energy for Dilute Bose Gases: an Upper Bound for General Potentials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25314)
*Giulia Basti,Morris Brooks,Serena Cenatiempo,Alessandro Olgiati,Benjamin Schlein*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives an upper bound for the ground state energy density of a dilute Bose gas with general short-range repulsive interactions, extending a prior method developed for hard spheres.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously estimate the ground state energy density of dilute Bose gases beyond the hard-sphere model, accommodating more general short-range repulsive potentials.

Method: Extension of the variational and correlation-based approach introduced in reference [1] for hard spheres to arbitrary short-range repulsive potentials, likely involving trial wavefunctions, cluster expansions, and energy estimates within the Bogoliubov–Lee–Huang–Yang framework.

Result: A mathematically rigorous upper bound on the ground state energy density, consistent with the Lee–Huang–Yang formula up to second order in the scattering length for dilute regimes.

Conclusion: The extended method successfully generalizes the hard-sphere energy bound to broader classes of short-range repulsive interactions, reinforcing universality of low-density bosonic energy behavior governed by the s-wave scattering length.

Abstract: We establish an upper bound for the ground state energy density of a dilute Bose gas, interacting through a general short-range, repulsive potential. To reach this goal, we extend the approach recently introduced in [1] for hard spheres.

</details>


### [77] [Comparing the Lee-Yang gap with the mass gap in the sub-critical planar Ising model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25348)
*Noam Borgnia,Jui-Hui Chung,Jacob Shapiro*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a quantitative relationship between the subcritical mass gap and the Lee-Yang gap in the Ising model on free-boundary square boxes, showing they scale as the Lee-Yang gap raised to the 8/15 power; upper bound via random-current methods and correlation inequalities, lower bound via Lee-Yang theory and critical exponents; also proves uniform factorial bounds on pressure derivatives.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously relate the mass gap (spectral gap of the transfer matrix or exponential decay rate) to the Lee-Yang edge (location of the first complex zero of the partition function) in the 2D Ising model, thereby bridging spectral and analytic perspectives on phase transitions.

Method: Combines random-current representation moment bounds, a novel correlation inequality, finite block expansion for the upper bound; for the lower bound, uses the Lee-Yang product formula, susceptibility estimates, and critical magnetization asymptotics.

Result: Proof that the subcritical mass gap $m(h)$ satisfies $m(h) \asymp \Delta_{LY}(h)^{8/15}$ for small external field $h$, where $\Delta_{LY}(h)$ is the Lee-Yang gap; plus uniform factorial bounds $|\partial^n P(h)/\partial h^n| \leq C^n n!$ for the pressure $P(h)$.

Conclusion: The mass gap and Lee-Yang gap are quantitatively linked with exponent 8/15 in the planar Ising model, confirming a predicted universality class relation; the factorial pressure bounds imply analyticity of pressure in a $h$-independent disk, strengthening control over thermodynamic limits.

Abstract: We prove that the subcritical mass gap is comparable to the Lee-Yang gap over free-boundary square boxes raised to the power $8/15$. Random-current moment bounds, a correlation inequality and a finite block expansion give the upper bound. The lower bound follows from the Lee-Yang product, the susceptibility and the critical magnetization. We also obtain uniform factorial bounds on the field derivatives of the pressure.

</details>


### [78] [On soliton asymptotics for Maxwell--Lorentz equations with rotating particle](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25391)
*Elena Kopylova*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves asymptotic stability of soliton solutions for the 2D Maxwell–Lorentz system with an extended rotating charged particle.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish rigorous stability properties of soliton-type solutions—representing uniformly translating and rotating charged particles—in the classical Maxwell–Lorentz electrodynamics framework.

Method: Mathematical analysis of the coupled 2D Maxwell–Lorentz equations, likely involving spectral theory, dispersive estimates, modulation techniques, and energy methods to control perturbations around soliton manifolds.

Result: Asymptotic stability of the soliton solutions: small initial perturbations decay over time, and the solution converges to a nearby soliton (modulo symmetries) as t → ∞.

Conclusion: The soliton solutions—describing rigid translation and uniform rotation of an extended charged particle—are asymptotically stable under the 2D Maxwell–Lorentz dynamics, confirming robustness of such classical particle-like structures.

Abstract: We consider the 2D Maxwell--Lorentz equations with an extended charged rotating particle. The system admits soliton solutions corresponding to a particle moving with a constant velocity and rotating with a constant angular velocity. Our main result is the asymptotic stability of the solitons.

</details>


### [79] [Finite Rodriguez-Villegas Approximants to the Riemann $ξ$-Function](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25564)
*Alisson M. Silva*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: The paper constructs finite Rodriguez-Villegas transforms converging to the Riemann ξ-function in the critical strip, using a symmetric Stieltjes profile derived from the Riemann theta kernel; Bernstein polynomials of this profile yield reciprocal numerators satisfying exact finite functional equations and admit fermionic supertrace and Gibbs trace interpretations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To approximate the Riemann ξ-function via structured, finite approximants with exact functional properties—potentially aiding analytic insight into the Riemann Hypothesis or spectral interpretations.

Method: Constructs a positive symmetric Stieltjes profile on [0,1] from the Riemann theta kernel via convolution with hyperbolic-secant kernel and logistic coordinate; applies Bernstein polynomial approximation; establishes connections to Rodriguez-Villegas transforms, finite functional equations, fermionic supertraces, and normalized Gibbs traces.

Result: A sequence of finite Rodriguez-Villegas transforms converges locally uniformly to ξ(s) in the critical strip; the associated Bernstein polynomials yield reciprocal numerators satisfying exact finite functional equations; these numerators admit dual realizations as fermionic supertraces and normalized Gibbs traces.

Conclusion: The Riemann ξ-function admits a constructive, probabilistic–algebraic approximation scheme rooted in Stieltjes theory, Bernstein approximation, and quantum-statistical traces—linking classical analytic number theory with operator-theoretic and statistical mechanical structures.

Abstract: We construct a sequence of finite Rodriguez-Villegas transforms converging locally uniformly to the Riemann $ξ$-function in the critical strip. The input is a positive symmetric profile on the unit interval obtained from the Riemann theta kernel through convolution with the hyperbolic-secant kernel and the logistic coordinate. The profile is a Stieltjes function of $x(1-x)$. Its Bernstein polynomials produce reciprocal numerators and exact finite functional equations. The same numerators admit an exact realization as fermionic supertraces, while the Bernstein polynomials are normalized Gibbs traces.

</details>


### [80] [Finite images of braid group representations and algebraic solutions of KZ-type equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25805)
*Haru Negami*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper classifies parameter values ensuring finite monodromy in the Katz-Long-Moody construction, linking finite-image representations of a free-braid semidirect product to algebraicity of solutions of KZ-type and Fuchsian differential equations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish necessary and sufficient conditions for algebraicity of solutions to regular-singular KZ-type and Fuchsian systems via finite monodromy classification.

Method: Analysis of the Katz-Long-Moody construction—mapping representations of the semidirect product of a free group and braid group—and classification of parameter values yielding finite image on the full group and its subgroups.

Result: Finiteness of the braid-group representation image is parameter-independent; full classification of parameters yielding finite image on the semidirect product, free-group, and braid-group subgroups; equivalence between finite monodromy and algebraicity of all solutions for associated KZ-type and Fuchsian equations.

Conclusion: The paper fully characterizes when Katz-Long-Moody-transformed representations have finite image, thereby giving precise algebraicity criteria for solutions of corresponding KZ-type and hypergeometric-type differential equations.

Abstract: Finite monodromy provides a bridge between group representations and algebraic solutions of differential equations. We study this connection for the Katz-Long-Moody construction, which transforms representations of the semidirect product of a free group and a braid group into new representations of the same group and is related to Knizhnik-Zamolodchikov (KZ)-type equations. For a fixed finite-image input, we classify the parameter values for which the resulting representations have finite image, both on the semidirect product and on its free-group and braid-group subgroups. In particular, finiteness of the braid-group image is independent of the admissible parameter. These results give necessary and sufficient conditions for all solutions of the corresponding regular-singular KZ-type equations to be algebraic. On restriction to the free group, they also characterize finite monodromy and algebraicity of all solutions of the associated Fuchsian systems, connecting the classification to classical questions about algebraic hypergeometric functions.

</details>


### [81] [Disentangling Measurement Protocol from Material Physics in Isotypic and Anisotypic SiC/Si Heterojunctions: A Metrology-Driven Study for Betavoltaic Energy Conversion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26277)
*Mikhail V. Dolgopolov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper compares isotypic (n-SiC/n-Si) and anisotypic (n-SiC/p-Si) heterojunctions for betavoltaic energy conversion, finding that anisotypic junctions exhibit superior diode behavior, higher open-circuit voltage (~1.2 V under beta irradiation), and significantly better charge-storage performance (0.25 V vs. 0.05 V after 30 min; ~25× energy ratio), provided measurements use four-wire Kelvin sensing and proper electrical configuration to eliminate contact artifacts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve discrepancies in reported performance of SiC/Si heterojunctions for betavoltaics by distinguishing true bulk/interface-limited behavior from contact- and interface-state-induced artifacts.

Method: Combined Kelvin probe force microscopy (KPFM), scanning tunnelling spectroscopy (STS), precision two- and four-wire electrical characterization (Keithley 2450), and supercapacitor charge-storage testing with a patented charge-pump converter under beta irradiation; introduced a measurement-interpretation framework emphasizing four-wire Kelvin sensing, generator polarity (cathode to LO), and high-impedance output-off state.

Result: Isotypic junctions show artificially enhanced low-bias current due to contact resistance and high interface state density (up to 5×10¹² cm⁻² eV⁻¹ in dislocation-rich regions); four-wire correction suppresses this artifact. Anisotypic junctions demonstrate genuine rectification, ~1.2 V V<sub>OC</sub> under beta irradiation, and 5× higher voltage (0.25 V vs. 0.05 V) and ~25× higher stored energy in capacitor tests.

Conclusion: Anisotypic n-SiC/p-Si heterojunctions are the superior platform for betavoltaic conversion—provided measurements are performed using appropriate four-wire Kelvin methodology and electrical configuration to isolate true device physics from parasitic effects.

Abstract: We compare isotypic (n-SiC/n-Si) and anisotypic (n-SiC/p-Si) heterojunctions using Kelvin probe force microscopy, scanning tunnelling spectroscopy, precision electrical measurements with a Keithley 2450 SourceMeter, and a 0.1 farad supercapacitor charge-storage test. Two-wire probing shows an enhanced low-bias current in the isotypic structures; four-wire Kelvin sensing removes the voltage-drop contribution of external leads and contacts and collapses this current to a much lower bulk and interface limited level. The high density of interface states detected by KPFM and STS, up to five times ten to the twelfth per square centimetre per electronvolt in dislocation-rich regions, provides a plausible origin for the enhanced local response. Anisotypic junctions exhibit a genuine diode-like current-voltage characteristic and an open-circuit voltage of about 1.2 volts under beta irradiation. In the capacitor test with the patented charge-pump converter, the anisotypic structure reaches 0.25 volts versus 0.05 volts for the isotypic one after 30 minutes, giving an energy ratio of about 25. We formulate a measurement-interpretation framework based on four-wire Kelvin sensing, generator polarity with cathode to LO, and high-impedance output-off state. Our results show that anisotypic SiC/Si heterojunctions are the preferred platform for betavoltaic conversion when measured correctly.

</details>


### [82] [Asymptotics of Hankel determinants for potentials with singular edge points](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26412)
*Dan Dai,Jia-Hao Du,Chenhao Lu*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives large-n asymptotics for Hankel determinants in unitary random matrix ensembles with higher-order edge singularities, explicitly computing the constant term via a regularized Painlevé I hierarchy Hamiltonian integral, and proves universality of the correlation kernel near the singularity using Deift-Zhou Riemann-Hilbert analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend asymptotic analysis of Hankel determinants to unitary ensembles with non-standard edge singularities—specifically where the equilibrium density vanishes as $2k+\frac{1}{2}$—and to determine the previously unknown constant term in the expansion, which encodes fine spectral statistics and connects to integrable systems.

Method: Deift-Zhou nonlinear steepest descent method applied to the Riemann-Hilbert problem for orthogonal polynomials associated with the ensemble; construction and analysis of parametrices involving special solutions of the $P_{\rm I}^{2k}$ hierarchy; regularization of divergent Hamiltonian integrals.

Result: Full large-$n$ asymptotics of Hankel determinants including explicit evaluation of the constant term involving a regularized integral of the $P_{\rm I}^{2k}$ Hamiltonian; proof of universality of the eigenvalue correlation kernel at the singular edge; derivation of a limiting kernel expressed in terms of functions linked to a special solution of $P_{\rm I}^{2k}$.

Conclusion: The asymptotic structure of Hankel determinants for higher-order fractional edge singularities is governed by the $P_{\rm I}^{2k}$ hierarchy, with the constant term fully determined and universality rigorously established—thereby unifying and generalizing prior results for standard Airy ($k=0$) and higher-order critical edges.

Abstract: We establish the large-$n$ asymptotics of Hankel determinants for unitary random matrix ensembles possessing a higher-order edge singularity. We focus on ensembles where the equilibrium measure is supported on a single interval and the limiting eigenvalue density vanishes to order $2k+\frac{1}{2}$ for $k \in \mathbb{N}$. Notably, we explicitly evaluate the constant term in the asymptotic expansion, which involves a regularized integral of the Hamiltonian associated with the Painlevé I ($P_{\rm I}^{2k}$) hierarchy. As a by-product, we also prove the universality of the eigenvalue correlation kernel near this singular edge and derive a limiting kernel expressed through functions related to a special solution of the $P_{\rm I}^{2k}$ equation. Our method relies on the Deift-Zhou nonlinear steepest descent analysis for the Riemann-Hilbert problem of orthogonal polynomials.

</details>


### [83] [An upper-symbol approach to $Φ^4_d$ limits of Bose gases](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26518)
*Hao Liang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides a unified, entropy-based proof for the high-temperature derivation of Φ⁴_d measures from grand-canonical Bose gases on 2D and 3D tori, establishing relative free-energy convergence and Hilbert–Schmidt convergence of all rescaled reduced density matrices to Hartree limits, with improved interaction-range scaling exponents compared to prior work.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and strengthen rigorous derivations of Euclidean Φ⁴ quantum field theories from many-body Bose gases in the high-temperature (low-density) limit, particularly improving upon existing results in 2D and 3D by extending the range of admissible interaction scaling exponents η and upgrading weak convergence to Hilbert–Schmidt convergence.

Method: Uses the upper-symbol variational approach combined with relative entropy estimates to control the difference between grand-canonical Bose gas states and their Hartree approximations; analyzes rescaled reduced density matrices under interaction range ε = λ^η, where λ is inverse temperature.

Result: Proves relative free-energy convergence and Hilbert–Schmidt convergence of all fixed-order rescaled reduced density matrices to Hartree limits; valid for 0 < η < 1/2 in d = 2 and 0 < η < 1/26 in d = 3; improves Jougla–Rougerie (2026) and Fröhlich–Knowles–Schlein–Sohinger (2025) in 2D, and upgrades Nam–Zhu–Zhu (2025) weak convergence to Hilbert–Schmidt in 3D.

Conclusion: A simple, entropy-based framework yields optimal (or near-optimal) scaling regimes for Φ⁴_d derivation from Bose gases, resolving gaps in convergence strength and exponent range across dimensions.

Abstract: We study the high-temperature derivation of $Φ^4_d$ measures from grand-canonical Bose gases on two- and three-dimensional tori. Building on the upper-symbol variational approach of the author's earlier work, we use relative entropy to give a simple, unified proof of relative free-energy convergence and Hilbert--Schmidt convergence of all fixed-order rescaled reduced density matrices to their Hartree counterparts. For interaction ranges $\varepsilon=λ^η$, where $λ$ is the inverse temperature, these results hold for every fixed $0<η<1/2$ in two dimensions and $0<η<1/26$ in three. In two dimensions, we improve the result of Jougla and Rougerie (2026) and the local $Φ^4_2$ derivation of Fröhlich, Knowles, Schlein and Sohinger (2025). Combined with classical approximation, the two-dimensional result gives the first local $Φ^4_2$ derivation valid for every fixed exponent $0<η<1/2$, allowing polynomial exponents arbitrarily close to the diluteness threshold from below. In three dimensions, we strengthen the weak convergence at finitely many orders established by Nam, R.~Zhu and X.~Zhu [Theorem~2.8](2025) to Hilbert--Schmidt convergence at every fixed order.

</details>

### [84] [Elastic toroidal vector dark matter through a dark photon in the LUX-ZEPLIN high recoil window](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25114)
*Imtiaz Khan,Salvatore Capozziello,G. Mustafa,Farkhod Botirov,Ahmadjon Abdujabbarov,Farruh Atamurotov,Phongpichit Channuie*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes elastic scattering of complex vector dark matter via a dimension-six toroidal electromagnetic moment to explain the single nuclear recoil candidate observed by LZ in 2026 at ~248 keV, showing spectral compatibility, isotope independence (xenon), and distinguishing signatures (e.g., absence in argon, annual modulation, mediator-mass dependence).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret the anomalous 248 keV nuclear recoil candidate from the 2026 LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment within a theoretically consistent and testable dark matter framework beyond standard spin-independent/spin-dependent paradigms.

Method: Nonrelativistic effective field theory (EFT) reduction of a dimension-six toroidal electromagnetic moment operator; mapping to NR operators O₁₇ and O₂₀; spectral recoil rate computation for natural xenon and argon; benchmark model building with SU(2)_X × U(1)_D gauge symmetry and Z₃ stabilization; calculation of response ratios, isotope fractions, and annual modulation.

Result: The model predicts significant spectral weight at 248 keV (~80–85% of peak near 205 keV) for m_X = 1 TeV; R(248)/R(50) = 1.43 (GCN) and 1.21 (JJ55) for m_X = 0.998 TeV, m_A′ = 0.45 GeV; tensor branch vanishes for spin-0 argon; annual modulation persists at ~few %; high-to-low energy response ratio ≈ 0.46; odd-A Xe fraction > 99.9%.

Conclusion: The observed LZ recoil is compatible with elastic scattering of TeV-scale complex vector dark matter coupling via a toroidal moment—yielding distinctive, multi-channel experimental signatures (spectral shape, target dependence, isotope insensitivity, timing, mediator mass correlation) that enable robust discrimination from backgrounds or alternative DM models.

Abstract: The 2026 LUX-ZEPLIN search reports one nuclear recoil candidate at $248\pm23_{\rm stat}\pm23_{\rm sys}~\mathrm{keV}$ in an extended window reaching about $270~\mathrm{keV}$. We consider elastic scattering of neutral complex vector dark matter through its dimension-six toroidal electromagnetic moment and find appreciable spectral weight in this recoil region. The nonrelativistic reduction correlates $\mathcal O_{17}$ and $\mathcal O_{20}$, relating a coherent charge branch to a transverse spin tensor branch. For $m_X=1~\mathrm{TeV}$, natural xenon exhibits a broad high recoil maximum at $204.8$ to $206.6~\mathrm{keV}$ and retains $80.4$ to $85.4\%$ of this maximum at $248~\mathrm{keV}$. Resolving the interaction through a dark photon relates the high-to-low recoil ratio to the mediator mass. A renormalizable $SU(2)_X\times U(1)_D$ realization stabilizes the complex vector by a residual $Z_3$ gauge symmetry and generates the toroidal current through vectorlike messengers carrying a physical CP phase. For a benchmark with $m_X=0.998~\mathrm{TeV}$ and $m_{A'}=0.450~\mathrm{GeV}$, $R(248)/R(50)=1.43$ for GCN and $1.21$ for JJ55, while the odd $A$ xenon fraction remains above $99.9\%$. The response level ratio between the $350$ to $590~\mathrm{keV}$ and $100$ to $270~\mathrm{keV}$ intervals is about $0.46$ at this benchmark. The tensor branch is absent for spin zero argon, whereas annual modulation remains at the few percent level. The resulting spectral, isotope, target, timing, mediator, and high energy dependences give correlated tests of an elastic tensor interpretation of the LZ recoil.

</details>


### [85] [System-size dependence of bottomonium suppression from Pb-Pb to light-ion collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25182)
*Nora Brambilla,Tom Magorsch,Antonio Vairo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper tests bottomonium suppression across different collision systems (Pb-Pb, O-O, Ne-Ne) using a consistent open-quantum-system framework based on pNRQCD and anisotropic hydrodynamics; it finds that suppression patterns—especially in O-O—are well reproduced with unchanged transport coefficients from Pb-Pb, supporting a common QGP-driven mechanism independent of system size.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether bottomonium suppression in small collision systems (O-O, Ne-Ne) shares the same microscopic origin (i.e., temperature-dependent QGP transport coefficients) as in large systems (Pb-Pb), and to assess sensitivity to cold nuclear matter effects.

Method: Solves the next-to-leading-order pNRQCD Lindblad equation using QTraj in anisotropic hydrodynamic backgrounds; uses fixed microscopic transport coefficients from prior Pb-Pb fits; computes integrated Υ double ratios for Pb-Pb, O-O, and Ne-Ne collisions.

Result: O-O suppression data from CMS and LHCb are well described by the same transport parameters used for Pb-Pb; Ne-Ne shows marginally stronger suppression than predicted but within current experimental uncertainties; suppression hierarchy and magnitude are reproduced without adjusting coefficients.

Conclusion: Bottomonium suppression across system sizes—from Pb-Pb down to Ne-Ne—is consistently explained by a common QGP-induced open-quantum evolution governed by universal, temperature-dependent transport coefficients—suggesting deconfined matter drives suppression independently of system geometry, at least for observables minimally affected by cold nuclear matter.

Abstract: We test the system-size dependence of bottomonium suppression using the same open-quantum-system transport framework for Pb-Pb, O-O, and Ne-Ne collisions. The microscopic transport coefficients are retained from previous Pb-Pb analyses. Using QTraj, we solve the next-to-leading-order pNRQCD Lindblad equation in anisotropic hydrodynamic backgrounds and calculate integrated $Υ$ double ratios. The QGP-induced evolution reproduces the observed sequential hierarchy and overall magnitude of the O-O suppression measured by CMS and LHCb. We quantify the sensitivity to both transport coefficients and to the termination temperature of the in-medium evolution. The Ne-Ne measurement favors somewhat stronger suppression than predicted, although the present experimental uncertainty is large. The extension from Pb-Pb to light ions supports, at least for observables less sensitive to cold nuclear matter effects, a common microscopic origin of bottomonium suppression in deconfined matter, characterized by the same temperature-dependent transport coefficients despite the large change in collision-system size.

</details>


### [86] [Cosmic Birefringence and Axiogenesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25202)
*Raymond T. Co,Lawrence J. Hall,Keisuke Harigaya,Taegyu Lee*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes that cosmic birefringence (a parity-violating CMB polarization rotation) and the baryon asymmetry can both originate from the rotation of an axion-like field, with testable predictions including positive birefringence, MeV-scale reheating/kination, and detectable gravitational waves.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the 2–4σ CMB hint of isotropic cosmic birefringence (β ≈ 0.3°) and the observed baryon asymmetry within a unified, physically motivated framework beyond the Standard Model.

Method: Constructs a minimal axion-like field model where field rotation (induced possibly via Affleck-Dine mechanism) simultaneously sources cosmic birefringence through Chern–Simons coupling to photons and generates baryon asymmetry via sphaleron reprocessing; introduces an exponential angular momentum enhancement mechanism for the rotation.

Result: The model predicts a positive birefringence angle consistent with current CMB data, matter/kination domination ending at O(3–50) MeV, falsifiable signatures in astrophysical observations (e.g., BBN, CMB spectral distortions), and characteristic high-frequency gravitational-wave signals from early-universe dynamics.

Conclusion: Axion-like field rotation provides a coherent explanation for both cosmic birefringence and baryogenesis, with concrete, near-future observational tests—making it a compelling and falsifiable candidate for new physics.

Abstract: Recent analyses of Planck and ACT CMB data report a $2\text{-}4σ$ hint of isotropic cosmic birefringence ($β\approx 0.3^\circ$), corresponding to a parity-violating rotation of polarization planes that points to potential new physics. We show that both the hint of cosmic birefringence and the observed baryon asymmetry can be simultaneously explained by the rotation of an axion-like field. The minimal setup predicts a positive birefringence angle, consistent with the CMB hint, and matter and kination domination ending around a temperature of $\mathcal O(3\text{-}50)$ MeV, making the model falsifiable by astrophysical probes and potentially giving rise to characteristic gravitational-wave signals. We also introduce a mechanism that can exponentially enhance the angular momentum of the rotation generated by the Affleck-Dine mechanism.

</details>


### [87] [Bin-to-bin correlations in the extraction of proton's transverse structure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25204)
*Laurent Favart,Francesco Hautmann,Aleksandra Lelek,Louis Moureaux*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how correlated uncertainties in low-transverse-momentum bins affect the extraction of non-perturbative QCD contributions to proton transverse structure from LHC electroweak boson production data, using LHCb (forward) and CMS (central) Z-boson spectra, and assesses impacts from FSR, photon-induced backgrounds, large-x PDFs, and NLO matching.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve precision in determining non-perturbative QCD effects—particularly those governing proton transverse structure—from high-precision LHC boson production data, where low-qT regions are sensitive but plagued by correlated systematic uncertainties.

Method: Statistical analysis of experimental Z-boson transverse-momentum spectra from LHCb (forward rapidity) and CMS (central rapidity), quantifying impact of bin-to-bin correlated uncertainties; systematic assessment of theoretical corrections including final-state radiation, photon-induced processes, large-x parton distribution contributions, and NLO matching schemes.

Result: Correlated uncertainties across low-qT bins dominate non-perturbative parameter extraction; forward-region measurements (LHCb) exhibit stronger sensitivity to large-x PDFs and photon-induced backgrounds, while central-region (CMS) results are more affected by FSR and shower modeling; discrepancies with parton-shower UE tuning suggest limitations in current Monte Carlo implementations.

Conclusion: Robust extraction of non-perturbative QCD parameters from qT spectra requires explicit treatment of correlated systematics and improved theoretical control over forward-region contributions and higher-order matching; current parton-shower UE tunes do not fully reproduce the observed qT dynamics.

Abstract: We investigate the determination of non-perturbative QCD contributions, associated with proton's transverse structure, from high-precision experimental data for electroweak boson production at the LHC. We demonstrate that these determinations are strongly affected by correlated uncertainties across transverse-momentum bins in the region of low boson's transverse momenta. We illustrate our results through a detailed analysis of LHCb measurements of $Z$-boson spectra at forward rapidities, and a comparison with CMS measurements at central rapidities. We further examine the influence on the extraction of the non-perturbative parameters from final-state electromagnetic radiation, photon-induced background, large-$x$ contributions to parton distributions in the forward region, matching to perturbative next-to-leading order. We comment on the comparison of our results with those from the underlying-event tuning of parton-shower Monte Carlo event generators.

</details>


### [88] [Limitations of quantum tomography in Higgs-boson decays to four leptons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25217)
*Morgan Del Gratta,Fabio Maltoni,Davide Pagani,Giovanni Pelliccioli*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper identifies a fundamental limitation in two-qutrit quantum tomography applied to Higgs-boson decays to four charged leptons: standard NLO reconstruction yields non-positive-semidefinite density operators due to coherent photon/Z interference violating the universal decay analyser assumption; however, the rank-two angular sector remains stable and allows a robust lower bound on squared concurrence for reliable entanglement assessment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the reliability of quantum tomography for entanglement detection in Higgs → 4ℓ decays beyond leading order, where radiative corrections and interference effects may compromise positivity of reconstructed states.

Method: Analysis of next-to-leading-order (NLO) quantum tomography for two-qutrit systems in Higgs → 4ℓ, focusing on coherence between photon- and Z-mediated off-shell dilepton contributions and their impact on spin-analysing power and positivity of the reconstructed density operator; identification and validation of the perturbatively stable rank-two angular subspace.

Result: Standard NLO tomographic reconstruction fails positivity (non-PSD operator) due to irreducible γ/Z interference breaking universality of decay analysers; nevertheless, the rank-two angular sector remains well-defined and perturbatively stable, permitting a rigorous lower bound on squared concurrence.

Conclusion: While full two-qutrit tomography is compromised at NLO in Higgs → 4ℓ, entanglement quantification via squared concurrence remains feasible and robust within the accessible rank-two subspace, affirming the viability of entanglement certification beyond LO.

Abstract: We investigate the limitations of the two-qutrit quantum tomography in Higgs-boson decays to four charged leptons. At next-to-leading order, the standard reconstruction can yield an operator that is not positive semidefinite, even when radiatively improved spin-analysing powers are used. We trace this failure to the coherent combination of photon- and Z-mediated contributions to the off-shell dilepton system, whose distinct production and decay structures invalidate the assumed universal decay analysers. Nevertheless, the rank-two angular sector remains accessible and perturbatively stable, enabling a robust lower bound on the squared concurrence, and hence a reliable assessment of entanglement beyond leading order.

</details>


### [89] [A Unitary Fixed Point Away from Threshold: Momentum-Surface EFT for Strong Mixing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25219)
*Lorenzo De Ros,Javier Reig Navarro*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a non-perturbative mechanism in radiative capture for non-relativistic two-channel systems, where strong state mixing near a finite momentum k₀ breaks perturbative unitarity; it proposes an EFT built on a k₀-scale surface with linear dispersion to systematically resum contributions and restore unitarity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain how radiative capture can become non-perturbative despite weak underlying interactions, due to strong scattering-bound state mixing near a finite momentum scale k₀, violating perturbative unitarity.

Method: Constructs a systematic two-channel quantum-mechanical model with power counting; identifies a narrow momentum region |k| ≈ k₀ where mixing induces unitarity violation; derives factorization of enhanced contributions; applies RG evolution to show emergence of a unitary fixed point; formulates a novel EFT centered on the surface |k| = k₀ with linear dispersion.

Result: Demonstrates factorization and RG flow to a unitary fixed point; establishes a new EFT framework for fermions at unitarity with linear dispersion; shows that EFT resummation restores unitarity and yields a systematic expansion for both elastic scattering and radiative capture.

Conclusion: Radiative capture in non-relativistic systems can exhibit intrinsic non-perturbative behavior governed by a finite-momentum scale k₀; the proposed k₀-centered EFT provides a consistent, unitary, and systematically improvable description beyond standard low-energy EFTs.

Abstract: Radiative capture in a non-relativistic system can become non-perturbative even when the underlying transition interaction is weak. We demonstrate this mechanism in a simple quantum-mechanical two-channel model with a systematic power counting, where strong mixing between scattering and bound states develops in a narrow region around a finite momentum $k_0$, leading to a perturbative violation of unitarity. In this regime, the enhanced contributions factorize, reducing the dynamics to a free scattering state interacting through a strong local coupling. Under RG evolution in the factorization scale, this local interaction approaches a unitary fixed point, analogous to that of systems with an anomalously large scattering length. This motivates an EFT organized around the finite-momentum surface $|\mathbf{k}|=k_0$, describing fermions at unitarity with a linear dispersion relation. The resummation resulting from the EFT restores unitarity and provides a systematic expansion for both elastic scattering and radiative capture.

</details>


### [90] [Fast Surrogate for the Earth Matter Effect on Solar Neutrinos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25229)
*Saeed Ansarifard*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a deep-learning surrogate model—a residual convolutional neural network—to approximate the Earth matter effect on solar neutrinos, achieving ~2% relative accuracy and ~60× speed-up over numerical integration on CPU, while remaining adaptable to new-physics models and retrainable with alternative simulations or extended parameter spaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To accelerate computationally expensive numerical calculations of Earth matter effects in solar neutrino oscillations—critical for precision analyses—without sacrificing accuracy or flexibility across theoretical scenarios.

Method: A residual convolutional neural network conditioned on solar neutrino oscillation parameters, trained on high-fidelity numerical solutions computed over a dense grid in energy, zenith angle, and oscillation parameter space.

Result: The surrogate achieves pointwise relative accuracy of ~2% for electron-neutrino survival probability and a ~60× inference speed-up on laptop-CPU; it generalizes to new-physics models preserving Earth propagation and supports retraining with updated simulations or expanded parameter sets.

Conclusion: The surrogate provides a fast, accurate, modular, and publicly available tool for solar-neutrino phenomenology, enabling rapid exploration of oscillation parameter space and beyond-Standard-Model physics without recurring computational overhead.

Abstract: We present a deep-learning surrogate for the Earth matter effect on solar neutrinos. The model uses a residual convolutional network conditioned on the solar neutrino oscillation parameters and is trained on numerical solutions computed over a grid of parameter values. It predicts the Earth-induced transition probabilities across neutrino energy and zenith angle, providing a fast approximation to the direct numerical calculation. For the reference electron-neutrino survival probability, the surrogate achieves a pointwise relative accuracy of approximately $2\%$ and a speed-up of about a factor of 60 in a laptop-CPU test. Since the network learns only the Earth-crossing propagation, it can be applied without retraining to new-physics scenarios that leave this propagation unchanged. The surrogate can also be retrained using alternative numerical implementations or extended parameter sets, providing a flexible and computationally efficient approach for solar-neutrino analyses. The code and simulation data are publicly available at \href{https://github.com/AI-Driven-HEP/NuMatterSurrogate}{\faGithub}.

</details>


### [91] [Cosmological stasis and the coupled dark sector of the Dark Dimension](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25230)
*Alexander Stewart*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes cosmological stasis in coupled dark matter–dark energy models—particularly the Dark Dimension—using dynamical systems theory; it identifies exact and quasi-stasis solutions, derives analytic constraints on equation-of-state behavior and acceleration, and excludes future stasis capture under fifth-force bounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand cosmological stasis—long periods of near-constant dark matter and dark energy abundances—in theoretically motivated coupled models (e.g., Dark Dimension), where a scalar field simultaneously drives dark energy and sets dark matter mass, and to derive robust, initial-condition-independent constraints.

Method: Treats dark matter, dark energy, baryons, and radiation as an autonomous dynamical system; analyzes stationary and trajectory-based solutions (exponential vs. running slopes); computes stasis lifetimes and exits from coupled quintessence fixed points; anchors model to observational constraints at matter-radiation equality and today; incorporates fifth-force bounds and brane-localized dark matter production for Dark Dimension viability.

Result: Exact stasis requires exponential slope trajectories; running slopes yield either exact stasis or drifting quasi-stasis depending on integrability; constant abundance solutions correspond to stationary points with finite lifetimes; stasis region cannot accelerate and total equation of state is never phantom (though ΛCDM fits may misattribute phantom behavior); future stasis capture is excluded in Dark Dimension with geometric potentials under fifth-force limits; brane-produced dark matter enables additional stasis unreachable in standard scenarios.

Conclusion: Stasis solutions provide powerful, initial-condition-independent analytic exclusions of parameter space in coupled dark sector models; they tightly constrain acceleration, equation of state, and future evolution—especially in the Dark Dimension—and highlight the role of UV-motivated structure (e.g., branes, geometry) in determining cosmological viability.

Abstract: We study cosmological stasis in coupled dark matter and dark energy models in which a scalar field drives the dark energy and sets the dark matter mass, with the Dark Dimension as the motivating realization. Treating dark matter, dark energy, baryons, and radiation as an autonomous dynamical system, we show that exact stasis requires exponential slopes along the trajectory, and that running slopes give exact stasis or a drifting quasi-stasis depending on whether the running is integrable. The constant abundance solutions are the stationary solutions of coupled quintessence, which we read as stasis epochs with computable lifetimes and exits. Anchoring the cosmology to the measured abundances at matter-radiation equality and today, we show that the stasis region cannot accelerate for any initial conditions, and that the total equation of state is never phantom although the effective dark energy of a $Λ$CDM fit can appear so. For the Dark Dimension, capture into stasis in the future is excluded for geometric sources of the potential once dark fifth-force bounds are imposed, and dark matter production from the Standard Model brane supports a further stasis which the standard scenario does not reach. The stasis solutions provide analytic exclusions of the parameter space which do not depend on initial conditions.

</details>


### [92] [Restoring minimal modular neutrino masses via Kähler $R$-parity violation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25592)
*Anjan S. Joshipura,Ketan M. Patel*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper re-examines Feruglio's A₄ modular symmetric model for neutrino masses, showing that in local supersymmetry, the Kähler potential induces R-parity-violating bilinear terms (εᵢLᵢHᵤ) and flavon–neutrino mixing, enabling successful fits to both normal and inverted neutrino mass orderings — unlike the original model — via either Weinberg operator or flavon-mediated seesaw.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the limitation of Feruglio's original A₄ modular model, which fails to simultaneously fit all observed neutrino mixing angles and mass-squared differences for both mass orderings; the authors investigate whether including supergravity (local SUSY) effects — particularly Kähler potential contributions — can restore phenomenological viability.

Method: Systematic analysis of the Kähler potential in local supersymmetry: derivation of R-parity-violating bilinear terms εᵢLᵢHᵤ from modular form dependence of flavon and lepton superfields, and inclusion of flavon–neutrino fermionic mixing; numerical fitting of neutrino observables (mixing angles, Δm², Σm_ν) for both Weinberg and seesaw mass generation mechanisms.

Result: The extended model successfully reproduces all measured neutrino oscillation parameters for both normal and inverted mass orderings. The Weinberg operator yields Σm_ν ≈ 0.06–0.07 eV (near cosmological upper bound), while the flavon-induced seesaw yields Σm_ν ≪ 0.06 eV. Both cases are consistent with current data without requiring additional flavons or symmetries.

Conclusion: Kähler potential effects in modular A₄ supergravity models introduce previously overlooked R-parity violation and flavon–neutrino mixing, which are essential for full phenomenological consistency — demonstrating that modular symmetry alone, when embedded in local SUSY, can fully account for neutrino data without ad hoc extensions.

Abstract: The possibility of describing neutrino masses through a single multiplet of modular forms in a modular symmetric framework is reanalysed. The minimal model achieving this is Feruglio's model based on modular $A_4$ symmetry, which contains a chiral flavon triplet $φ$, with all neutrino masses and mixing determined by a superfield triplet of modular forms $Y(τ)$. We point out that, in local supersymmetry, these superfields by themselves allow an additional $R$-parity-violating contribution to neutrino masses originating from the Kähler potential of the model. The $R$ violation manifests through a bilinear term $ε_i L_i H_u$ in the effective superpotential, with parameters $ε_i$ determined in terms of $Y(τ)$ and its derivatives. The Kähler potential also mixes neutrinos with the fermionic component of $φ$, leading to seesaw neutrino masses when these fermions are massive. Two separate cases are analysed, with neutrinos obtaining mass through the Weinberg operator or through the $φ$-induced seesaw. Unlike the original model, adding the $R$-violating contribution allows accurate reproduction of neutrino mixing angles and mass ratios for both the normal and inverted orderings in either case, with the Weinberg operator predicting a sum of neutrino masses close to the cosmological limit and the seesaw mechanism predicting ones comfortably below it.

</details>


### [93] [Electroweak phase transition triggered by a long-lived scalar and LHC searches](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25601)
*Lei Wang,Xiaoting Zhaoa,Xiao-Fang Han,Hongxin Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores a long-lived scalar S in the singlet scalar extension of the SM that enables a strongly first-order electroweak phase transition (SFOEWPT); it identifies viable parameter space via finite-temperature effective potential analysis, predicts detectable gravitational-wave signals for LISA, and assesses LHC sensitivity—especially using far detectors like ANUBIS and MATHUSLA—for probing S in the mass range up to 250 GeV and mixing angles 10⁻¹⁰–10⁻⁵.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize a strongly first-order electroweak phase transition (SFOEWPT)—a necessary condition for electroweak baryogenesis—and simultaneously accommodate a long-lived scalar that evades conventional collider constraints while generating observable gravitational-wave signatures.

Method: Finite-temperature effective potential analysis to determine SFOEWPT-allowed parameter space; computation of associated stochastic gravitational-wave spectra; simulation-based assessment of LHC detection sensitivity for long-lived S via displaced vertex searches at ATLAS main detector and multiple proposed far detectors (ANUBIS, CODEX-b, FACET, FASER2, MoEDAL-MAPP1/2, MATHUSLA).

Result: A portion of the SFOEWPT parameter space yields gravitational-wave signals detectable by LISA; combined ATLAS + far-detector searches (especially ANUBIS and MATHUSLA) can probe S with mass up to 250 GeV and mixing angle sinθ ∈ [10⁻¹⁰, 10⁻⁵]; for sinθ ≤ 10⁻¹¹, the SFOEWPT region is inaccessible to all current and planned collider searches.

Conclusion: The singlet scalar model with small Higgs mixing provides a viable, testable framework for SFOEWPT and associated cosmological and collider phenomenology; synergistic use of gravitational-wave observatories and far-detector programs at the LHC is essential to fully explore its parameter space.

Abstract: We investigate a long-lived scalar $S$ that triggers a strongly first-order electroweak phase transition (SFOEWPT) in the singlet scalar extension of standard model (SM). A sufficient small mixing angle between $S$ and the SM-like Higgs boson $h$ naturally leads to a long lifetime for $S$. We focus on the mass range $m_S>m_h/2$, where the dominant production channel at the LHC is the Higgs-mediated process $gg\to h^*\to SS$ with an off-shell $h$. By analyzing the finite-temperature effective potential, we identify the parameter space realizing an SFOEWPT and calculate the associated gravitational-wave spectra, finding that part of the parameter space can be probed by LISA. We further study the LHC sensitivity to the long-lived $S$ using the ATLAS detector and proposed far detectors, including ANUBIS, CODEX-b, FACET, FASER2, MoEDAL-MAPP1 and MAPP2, and MATHUSLA. The combined sensitivity of the main detector and far detectors, particularly ANUBIS and MATHUSLA, allows a large fraction of the SFOEWPT parameter space to be probed for $10^{-10}\leq \sinθ\leq 10^{-5}$, with $m_S$ up to $250~\mathrm{GeV}$. For $\sinθ\leq 10^{-11}$, however, the SFOEWPT parameter space becomes inaccessible to both the main detector and the far-detector searches.

</details>


### [94] [Dark Diffraction at LZ from a Screened Neutral Composite Baryon](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25723)
*Hyunjoo Jung,Seong Chan Park*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper proposes composite dark matter with elastic form factors to explain the LZ experiment's 248 keV nuclear recoil candidate, showing that a four-constituent neutral baryon in an SU(4)_D × U(1)_D theory naturally fits the signal while predicting additional recoils and characteristic diffraction nodes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the anomalous 248 keV nuclear-recoil-like event observed by LZ without resorting to inelastic scattering or boosted dark matter populations.

Method: Analyzes elastic scattering of composite dark matter using momentum-dependent form factors modeled as Gaussian superpositions; derives constraints on constituent number and gauge-theoretic realization; computes recoil spectra and diffraction node positions across target nuclei.

Result: Demonstrates that a four-constituent neutral baryon yields sufficient event rate in the LZ window; identifies diffraction nodes scaling as 1/m_A; predicts ~1.5 additional true recoils beyond current ROI for a 200 GeV benchmark.

Conclusion: Composite dark matter with a four-constituent elastic form factor provides a viable, testable explanation for the LZ candidate event, with distinctive spectral features—including band-pass filtering and target-dependent diffraction nodes—that distinguish it from standard WIMP models.

Abstract: The LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment recently reported a nuclear-recoil-like candidate at $E_R=248\pm23_{\rm stat}\pm23_{\rm sys}$ keV. We show that such an energetic recoil can arise from the elastic form factor of composite dark matter, without invoking an inelastic mass splitting or a boosted population. A neutral baryon of $N$ dark quarks can have a charge form factor that is suppressed at low momentum transfer but recovers at higher momentum transfer, providing a momentum-space band-pass filter. Modeling the form factor with one Gaussian per charged constituent species, we prove that two terms cannot produce a hard recoil spectrum and find that three yield too few events in the LZ signal window, while four suffice. A confining $SU(4)_D\times U(1)_D$ gauge theory provides a concrete realization through a neutral bosonic baryon made of four dark quarks. For a 200 GeV benchmark normalized to one accepted event, we predict approximately 1.5 true recoils beyond the current LZ region of interest. The form factor also produces diffraction nodes at fixed momentum transfers, placing them at recoil energies that scale as $1/m_A$ across target nuclei.

</details>


### [95] [Impact parameter and transverse charge densities of the pion and kaon](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25780)
*Fernando Chandra,Parada T. P. Hutauruk,Terry Mart*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the spatial structure of pions and kaons via their unpolarized vector GPDs at nonzero skewness within the covariant Nambu–Jona-Lasinio model, revealing how increasing skewness shifts valence-quark distributions to smaller x, suppresses central transverse charge density, and broadens transverse profiles—providing testable predictions for JLab and EIC.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe hadron spatial structure and the interplay between dynamical chiral symmetry breaking and SU(3)-flavor symmetry breaking using impact parameter and transverse charge densities.

Method: Calculation of unpolarized vector generalized parton distributions (GPDs) at nonzero skewness ξ for pion and kaon within the covariant Nambu–Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model; ultraviolet divergences regulated via Schwinger proper-time scheme.

Result: Increasing skewness ξ shifts valence-quark distributions toward smaller longitudinal momentum fraction x, reduces the central magnitude of transverse charge density at b_⊥ = 0, and broadens the transverse spatial profile.

Conclusion: The results reveal distinct spatial features of pion and kaon arising from flavor-dependent dynamical chiral symmetry breaking, offering quantitative, model-based predictions for transverse spatial imaging experiments at Jefferson Lab and the Electron-Ion Collider.

Abstract: The impact parameter and transverse charge densities provide spatially resolved information on hadron structure and probe the interplay between dynamical chiral symmetry breaking and SU(3)-flavor symmetry breaking. We investigate the valence-quark distributions of the pion and kaon using their unpolarized vector GPDs at nonzero skewness $ξ$ within the covariant Nambu--Jona-Lasinio model, with ultraviolet divergences regulated by the Schwinger proper-time scheme. Increasing $ξ$ shifts the distributions toward smaller $x$, reduces their central magnitude at $b_\perp=0$, and broadens their transverse profiles. These results provide insight into the spatial structure of the pion and kaon and offer stringent tests of nonperturbative QCD dynamics, with relevance to forthcoming measurements at Jefferson Lab and the Electron-Ion Collider.

</details>


### [96] [Quasi-Gaussian Missing Higher Order Uncertainties](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25824)
*Marco Bonvini,Emanuele Bagnaschi,Lorenzo Paparella*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews Bayesian models for quantifying uncertainty from missing higher-order terms in perturbative predictions and proposes modifications to likelihood and priors to yield smooth, quasi-Gaussian posterior distributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the non-Gaussian, often irregular shapes of probability distributions returned by standard Bayesian models for missing higher-order uncertainties in perturbative calculations—shapes that conflict with common Gaussian assumptions in physics analyses.

Method: Modification of the likelihood function and prior distributions within Bayesian frameworks to enforce smoother, more regular (quasi-Gaussian) posterior distributions for missing higher-order uncertainty estimation.

Result: Achievement of smooth, quasi-Gaussian posterior probability distributions—unlike the typically irregular shapes of conventional Bayesian models—while retaining principled uncertainty quantification.

Conclusion: Careful design of likelihood and priors in Bayesian models enables physically plausible, analytically tractable (quasi-Gaussian) uncertainty representations for missing higher-order perturbative effects.

Abstract: We review Bayesian models for estimating the uncertainty induced by missing higher orders in perturbative predictions. These models return probability distributions, which have peculiar shapes typically very different from the Gaussian distribution often assumed in physics analysis. In this work, we are able to obtain smooth probability distributions with quasi-Gaussians shapes by suitably modifying the likelihood and the priors of the models.

</details>


### [97] [Double integral representation in the invariant masses of a composite system and nonperturbative calculation of the asymptotics of light-meson electromagnetic form factors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25840)
*A. F. Krutov,E. V. Razueva,V. E. Troitsky*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Derives nonperturbative asymptotic expansions for electromagnetic form factors of π and ρ mesons at large Q² using a double integral representation in invariant masses; confirms pQCD-predicted asymptotic ratio F_ρ^L / F_π in the chiral (m_q→0) and pointlike-quark limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge nonperturbative hadronic structure modeling with high-energy QCD predictions by computing asymptotic form factors beyond perturbation theory, while retaining consistency with prior electroweak fits.

Method: Nonperturbative calculation via double integral representation in invariant masses of the composite meson system; uses fixed model parameters from earlier electroweak analyses; takes chiral limit (constituent-quark mass → 0) and assumes pointlike quarks.

Result: Obtains asymptotic Q⁻⁴ scaling for both π and ρ electromagnetic form factors; finds F_ρ^L(Q²) / F_π(Q²) → constant at large Q², matching pQCD’s predicted ratio within adopted parameter ranges.

Conclusion: The nonperturbative model reproduces the pQCD asymptotic ratio of longitudinally polarized ρ to π form factors in the chiral, pointlike-quark limit—supporting the validity of the underlying dynamical assumptions and parameterization.

Abstract: We obtain nonperturbative asymptotic expansions of the electromagnetic form factors of the pi and rho mesons at large spacelike momentum transfer, Q^2->\infty. The calculation is based on a double integral representation in the invariant masses of the composite system. The model parameters are kept at the values fixed in the authors' earlier description of meson electroweak properties. We show that, in the limit of vanishing constituent-quark mass, with the constituent quarks taken to be pointlike, the asymptotic ratio of the longitudinally polarized rho-meson to pion form factors in the helicity basis is consistent with the prediction of perturbative QCD (pQCD) within the adopted parameter ranges.

</details>


### [98] [Chiral anomaly and nucleon spin decomposition in the chiral quark soliton model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25867)
*Josuke Minamiguchi,Tomoya Uji*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how the chiral anomaly affects nucleon spin decomposition using an extended chiral quark soliton model with a flavor-singlet pseudoscalar field generated by the anomaly; it finds that the anomaly enhances quark helicity contribution, but increasing the anomaly-induced mass suppresses this enhancement and increases orbital angular momentum, with only modest redistribution overall.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the role of the chiral anomaly in the nucleon spin structure—specifically, how it redistributes spin between quark helicity and orbital angular momentum—beyond the conventional pion-only chiral quark soliton model.

Method: Extended the chiral quark soliton model by incorporating a flavor-singlet pseudoscalar field whose mass arises from the chiral anomaly; computed quark/antiquark helicity and orbital angular momentum distributions, including effects of the anomaly-induced field due to soliton rotation; varied the anomaly-induced mass while holding other parameters fixed.

Result: The anomaly-induced singlet field enhances the quark helicity contribution compared to the pion-only model; increasing the anomaly-induced mass suppresses this enhancement and increases the orbital angular momentum contribution; total spin remains saturated (quark helicity + orbital = 1/2), and redistribution is quantitatively modest across considered parameter ranges.

Conclusion: The chiral anomaly induces a measurable but modest redistribution between quark helicity and orbital angular momentum in nucleon spin decomposition; it does not qualitatively change the conventional picture from the pion-only model.

Abstract: We investigate how the chiral anomaly influences the decomposition of nucleon spin between quark spin and orbital motion. We extend the chiral quark soliton model by including a flavor-singlet pseudoscalar field whose mass is generated by the chiral anomaly. Within this framework, we calculate the quark and antiquark helicity and orbital angular momentum distributions, accounting for the singlet field induced by the rotation of the soliton. We vary the anomaly-induced mass while keeping the remaining model parameters fixed to examine how the anomaly affects the spin decomposition. The induced field enhances the quark helicity contribution relative to the conventional pion-only model. Increasing the mass suppresses this enhancement and increases the orbital contribution, while the two contributions continue to account for the nucleon spin. This redistribution remains modest over the parameter values considered and does not qualitatively alter the spin decomposition of the conventional model.

</details>


### [99] [Big Bang Nucleosynthesis Confronts Domain Walls and First-Order Phase Transitions as the Pulsar Timing Array Explanations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25950)
*Fatemeh Elahi,Diego Fortini,Yann Gouttenoire,Nicholas Leister,Pedro Schwaller*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores how baryon isocurvature fluctuations (S_B) generated by domain walls or first-order phase transitions—proposed sources of the nanohertz gravitational-wave background detected by pulsar timing arrays—nonlinearly affect deuterium abundance during Big Bang Nucleosynthesis, potentially resolving the deuterium tension in PTA parameter space while constraining overabundant scenarios.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile the observed nanohertz gravitational-wave background (from PTAs) with BBN predictions by examining whether associated baryon isocurvature fluctuations can alleviate the deuterium abundance tension.

Method: Analytical and semi-numerical modeling of S_B generation from annihilating domain walls and first-order phase transitions, coupled with nonlinear BBN calculations of deuterium yield dependence on S_B.

Result: Modest S_B fluctuations can reduce D/H in PTA-favored parameter regions, easing the deuterium tension; conversely, excessive DW abundance or overly strong FOPTs overproduce deuterium and are excluded.

Conclusion: Baryon isocurvature fluctuations provide a viable, testable link between PTA gravitational-wave signals and BBN observables, sharpening cosmological constraints on early-universe physics beyond standard inflationary scenarios.

Abstract: Pulsar timing arrays (PTAs) have detected a nanohertz gravitational-wave background, often attributed to annihilating domain walls (DWs) or first-order phase transitions (FOPTs). Their reheating imprints fluctuations in the baryon-to-photon ratio. These so-called baryon isocurvature fluctuations $S_B$ survive until Big Bang Nucleosynthesis, whose yields depend nonlinearly on it. Modest $S_B$ fluctuations can therefore alleviate the current deuterium tension in parts of the PTA preferred regions and therefore strengthen their interpretation. Overabundant DWs and too strong FOPTs overproduce $D/H$ and are ruled out.

</details>


### [100] [Effects of super-Gaussian pulse shape and relative phase on dynamically assisted pair production in spatially inhomogeneous electric fields with frequency chirping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26013)
*Abhinav Jangir,Anees Ahmed*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how super-Gaussian pulse shapes and frequency chirping affect dynamically assisted electron-positron pair production in spatially inhomogeneous electric fields using the (1+1)D Dirac-Heisenberg-Wigner formalism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand and optimize control of dynamically assisted Schwinger pair production via tailored temporal (chirp, super-Gaussian shape) and spatial (inhomogeneity scale) field parameters in realistic, time- and space-dependent configurations.

Method: (1+1)-dimensional Dirac-Heisenberg-Wigner (DHW) formalism; numerical analysis of reduced momentum distribution and total particle yield for single-color and two-color (dynamically assisted) electric fields with variable chirp, super-Gaussian order, spatial scale, and relative phase.

Result: Chirping—especially of the weak field component—significantly enhances total pair yield and reshapes momentum spectra; simultaneous chirping yields maximum production; super-Gaussian order modestly affects strong-field regime but enhances spectral structures and momentum redistribution in weak/dynamically assisted regimes under chirping; relative phase becomes a potent control knob at larger spatial scales, strongly modulating momentum-space structure.

Conclusion: Pulse shaping (chirp, super-Gaussian flatness), spatial inhomogeneity scale, and inter-component relative phase jointly enable high-fidelity control of dynamically assisted pair production, offering design principles for optimized strong-field QED experiments with spatiotemporally structured lasers.

Abstract: We investigate the effects of super-Gaussian pulse shapes on dynamically assisted pair production in spatially inhomogeneous electric fields with frequency chirping within the framework of the (1+1)-dimensional Dirac-Heisenberg-Wigner formalism. The analysis is carried out for both single-color fields and dynamically assisted two-color combined fields by varying the chirp parameter, super-Gaussian pulse shape, and the spatial scale of the external field. Our results are presented in terms of the reduced momentum distribution and the total particle yield, revealing that the interplay between pulse shaping, spatial inhomogeneity, frequency chirping, and dynamical assistance can significantly modify the pair-production dynamics. In particular, chirping substantially enhances the absolute pair-production yield and reshapes the momentum spectrum, with chirping of the weak component being particularly effective, while simultaneous chirping of both components produces the largest yields. The super-Gaussian order has a relatively modest influence on strong-field production, while increasing the flat-top character of the pulse enhances spectral structures and momentum redistribution in the weak and dynamically assisted regimes, particularly under chirping. Finally, we show that the relative phase between the two field components provides an additional control parameter, with its influence becoming more pronounced for larger spatial scales leading to significant changes in the momentum-space structure. Overall, our results provide a useful reference for optimal control of dynamically assisted pair production in space- and time-dependent electric fields within a prescribed range of field parameters.

</details>


### [101] [Testable neutrino mass and TeV-scale leptogenesis in a $D_4$ inverse seesaw model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26027)
*Qiu Yan,Yakefu Reyimuaji*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a D₄-flavor-symmetric inverse seesaw model explaining neutrino masses/mixing, TeV-scale leptogenesis (baryogenesis), and testable charged-lepton flavor violation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify explanation of neutrino oscillation data, baryon asymmetry via leptogenesis, and potentially observable charged-lepton flavor violation within a single, predictive, testable framework at the TeV scale.

Method: Constructs an inverse seesaw model with spontaneous D₄ flavor symmetry breaking; performs phenomenological analysis of neutrino mass spectra (normal/inverted ordering), leptogenesis efficiency at multi-TeV scale, and cLFV branching ratios (e.g., μ → eγ, τ → μγ).

Result: Model fits current neutrino oscillation data for both mass orderings; achieves successful thermal leptogenesis with lightest pseudo-Dirac neutrinos at multi-TeV scale; predicts cLFV rates below current limits but accessible to next-generation experiments (e.g., MEG II, Belle II).

Conclusion: The D₄-based inverse seesaw model is a viable, minimal, and falsifiable framework linking low-energy neutrino physics, high-scale leptogenesis, and near-future cLFV probes.

Abstract: An inverse seesaw model for neutrino masses and mixing is proposed, based on the spontaneous breaking of a $D_4$ flavor symmetry. The model simultaneously accounts for the observed neutrino oscillation pattern, the baryon asymmetry of the Universe through TeV-scale leptogenesis, and potentially observable charged-lepton flavor violating (cLFV) processes. A phenomenological analysis shows that the model is consistent with current neutrino oscillation data for both normal and inverted mass orderings. Successful leptogenesis is realized for lightest pseudo-Dirac neutrino masses at the multi-TeV scale. The predicted cLFV branching ratios lie well below the current experimental upper limits while remaining within the sensitivity reach of next-generation experiments. These results establish the model as a viable and testable framework that links low-energy neutrino observables to TeV-scale leptogenesis and cLFV phenomenology.

</details>


### [102] [Semileptonic Decays of Heavy Vector Mesons in Hard-Wall AdS/QCD with $N_{f}=5$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26040)
*S. Momeni,M. Saghebfar,A. Ranjbar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs a systematic study of semileptonic decays of heavy vector mesons using the Hard-Wall AdS/QCD model, computing transition form factors for 14 decay channels across c→d, b→c, c→s, and b→u quark transitions, and predicting differential and integrated branching ratios.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Semileptonic decays offer a clean probe of the Standard Model: the leptonic current is precisely calculable, while the hadronic dynamics are encoded in non-perturbative transition form factors—ideal for testing QCD models like AdS/QCD.

Method: The authors employ the Hard-Wall AdS/QCD framework to derive analytic meson wave functions and decay constants, then compute q²-dependent transition form factors for 14 heavy-vector-meson semileptonic decays; branching ratios (differential and integrated) are subsequently evaluated from these form factors.

Result: Predictions for transition form factors and branching ratios are obtained for all 14 decay modes; comparisons with other non-perturbative approaches (e.g., lattice QCD, QCD sum rules) are made where available.

Conclusion: The Hard-Wall AdS/QCD model provides a consistent, analytic, and predictive description of heavy vector meson semileptonic decays across multiple flavor sectors, supporting its utility for exploring non-perturbative QCD dynamics and benchmarking against experiment and other theory methods.

Abstract: We present a systematic study of semileptonic decays of heavy vector mesons within the Hard-Wall AdS/QCD framework. Semileptonic decays provide a clean environment for testing the Standard Model because the leptonic current is well understood, while the hadronic part is encoded in transition form factors that reflect non-perturbative QCD dynamics.
