The processes under consideration include: \( ψ\to D^{*-} ( D^{-} ) \ell^{+} ν_{\ell} \), \( D^{*0} \to π^{-} \ell^{+} ν_{\ell} \), \( D^{*+}_{s} \to K^{0} \ell^{+} ν_{\ell} \), \( D^{*0} \to ρ^{-} \ell^{+} ν_{\ell} \), \( B_c^{*+} \to B^{*0} \ell^{+} ν_{\ell} \), \( B_c^{*+} \to ψ\,\ell^{+} ν_{\ell} \), \( B^{*-} \to D^{*0} \ell^{-} \barν_{\ell} \), \( B^{*0} \to D^{*+}(D^{+}) \ell^{-} \barν_{\ell} \), \( B^{*0}_{s} \to D^{*+}_{s} (D^{+}_{s}) \ell^{-} \barν_{\ell} \), \( ψ\to D^{*-}_{s}(D^{-}_{s}) \ell^{+} ν_{\ell} \), \( D^{*0} \to K^{*-} (K^{-}) \ell^{+} ν_{\ell} \), \( B_c^{*-} \to D^{*0} \ell^{-} \barν_{\ell} \), \( \bar{B_{s}}^{*0} \to K^{*+} \ell^{-} \barν_{\ell} \), and \( \bar{B}^{*0} \to ρ^{+} \ell^{-} \barν_{\ell} \). These decays correspond to four different quark-level transitions: \(c\to d\), \(b\to c\), \(c\to s\), and \(b\to u\), allowing us to explore a broad range of flavor dynamics.\\ Using the Hard-Wall AdS/QCD model, we compute the relevant transition form factors as functions of the momentum transfer \(q^{2}\). The framework provides analytic expressions for meson wave functions and decay constants, which serve as inputs for the form-factor calculations. From these form factors, we evaluate the differential and integrated branching ratios for each decay mode. Where possible, we compare our predictions with results obtained from other non-perturbative approaches.

</details>


### [103] [Singlet Extended Mirror Standard Model World as Dark Matter and Gravitational Waves Imprints of a High Scale Mirror Phase Transitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26271)
*Asterios T. Papadopoulos,V. K. Oikonomou*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores a mirror Standard Model with a singlet scalar extension coupled to the mirror Higgs via dimension-six operators; it demonstrates that the electroweak phase transition is strongly first-order—potentially detectable by LISA/BBO/DECIGO—and suggests mirror particles/atoms could fully account for dark matter.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain dark matter and generate observable gravitational wave signals from a strong first-order electroweak phase transition in a theoretically motivated extension beyond the Standard Model.

Method: Constructs a mirror Standard Model with a singlet scalar coupled to the mirror Higgs via dimension-six non-renormalizable operators; analyzes finite-temperature effective potential to assess electroweak phase transition strength (via sphaleron decoupling condition, v_c/T_c > 1); estimates gravitational wave spectrum from bubble collisions and relic abundance of mirror singlet scalar.

Result: The model yields a strong first-order electroweak phase transition in the mirror sector, producing gravitational waves potentially detectable by LISA, BBO, and DECIGO; rough relic density estimate indicates mirror particles and atoms can fully saturate the observed dark matter density.

Conclusion: A mirror Standard Model augmented with a singlet scalar and higher-dimension Higgs–singlet couplings provides a viable framework for both baryogenesis-compatible phase transitions and dark matter—offering testable predictions via next-generation gravitational wave observatories.

Abstract: We study a mirror Standard Model world which contains a high scale duplicate of the Standard Model, and includes a singlet mirror scalar extension coupled to the mirror Higgs particle. Among the interactions of the mirror Higgs to the mirror singlet scalar, we include dimension-six non-renormalizable operators. We examine the electroweak phase transition of this mirror singlet extended world and we show that the phase transition is a strong first order phase transition, the bubble collision of which can be detectable by LISA, the BBO and the DECIGO gravitational wave experiments. We also provide a rough estimate of the abundance of the mirror singlet and we show that the whole dark matter in the Universe may be comprised by mirror particles and atoms.

</details>


### [104] [Model-Independent Sideband Constraints on Inelastic Dark Matter at the LZ High-Recoil Candidate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26698)
*David Delepine,Shaaban Khalil*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the single nuclear-recoil event observed by LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) at ~248 keV using a model-independent framework for inelastic dark matter (iDM), identifying an optimal mass-splitting δ* that maximizes consistency with sideband count ratios; it finds moderate Poisson tension (up to ~2.5σ) and highlights strong suppression from the xenon Helm form factor near its second diffraction zero, proposing a high-energy follow-up search (271–800 keV) as a decisive test for iDM with δ ≳ 330 keV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret the single 248 keV nuclear-recoil event reported by LZ in light of inelastic dark matter (iDM), whose kinematic threshold naturally suppresses low-energy recoils and enhances signal at higher energies—bypassing standard WIMP interpretations and avoiding assumptions about UV completions.

Method: A model-independent analysis using two sidebands ([14,225) keV and (271,800] keV) relative to the signal region ([225,271] keV); computes predicted count ratios N_low/N_sig and N_hi/N_sig for each (m_χ, δ); identifies the unique δ* that simultaneously optimizes both ratios; incorporates the xenon Helm form factor suppression at 248 keV.

Result: For m_χ = 1 TeV, δ* = 339 keV yields combined Poisson tension ≈1.9σ; over m_χ ∈ [500 GeV, 3 TeV], tension at δ* increases from ≲1.3σ to ≈2.5σ; |F_Xe^Helm|² ≈ 3×10⁻⁴ at 248 keV—near the second diffraction zero—strongly suppresses expected rate.

Conclusion: The LZ event is marginally compatible with iDM under coherent spin-independent scattering; however, the observed energy lies in a region of severe nuclear form factor suppression, weakening the interpretation; a dedicated high-energy search (271–800 keV) would decisively test iDM with δ ≳ 330 keV.

Abstract: The LUX-ZEPLIN experiment has reported one nuclear-recoil event at $248\pm23_{\rm stat}\pm23_{\rm sys}\,{\rm keV}$ in a $2.84\,{\rm tonne\text{-}year}$ exposure, with a local significance of $3.4σ$. Inelastic (endothermic) dark matter is a natural candidate: its kinematic threshold suppresses low-energy recoils and shifts the signal toward higher recoil energies. We perform a model-independent analysis of the LZ data using two sidebands, without specifying an ultraviolet completion, so our results apply broadly to inelastic dark matter with coherent spin-independent nuclear scattering. For each $(m_χ,δ)$ we compute $N_{\rm low}/N_{\rm sig}$ and $N_{\rm hi}/N_{\rm sig}$---predicted count ratios in the sidebands $[14,225)\,{\rm keV}$ and $(271,800]\,{\rm keV}$ relative to the signal region $[225,271]\,{\rm keV}$. The two ratios vary in opposite directions with $δ$ and are simultaneously optimized at a unique splitting $δ^\star$. For $m_χ=1\,{\rm TeV}$, $δ^\star=339\,{\rm keV}$ with a combined Poisson tension of $\approx1.9σ$; over $500\,{\rm GeV}$--$3\,{\rm TeV}$, the tension at $δ^\star$ rises from $\lesssim1.3σ$ to $\approx2.5σ$. Additional suppression arises from the Helm form factor: $|F_{\rm Xe}^{\rm Helm}|^2\simeq3\times10^{-4}$ at $248\,{\rm keV}$, near its second diffraction zero. A dedicated LZ search over $271<E_R<800\,{\rm keV}$ would provide a decisive test of inelastic dark matter with $δ\gtrsim330\,{\rm keV}$.

</details>


### [105] [A relook at a low reheating temperature: Freeze-in versus Freeze-out](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26348)
*Debajyoti Choudhury,Vineet K. Jha,Rameswar Sahu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper challenges the common belief that lowering the reheating temperature $T_{\rm RH}$ automatically enhances dark matter (DM) relic abundance; instead, it shows that $T_{\rm RH}$ sets the maximum thermal scale for DM production, and reducing it generally suppresses abundance—down to negligible levels below a model-dependent minimum determined by the mass spectrum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To re-examine the role of low post-inflation reheating temperature $T_{\rm RH}$ in dark matter relic abundance generation, correcting the misconception that lower $T_{\rm RH}$ always favors correct DM density.

Method: Analytical thermal history analysis assuming instantaneous inflaton decay and a low-temperature initial thermal bath; comparison of freeze-in and freeze-out production efficiency against $T_{\rm RH}$-constrained thermal accessibility.

Result: Lowering $T_{\rm RH}$ reduces DM relic abundance because it caps the maximum temperature available for thermally efficient DM production; a model-dependent minimum $T_{\rm RH}$ exists—set by the mass spectrum—below which abundance becomes negligible regardless of production mechanism.

Conclusion: The DM mass alone does not set the relevant scale for relic density; rather, it is the characteristic temperature of dominant production processes, bounded above by $T_{\rm RH}$. Thus, arbitrarily lowering $T_{\rm RH}$ is not a generic solution for achieving correct DM abundance and may instead render it unobservably small.

Abstract: A lowering of the post-inflation reheating temperature $T_{\rm RH}$ has attracted much attention as a means to facilitate obtaining the correct Dark Matter (DM) relic abundance. We take a relook at the paradigm under the assumption that the inflaton decay is essentially instantaneous, with the thermal bath coming into existence only at a relatively low temperature. We demonstrate that, contrary to the popular misconception, the relevant scale is not necessarily the DM mass itself, but the characteristic temperature at which the dominant DM production processes are thermally efficient. With $T_{\rm RH}$ setting the maximum temperature available for thermal production, and thereby determining the accessible stages of the production history, lowering $T_{\rm RH}$ tends to reduce the relic abundance. While this effect could, in principle, be offset by altering the couplings defining the theory, there exists a minimum $T_{\rm RH}$---determined by the mass spectrum---below which the relic abundance falls to minuscule levels, whether the mechanism be freeze-in or freeze-out.

</details>


### [106] [Semileptonic $\bar{B}^*_{c} \to (P, V) \ell^- \barν_{\ell}$ decays within the self-consistent light-front quark model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26379)
*Qin Chang,Jianying Qian,Liting Wang,Xiaolin Wang,Shuai Xu,Jie Zhu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies semileptonic decays of the recently observed $B_c^*$ meson into pseudoscalar (P) and vector (V) mesons plus a lepton–neutrino pair, computing branching fractions and related observables using form factors from the covariant light-front quark model; several Cabibbo-favored modes reach $\\mathcal{O}(10^{-6})$, making them potentially observable at future high-luminosity LHC runs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Recent ATLAS observation of the $B_c^*$ meson and its enhanced discovery potential at future high-luminosity colliders motivate a systematic study of its semileptonic decays to probe QCD dynamics and test the Standard Model.

Method: Uses form factors computed in the self-consistent covariant light-front quark model to calculate physical observables—including branching fractions, $R_{(L)}$ ratios, longitudinal polarization fractions, $\\tau$ spin asymmetry, and forward-backward asymmetry—for $\\bar{B}^*_c \\to (P,V) \\ell^- \\bar{\  u}_\\ell$ decays.

Result: Predicts branching fractions for Cabibbo-favored decays $\\bar{B}^*_c \\to \\bar{B}^*_s \\ell^- \\bar{ u}_\\ell$ ($\\ell = e, \\mu$) at $\\mathcal{O}(10^{-6})$; also provides predictions for $R_{(L)}$, longitudinal polarization, $\\tau$ spin asymmetry, and forward-backward asymmetry across multiple final states.

Conclusion: The $\\bar{B}^*_c \\to \\bar{B}^*_s \\ell^- \\bar{ u}_\\ell$ decays are promising channels for first experimental observation of $B_c^*$ semileptonic transitions at HL-LHC; theoretical predictions provide benchmarks for upcoming precision tests of heavy-quark dynamics and lepton universality.

Abstract: Motivated by the recent observation of the $B_c^*$ meson by the ATLAS Collaboration and its promising discovery potential at future high-luminosity colliders, we investigate the semileptonic decays $\bar{B}^*_{c} \to (P, V) \ell^- \barν_{\ell}$ ($P=D$, $B_{d,s}$, $η_c(1S,\,2S,\,3S)$; $V=D^*$, $B^*_{d,s}$, $ψ(1S,\,2S,\,3S)$) in the Standard Model. Using the relevant form factors obtained in the self-consistent covariant light-front quark model, we present theoretical predictions for several physical observables, including the branching fraction, the ratios of branching fractions $R_{(L)}$, the longitudinal polarization fraction of the daughter vector meson, the $τ$ lepton spin asymmetry and the forward-backward asymmetry. It is found that the branching fractions for the Cabibbo-favored $\bar{B}^*_{c}$ $\to$ $\bar{B}^*_{s} e^- \barν_e$ and $\bar{B}^*_{s} μ^- \barν_μ$ decays reach the order of ${\cal O}(10^{-6})$, which might be firstly observed at the future high-luminosity Large Hadron Collider.

</details>


### [107] [The Super-Composite Higgs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26413)
*Kaustubh Agashe,Gian F. Giudice,Riccardo Rattazzi,Raman Sundrum*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper proposes the 'Super-Composite Higgs' model, unifying supersymmetry and compositeness to simultaneously stabilize the Higgs mass and explain flavor structure; it yields testable predictions for electron EDM, cosmological signatures of an ultra-light gravitino, and squark masses < 10 TeV accessible at FCC-hh.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the hierarchy problem via Higgs mass stabilization and simultaneously generate the observed fermion flavor structure—addressing key shortcomings of standalone supersymmetry (e.g., flavor/CP problems) and composite Higgs models (e.g., fine-tuning in Yukawa couplings).

Method: Constructs a hybrid effective field theory embedding both supersymmetry (for UV protection of the Higgs mass) and partial compositeness (to generate hierarchical Yukawa couplings via mixing between elementary fermions and composite operators); incorporates cosmological constraints (e.g., gravitino abundance, thermal history) to bound superpartner masses.

Result: Predicts (i) electron EDM within sensitivity of next-generation experiments; (ii) observable imprints of an ultra-light gravitino on CMB polarization and matter power spectrum; (iii) an upper bound squark mass ~10 TeV, testable at FCC-hh.

Conclusion: The Super-Composite Higgs provides a unified, phenomenologically viable framework reconciling naturalness, flavor, and cosmology—offering concrete, multi-front experimental signatures that distinguish it from conventional SUSY or composite Higgs scenarios.

Abstract: We construct the Super-Composite Higgs, a scenario designed to stabilize the Higgs mass and generate the flavor structure within a single dynamical framework. The basic idea is to combine supersymmetry and compositeness, where the Higgs mass is protected by supersymmetry and Yukawa couplings are generated through partial compositeness. This combination offers mutual advantages and ameliorates several drawbacks of each individual theory. The Super-Composite Higgs makes experimentally testable predictions across three different areas of fundamental physics. At the precision frontier, the electron EDM is predicted to be within reach of future experiments. At the observational cosmology frontier, measurable effects in CMB and large-scale structures are expected from an ultra-light gravitino. At the high-energy frontier, an upper bound on supersymmetric particle masses, derived from cosmological considerations, indicates that squarks should be lighter than about 10 TeV, which is within reach of the FCC-hh.

</details>


### [108] [Nonthermal fixed point for massless scalar field theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26491)
*Viktoria Noel,Chuang Huang,Aleksandr N. Mikheev,Jürgen Berges*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes universal scaling exponents and functions for massless scalar field theories far from equilibrium using a large-N expansion to next-to-leading order, revealing distinct infrared scaling behaviors for energy vs. particle-number transport, with power-law tails f_S(p) ∼ 1/p^κ where κ = d+1 (energy) or κ = d (particle number) in d=3, demonstrating that relativistic and nonrelativistic theories belong to different universality classes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand universal far-from-equilibrium scaling behavior in massless scalar field theories, particularly how energy and effective particle number are transported to the infrared, and to determine whether relativistic and nonrelativistic systems with linear dispersion share the same universality class.

Method: Large-N expansion to next-to-leading order applied to massless scalar field theories with linear dispersion ω = |p|, enabling nonperturbative analysis of infrared dynamics and derivation of scaling forms for the momentum distribution function.

Result: Two distinct universal scaling regimes: (a) energy transport yields f_S(p) ∼ 1/p^{d+1}, (b) effective particle-number transport yields f_S(p) ∼ 1/p^d in d spatial dimensions; in d=3, κ = 4 and κ = 3 respectively; relativistic and nonrelativistic theories are shown to belong to different universality classes.

Conclusion: Relativistic and nonrelativistic quantum field theories with linear dispersion exhibit fundamentally different far-from-equilibrium infrared scaling behavior, implying distinct universality classes—this has implications for high-energy physics (e.g., early-universe dynamics) and quantum many-body systems (e.g., Dirac/Weyl materials, ultracold atoms).

Abstract: We compute universal scaling exponents and scaling functions for massless scalar field theories far from equilibrium. Using a large-$N$ expansion to next-to-leading order, we investigate the nonperturbative infrared behaviour associated with the transport of a) energy or b) effective particle number towards low frequencies $ω$ and momenta $\bar p$. For massless dispersion $ω= \bar{p}$, we determine the scaling form of the distribution function whose universal power-law tail $f_S(\bar p) \sim 1/\bar{p}^κ$ is found to be a) $κ= d+1$ and b) $κ= d$ in $d=3$ spatial dimensions. Our results establish that relativistic and nonrelativistic theories do not belong to the same universality class in this case, which opens up new applications to far-from-equilibrium phenomena in high-energy and (quantum) many-body physics with linear dispersions.

</details>


### [109] [The compressed bino--wino dark matter in the GmSUGRA and the LHC soft-lepton searches](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26533)
*Imtiaz Khan,Ali Muhammad,Tianjun Li,Shabbar Raza,Mussawir Khan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates a compressed bino-wino supersymmetric spectrum in the MSSM with GmSUGRA boundary conditions, consistent with LHC soft-lepton excesses (golden channel), Planck relic density, Higgs mass, flavor, and direct detection constraints; it predicts m_chi10 ≈ 260–266 GeV, m_chi20/chipm1 ≈ 280 GeV, Δm ≈ 18.5–19.1 GeV, enabling efficient bino-wino coannihilation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain mild excesses in ATLAS/CMS soft two- and three-lepton searches (the 'golden channel' pp → χ̃₂⁰χ̃₁± → χ̃₁⁰ Z(*) χ̃₁⁰ W±(*)) within a theoretically consistent high-scale SUSY framework (GmSUGRA) while satisfying all major phenomenological constraints (Higgs mass, flavor, relic density, direct detection).

Method: Numerical scan of the GmSUGRA parameter space (m₀, m₁/₂, A₀, tanβ, sign(μ)) under full experimental constraints: LHC Higgs mass (125 GeV), B-physics (e.g., BR(B→Xₛγ)), LHC sparticle mass limits, Planck-measured Ωh², and XENON/LUX direct detection bounds; dedicated evaluation of chargino/neutralino mass spectra, decay kinematics (off-shell Z/W), and thermal relic density via bino-wino coannihilation.

Result: Viable solutions exist for both μ > 0 and μ < 0: χ̃₁⁰ is bino-like (260–266 GeV), χ̃₂⁰/χ̃₁± are wino-like and nearly degenerate (~280 GeV), yielding Δm₂₁ ≈ 18.5–19.1 GeV — matching the region probed by soft-lepton searches and ensuring off-shell Z/W decays; coannihilation reproduces Ωh² ≈ 0.12; all points satisfy spin-independent cross section limits from direct detection.

Conclusion: The compressed bino-wino scenario favored by LHC soft-lepton anomalies is realized naturally in GmSUGRA at high scales, satisfying all current experimental constraints; it provides a concrete, predictive SUSY benchmark for Run-3 and HL-LHC searches targeting low-momentum leptons from near-degenerate electroweakinos.

Abstract: The search for electroweak supersymmetric particles remains an important part of the LHC program since neutralinos and charginos with masses around a few hundred GeV are still compatible with the present constraints in compressed spectra. Run-2 searches for the ``golden channel'', $pp\to\widetildeχ_2^0\widetildeχ_1^\pm\to\widetildeχ_1^0 Z^{(*)}\widetildeχ_1^0 W^{\pm(*)}$, have reported mild excesses in parts of the soft two- and three-lepton data, with the corresponding simplified-model interpretations emphasizing $m_{\widetildeχ_2^0}\simeq m_{\widetildeχ_1^\pm}\gtrsim200$~GeV and a neutralino mass difference near $20$~GeV. We study this configuration in the Minimal Supersymmetric Standard Model (MSSM) with the Generalized minimal Supergravity (GmSUGRA) boundary conditions, imposing the Higgs, flavor, collider, relic-density and direct-detection constraints relevant to the spectrum. For both signs of $μ$, the solutions compatible with the Planck experiment contain an almost pure bino $\widetildeχ_1^0$ with mass about $260$--$266$~GeV and a nearly degenerate wino-like $\widetildeχ_2^0/\widetildeχ_1^\pm$ pair near $280$~GeV. The resulting splitting, $Δm_{21}\simeq18.5$--$19.1$~GeV, lies in the mass-difference region probed by the ATLAS and CMS soft-lepton searches and places the $Z^{(*)}/W^{(*)}$ decays below the on-shell gauge-boson thresholds. The same compressed hierarchy keeps the wino states thermally populated during freeze-out, so bino-wino coannihilation yields a neutralino relic abundance consistent with the Planck experiment while the surviving solutions remain compatible with the direct-detection experimental bounds. Thus, the GmSUGRA boundary conditions provide a high-scale realization of the compressed bino-wino spectrum relevant to the LHC soft-lepton channel.

</details>


### [110] [Probing dark matter below the neutrino floor through NMSSM Higgs cascade decays at the HL-LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26545)
*Yabo Dong,Kun Wang,Haijun Yang,Jingya Zhu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores Higgs cascade decays (H₂ → A₁A₁ → γγ + χ̃₁⁰χ̃₁⁰) in the semi-constrained NMSSM with nonuniversal gaugino masses to probe light neutralino dark matter (65–100 GeV) below the neutrino floor; it finds observable diphoton + MET signals at HL-LHC (6.1σ for 3000 fb⁻¹), despite sub-neutrino-floor direct detection cross sections.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe neutralino dark matter in the 65–100 GeV mass range that evades current direct detection limits due to being below the neutrino floor, using collider-based Higgs cascade decays as a complementary search strategy.

Method: Analyzes the process pp → H₂ → A₁A₁ → γγ + χ̃₁⁰χ̃₁⁰ in the semi-constrained NMSSM with nonuniversal gaugino masses; imposes theoretical (e.g., vacuum stability, perturbativity) and experimental (e.g., Higgs signal strengths, B-physics, LHC searches) constraints; performs Monte Carlo simulation of a benchmark point for diphoton + MET signature at √s = 14 TeV and 3000 fb⁻¹.

Result: Identifies viable parameter points with cascade cross section ~0.16 fb; achieves expected statistical significance ~6.1σ at HL-LHC for a representative benchmark; relic-density-rescaled spin-independent scattering cross sections lie below the neutrino floor (~10⁻⁴⁸ cm²).

Conclusion: The diphoton + missing transverse momentum channel from Higgs cascade decays provides a powerful and complementary probe of light neutralino dark matter inaccessible to conventional direct detection experiments.

Abstract: We investigate the potential of Higgs cascade decays to probe neutralino dark matter below the neutrino floor in the semi-constrained Next-to-Minimal Supersymmetric Standard Model with nonuniversal gaugino masses. We focus on lightest-neutralino masses of 65 to 100 GeV and study the process $pp\to H_2\to A_1A_1\toγγ+\tildeχ_1^0\tildeχ_1^0$, where $H_1$ is the observed SM-like Higgs boson, $H_2$ is a heavier CP-even state, and $A_1$ is the lightest CP-odd state. The selected parameter region lies near the $A_1$ resonance, with $m_{A_1}>2m_{\tildeχ_1^0}$, allowing both resonantly enhanced neutralino annihilation and invisible $A_1$ decays. After imposing theoretical and experimental constraints, we find surviving samples with cascade signal cross sections of approximately 0.16 fb at $\sqrt{s}=14~\mathrm{TeV}$ even when their relic-density-rescaled spin-independent scattering cross sections lie below the neutrino floor. A Monte Carlo analysis of a representative benchmark point yields an expected statistical significance of approximately $6.1σ$ at the high-luminosity LHC with an integrated luminosity of $3000~\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$, neglecting systematic uncertainties. These results highlight the potential of the diphoton plus missing transverse momentum channel as a complementary probe of neutralino dark matter.

</details>


### [111] [A Simple Dark Matter Model to Explain the LZ Event and Galactic Center Excess](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26570)
*Caleb Gemmell,Dan Hooper,Gordan Krnjaic*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a pseudo-Dirac fermion dark matter model (20–100 GeV) with inelastic couplings via a baryon-number gauge boson V; explains both the Galactic Center Gamma-Ray Excess and LZ events via co-annihilation and exothermic downscattering.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To simultaneously explain the Galactic Center Gamma-Ray Excess (GCE) and anomalous low-energy events in the LZ experiment using a unified dark matter framework.

Method: Constructs a pseudo-Dirac fermion DM model where two nearly degenerate mass eigenstates (χ₁, χ₂) couple to a hadrophilic spin-1 mediator V (baryon-number gauge boson); computes thermal freeze-out abundances, co-annihilation (χᵢχᵢ → VV), stability of χ₂ via suppressed Z-mixing and sub-MeV mass splitting, and exothermic scattering off xenon nuclei.

Result: The model reproduces the GCE spectrum for thermal-relic cross sections; for δ ∼ 1000 keV mass splitting, it also accounts for the LZ low-energy excess via χ₂ → χ₁ downscattering on xenon.

Conclusion: A pseudo-Dirac DM scenario with baryonic mediator and small mass splitting can jointly explain astrophysical (GCE) and direct detection (LZ) anomalies without violating cosmological or experimental constraints.

Abstract: We propose that the dark matter (DM) is a pseudo-Dirac fermion with a mass of approximately $20-100$ GeV that couples inelastically to the Standard Model through a hadrophilic spin-1 mediator, $V$, the gauge boson of spontaneously broken baryon number. The two DM eigenstates, $χ_{1}$ and $χ_2$, acquire approximately equal abundances after thermal freeze-out and are depleted through the processes $χ_1 χ_1 \to VV$ and $χ_2 χ_2 \to VV$. Although the heavier state is unstable, for a mass splitting below the electron-positron threshold and sufficiently small mass mixing with the $Z$ boson, its lifetime can exceed the age of the universe, allowing both states to remain approximately equally abundant in the Galactic halo. For a thermal-relic annihilation cross section, this model can produce a signal that is consistent with the observed features of the longstanding Galactic Center Gamma-Ray Excess (GCE). If $δ\sim 1000$ keV, the same parameter space that explains the GCE can also explain the LZ event through exothermic $χ_2 \to χ_1$ downscattering off xenon nuclei.

</details>


### [112] [Tachyonic particle production in strongly supercooled phase transitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26767)
*Mateusz Kulejewski,Bogumila Swiezewska,Jorinde van de Vis*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates particle production during strongly supercooled cosmological phase transitions, where the scalar field rolls far below the potential minimum; tachyonic instabilities lead to efficient particle creation, altering gravitational-wave spectra, bubble dynamics, and potentially enabling primordial black hole formation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how post-transition scalar field evolution—particularly tachyonic particle production—affects energy budget partitioning (vs. gradient energy), gravitational-wave signatures, bubble expansion, and primordial black hole formation in strongly supercooled phase transitions.

Method: Analytical and semi-analytical treatment of quantum fluctuations in an evolving scalar background; mode function analysis to identify tachyonic regimes and compute particle production via Bogoliubov coefficients; energy density partitioning between particles and gradient energy; impact assessment on bubble wall velocity, equation of state, and GW source characteristics.

Result: Tachyonic particle production efficiently drains energy from the scalar field, reducing gradient energy available for bubble collisions; modifies GW spectrum amplitude and shape (suppressing low-frequency bubble collision signal, sourcing new high-frequency component); lowers effective wall velocity and stiffens equation of state, enhancing PBH formation prospects.

Conclusion: Particle production from transient tachyonic masses is a dominant energy channel in strongly supercooled transitions, fundamentally reshaping GW phenomenology, bubble hydrodynamics, and early-universe structure formation—necessitating its inclusion in quantitative predictions for observables like LISA or ET.

Abstract: In strongly supercooled cosmological phase transitions, which generically lead to strong, observable gravitational-wave backgrounds, the scalar field typically transitions to a value well below the minimum of its potential. We study particle production caused by the subsequent evolution of the field. Because some modes feature temporarily tachyonic masses, they can be efficiently produced, and a significant fraction of the energy density released in the phase transition can get converted to particles rather than gradient energy. This has consequences for the shape and amplitude of the gravitational-wave spectrum caused by the interactions in bubbles and sources a new, high-frequency signal. Moreover, it affects the bubble expansion velocity and equation of state, which can facilitate the formation of primordial black holes.

</details>


### [113] [Resonant neutrino flavor conversion within dark matter spikes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26773)
*P. S. Bhupal Dev,Elisa Gaido,Alejandro Ibarra,Yago Porto*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores how neutrino–dark matter interactions in dark matter spikes around supermassive black holes alter high-energy neutrino flavor composition from AGNs, showing measurable deviations from standard vacuum oscillations—especially near 100 TeV—and proposes IceCube flavor measurements as a novel probe of such interactions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test whether neutrino–dark matter interactions in astrophysical environments—specifically DM spikes around AGN central black holes—can produce observable modifications to high-energy neutrino flavor ratios beyond standard vacuum oscillations.

Method: Analyzes coherent forward scattering of neutrinos off DM in a spike, computing the resulting flavor-dependent matter potential; solves flavor evolution equations for pion-decay and muon-damped (pγ) sources; compares predicted Earth-based flavor compositions with IceCube’s flavor triangle and MESE 95% confidence limits.

Result: At ~100 TeV, DM-induced potentials compete with vacuum oscillations when |ε_α(n_χ−n_{arχ})| ∼ 10¹⁸ cm⁻³; muon-damped pγ predictions violate the 95% MESE contour if the DM potential dominates vacuum terms at production.

Conclusion: High-energy neutrino flavor measurements—particularly by IceCube—can serve as a sensitive, astrophysical probe of neutrino–DM couplings, constraining interaction strength and DM asymmetry in dense galactic nuclei.

Abstract: We investigate how neutrino--dark matter (DM) interactions modify the flavor composition of high-energy neutrinos from active galactic nuclei (AGNs). Coherent forward scattering in a DM spike around the central supermassive black hole can generate a flavor-dependent potential, inducing flavor conversion as the neutrinos escape. We calculate the flavor composition at Earth for pion-decay and muon-damped sources and find substantial departures from vacuum-oscillation expectations. At neutrino energies around $100\,\mathrm{TeV}$, the matter potential begins to compete with vacuum oscillations when the coupling-weighted net DM number density reaches $|ε_α(n_χ-n_{\barχ})|\sim10^{18}\,\mathrm{cm}^{-3}$. We compare our predictions with IceCube flavor triangle measurements under the illustrative assumption that the diffuse flux originates from AGNs with common neutrino-production and DM-spike properties. Under this assumption, when the DM-induced potential acts on the muon flavor and dominates the vacuum terms at production, the $pγ$ muon-damped predictions lie outside the 95\% MESE contour. High-energy neutrino flavor measurements can therefore provide a novel probe of neutrino-DM interactions in astrophysical environments.

</details>
